Names: OSM name, else route name, else ref (since T102 for trails too), else the class ("the path"). Lead distance: about 20 s ahead on foot (20 to 60 m) and on a bike (40 to 200 m), 45 s in a car at the approach road's speed (150 m to two miles), never past the previous instruction. The apps show priorities 1 and 2. Measured on the Rainier pack (`devtool route`): Paradise to Sunrise by car 14 instructions, 12 shown (the five turns onto the park roads, the Sunrise Point parking lot that the route cuts through at the hairpin, the visitor-centre lot); Paradise to Panorama Point 8, Reflection Lakes to Pinnacle Peak 7, Mowich Lake to Spray Park 7, Sunrise to Frozen Lake 8. Before the pull-out and merge rules the drive had 21, ten of them "keep left to stay on" at pull-outs.
- **Directions with a trip, crow, and T102's follow-ups (T108; ADR 0033).** *Format:* a trip's turn directions are a third route field, `directions` (`RouteDirections`: version 1, the FNV-1a 64 key of the path value they were made for, then per instruction its type code, priority, along distance in decimetres, position in 1e-7 degrees, name, class, flags, turn, bearing, lead and stretch; base64url; at most 64 KiB, about 600 to 700 characters for the Paradise to Sunrise drive), written after each `path` write by a T108 build: Keep saves the planned route's own (the server's for a route it planned), a new trip and every edit save the editor's, and an edit keeps the directions of the legs it left alone (`Directions.splice`, so a leg planned on the server or on another phone keeps what was said along it). The merge takes them only while their key is the route's path, so a path written without them (the browser's Trips box, a build before T108, two phones editing at once) leaves them stale and every replica drops them alike; the track database's route table has a `directions` column (schema 5, `4.sqm`), filled from the log once on the first open after the update. *Migration:* trips saved before T108 or in the browser get their directions when opened on a phone whose packs' graph covers them (in the background; every-leg-straight trips at once), else the trip card says they were not saved; the fixture `sync/v0.1.7-trips.dsync`, written by v0.1.7-alpha itself, round-trips byte for byte and gains them (`V017TripFixtureTest`). A v0.1.7 phone ignores the field (not one of its route fields), stores and forwards it. *Crow:* `TravelMode.CROW` (`Crow.kt`), a straight line between the points: no graph, no snapping, every leg straight, a point every 25 m with the DEM's height (at most 2,001 per leg), each leg's initial bearing in the summary (`bearings`), one arrival as its directions, the hike model's time; every router answers it without searching (`RemoteRouter` never asks the server). Paradise to Panorama Point: 1,873.2 m, bearing 18°, +430.4 m / -27.9 m, 1:06, the same in `devtool route`, the server and on the phone. A crow trip synced to a v0.1.7 phone shows "crow" as its mode with the hike model's time and its legs dashed "off trail", navigates as a hike ("Head north off the trails", arrive), and an edit there replans the moved legs along trails as a hike under the crow name. This build names a mode it does not know plainly ("walk (unknown mode)") and refuses to edit it with a reason (`RouteEditor`; the server answers 400), so a newer build's mode is never replanned as a hike. *Follow-ups of T102:* the graph file is version 6 (version 5's layout), so files built before T102 are rebuilt once and trails known only by their `ref` get their names; drive assumes 15 km/h on service roads (20 before): at 20 the Sunrise Point parking lot (73 m) and Sunrise Park Road's hairpin round it (205 m) were both 13 s and the drive cut through the lot; now Paradise to Sunrise keeps to the road, 82,069.0 m and 1:26 (81,937.1 m before; 11 instructions, 8 shown, against 14 and 12); the route on the card not yet kept is written to `files/route-session.json` and read back once per process, so it survives the app being stopped while the font size is changed in the system settings (a recreated activity already kept it: the card's state lives in `RouteSession`, a process object; `fontScale` stays out of the manifest's `configChanges`).
- Not yet: turn costs and restrictions, level-2 cells on the phone (no warm-up there; the server's cell file could travel with a pack), graph tiles from a server or a peer instead of built per pack (the file and the cache key already allow it), a component summary across packs (two open parts in different packs are still searched).

### Concurrency
- Kotlin coroutines + Flow throughout.
- CPU-heavy work (DEM decoding, contour generation, tile rendering) runs on `Dispatchers.Default`, which sizes to the device's cores. Large jobs are split per tile so they parallelize. This is where multi-core and hyperthreaded CPUs pay off.
- I/O (disk, network, database) runs on `Dispatchers.IO`. GPS tracking runs in a foreground service.

## Phasing
Build and explore ideas first; the full system comes later.

| Phase | Goal | Where it runs |
|---|---|---|
| 0. Explore map generation | DEM → hillshade, slope shading, contours. Output PNGs and tiles; check quality and speed. | JVM: `shared` + `devtool` CLI (fast iteration, no emulator) |
| 0b. OSM base map on desktop | Planetiler → PMTiles for a region; MVT decoding in `shared`; trails, water, and names drawn over the terrain in `devtool` | JVM |
| 1. Android shell | MapLibre map showing phase-0 output from local files; GPS location; debug server | Android, early, so phone constraints show up quickly |
| 2. Offline + user data | Download manager, tracks, waypoints, GPX import/export, local DB | Android |
| 3. Sync | Op log, sync simulator, then a Ktor peer | `shared` + server |
| Later | OSM base map + routing, P2P transports, desktop, web, iOS | |

Phases 0b and 1 are broken into independent tasks for parallel agents in [TASKS.md](TASKS.md).

### Phase 0 log
**2026-10-09: first pipeline working.** `devtool render` fetches Terrarium elevation tiles (Mapzen/AWS, global, z≤15), stitches and crops them to an area, then draws hillshade + contours to PNG. JVM timings for Mount Rainier at z13 (1166×1279 px, 13 m/px, 30 tiles): hillshade 41 ms, contours 577 ms (273 levels at 40 ft, 1.1M points), drawing 785 ms. That's whole-area work in ~0.6 s total, so per-tile work on a phone looks very feasible.

Findings to follow up:
- **Contour density must depend on zoom.** 40 ft at 13 m/px is an unreadable solid mass on steep terrain; 200 ft is clean. Need a zoom → interval table, and probably to hide lines where they'd merge (steep cliffs).
- **Faint striping on smooth slopes** (visible on Rainier's glaciers in the hillshade). Likely the source being upsampled from lower resolution; check other zooms, and try light smoothing before hillshade/contours.
- **Contours are unsmoothed and unsimplified.** Add Douglas–Peucker simplification (point count) and possibly smoothing.
- Terrarium is a convenient starting source, but its resolution varies by region. Next sources to try directly: Copernicus GLO-30 and USGS 3DEP.

**2026-10-09: USGS 3DEP 1/3″ source + `devtool compare`.** Pure-Kotlin GeoTIFF reader in `shared` (tiled float32, LZW + floating-point predictor, georeferencing; deflate injected on JVM) reads 3DEP Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFFs over HTTP range requests: only 22 requests and 20 internal tiles for a 16 km area instead of two ~400 MB files. Compared with Terrarium on an identical Web Mercator grid (a 16 km Colorado mountain area, z14, 4.6M cells): mean difference 0.9 m, RMS 7 m, max 140 m (cliffs). 3DEP removes Terrarium's seam line and terrace striping and gives crisper gullies and ridges. Next: 3DEP 1 m lidar, then compositing.

**2026-10-09: 1 m lidar + first compositing.** `shared` gained UTM projection math (Krüger series), GeoTIFF overviews and predictor 2, and `Composite.over` (fills gaps from a fallback, smoothstep-feathered over a band at coverage edges using a chamfer distance transform). New devtool sources: `3dep-1m` (lidar only: finds tiles via the USGS TNM API, samples each survey project separately, composites newer surveys on top) and `3dep-best` (lidar over the 10 m DEM). Each lidar tile is read at the coarsest internal overview that still matches the output resolution, so a zoomed-out render reads 4 m data, not 1 m.

Test in a Colorado mountain area covered by two overlapping lidar surveys:
- At ~1.9 m/px (z16) lidar shows talus, gullies, and cliff bands that 10 m data smooths away. The highest point matches the published summit elevation to within 0.2 m; the 10 m DEM is 4 m low.
- At ~7.5 m/px (z14) over 16 km: no visible seam between the two surveys. Lidar vs 10 m: mean −0.04 m, RMS 2.3 m. First fetch 13 s (180 range requests), 2.4 s once cached.
- Not yet exercised on real data: the lidar → 10 m fallback seam (this area is fully covered). It's unit-tested; needs a real test area at a lidar coverage edge.
- For per-tile rendering later: feathering needs a halo of neighboring data, or tiles will disagree at their borders when a coverage edge sits just outside a tile.

**2026-10-09: global coverage + common vertical datum.** All sources now output **EGM2008** heights. 3DEP (NAVD88) is converted per pixel with NGS GEOID18 + the NAD83(2011)→WGS84 ellipsoid shift − NGA EGM2008, using public-domain grids from the PROJ CDN read by range requests. New `copernicus` source (GLO-30 COGs on AWS; samples across 1° cell boundaries so cells join seamlessly) and `best` source: Copernicus globally, 3DEP 10 m over it (300 m blend band), lidar over that (60 m band). `best` is now the default.
- Datum check on bare, flat ground (Bonneville Salt Flats): converted 3DEP minus Copernicus = **+0.20 m** mean (0.40 m mean abs). Unconverted it would be +1.0 m; with the ellipsoid shift's sign flipped, +1.5 m. Conversion confirmed.
- Border seam (Glacier/Waterton, 49°N): contours and hillshade continue smoothly through the US→Canada transition. US data's edge follows lidar UTM tile edges, not the border, so the transition is irregular; the feathering handles it.
- Copernicus is a surface model (includes forest canopy): in forest it sits several meters above bare-earth 3DEP (mean −3.7 m best vs Copernicus at the border). Feathering hides that as a gentle ramp, but contours outside the US will run high in forests. Bare-earth global alternatives: FABDEM (CC BY-NC-SA, non-commercial: check against our licensing before use), or national lidar DTMs (Canada HRDEM, Swisstopo, etc.) added as more layers.
- Bug found by viewing output: pixel-is-point cells (Copernicus) left a 1-px seam at every 1° boundary until sampling read neighbors across cells.

**2026-10-09: T5 merged (PR #1): MVT decoder.** Hand-written protobuf wire parser and Mapbox Vector Tile decoder/encoder in `shared/.../mvt/` (pure Kotlin): points, lines, polygons with holes by winding, every property value type. `devtool mvt dump` summarizes a tile. A real z2 tile from the MapLibre demotiles set (BSD-3; Natural Earth data) is a committed fixture: 147 features, 20.9k vertices, decoded in 16 ms. First task delivered by the parallel-agent workflow (implementation on one agent, verification on a cheaper one).

**2026-10-09: T3 merged (PR #2): PMTiles v3.** Pure-Kotlin reader and writer in `shared/.../pmtiles/` (header, Hilbert tile IDs, run-length directories, leaf directories, injected codecs, a small JSON parser for metadata) plus `devtool pmtiles info`. Tests include a 30 001-tile archive that forces leaf directories (0.12 s). Against the real 138 GB Protomaps planet archive, three Rainier-area tiles cost 7 range requests and 449 KB, and the decoded tiles name Mount Rainier National Park and the Nisqually River, which confirms the Hilbert mapping. This is the container format for region packs (vector tiles now; DEM tiles once T4 lands).

**2026-10-09: T1 merged (PR #3): contour readability.** Contour interval now follows zoom (feet: 1000 ft at z11 down to 20 ft at z16+; meters: 200 m down to 5 m), with `--interval` as an override. Douglas–Peucker in pixel units cuts Rainier z13 from 1.15M points to 35k with no visible change; non-index lines are thinned where they'd be closer than 3 px, so cliffs show index lines instead of a brown smear; elevation labels sit on straight stretches of index contours, upright, collision-free, haloed, sized for a phone (`--text-scale`, default 1.4). Review on the images: 14000/13000 ft on the crater rings, labels dense but never overlapping on the z15 faces. Open: curved labels for low zoom (z11 on a small window gets none), and a degenerate 2-point ring case in simplification.

**2026-10-09: T4 merged (PR #4): DEM tile export.** `devtool tiles` writes the composited `best` DEM as Terrarium XYZ tiles plus `metadata.json`, each tile rendered through its own window with a halo (feather band rounded up to 16 cells) so the feathering gives identical values on both sides of every border; `--verify` compares neighbours bit for bit. Rainier z8–13: 52 tiles, 8.7 MB, 5 s warm (81 s cold, nearly all of it the z8 tile's 236 lidar headers; TNM index queries are the cold-start cost at low zoom). Zero seam mismatches, decode error 1/512 m. The seam check caught a real bug: the geoid-offset lattice was clamped at window edges, shifting values by ≤1.5 mm in the last 16 columns; fixed. These tiles are what the Android shell (T10) will display and what T3's writer will package.

**2026-10-09: T6 merged (PR #5): OSM → PMTiles with Planetiler.** New `tilebuild` module (JVM only). Planetiler 0.9.3 with the OpenMapTiles 3.16 profile (0.9.3 because the profile artifact on JitPack is built against it; 0.10 has no published profile). One-time aux data download of 1.45 GB (Natural Earth, water polygons, lake centerlines) into the shared cache; after that Liechtenstein builds in 31 s (182 tiles, 2.4 MB, 15 layers) and Washington clipped to the Rainier box in 47 s (364 MB extract, 170 tiles, 1.2 MB). ODbL and OpenMapTiles attribution plus `dystopo:*` provenance keys live in the archive metadata, confirmed with `pmtiles info`. Licensing: GeoTools (LGPL-2.1) comes with Planetiler; the rule was amended to tolerate LGPL in build-side tooling only. Wave 1 complete: all five tasks merged in one session. Next on this track: a custom outdoors profile (trails with surface/visibility, huts, passes), and drawing these tiles over the terrain in devtool (T5 + T6).

**2026-10-09: T2 merged (PR #6): slope-angle shading and multidirectional hillshade.** Slope angle/aspect per cell in `shared`; CalTopo-style bands (27–29 yellow through 60+ blue) as a translucent overlay (`--slope-overlay`), and `--shading multi` blending four light directions with a north-west bias so faces turned away from the light keep their ribs and couloirs. Rainier z13: 17% of cells in the 35–45° band, 1.4% over 60°; Mont Blanc 25% and 1.9%. Slope costs ~100 ms for a 1.5 Mpx area. Known: the Terrarium-era striping on Rainier's upper east-side glaciers flips bands at the 27–35° edges; DEM smoothing before slope/hillshade is the fix (follow-up).

**2026-10-09: T7 merged (PR #8): dependency license check.** `./gradlew check` now fails on any GPL/AGPL dependency in any module, on LGPL outside `tilebuild`, and on artifacts whose POM chain has no license (unless listed in `gradle/licenses/artifact-overrides.txt`). Current state: shared 2 artifacts, devtool 24, tilebuild 135 (10 LGPL, tolerated). Policy choice recorded: multiple licenses in a POM are treated as alternatives (best wins), which is what lets dual-licensed JNA/jffi pass; the report lists every license so cumulative cases can be found by hand.

**2026-10-09: T8 merged (PR #7): golden-image tests.** Six synthetic-terrain renders (cone, ridge and saddle, flat lake with shoreline, 80° cliff band, rolling ground) go through the full pipeline in `./gradlew check` and are compared against committed PNGs with a strict tolerance outside label boxes and a loose one inside. A 15° change to the light azimuth fails all six with diff images. Caveat recorded: label glyphs depend on the machine's fonts, so goldens are tied to this Windows/JDK 21 setup until CI exists. Found while making them: on a merged index-contour band along a cliff, labels stack (cosmetic; follow-up).

**2026-10-09: T9 merged (PR #9): data source registry.** Licensing hard rule 4 is now code: `sources.json` in `shared` lists every source with license, attribution (plain and HTML), vertical datum, coverage and cadence; the registry validates on load. Reports carry provenance, i.e. only the sources that actually contributed (Rainier z13: 3DEP + GEOID18 + EGM2008, no Copernicus, because US coverage is complete there; at the Glacier/Waterton border all five), tile packs get registry attribution in their metadata, and tilebuild credits come from the same place. Wave 2 complete: nine of ten tasks merged; only the Android shell (T10) remains in wave 1's list.

**2026-10-09: T11b merged (PR #10): hardening sweep.** Corrupt PMTiles archives now fail fast with a reason instead of allocating or looping (11 fixtures built by patching real writer output); tilebuild leaves no temp dirs; the license policy no longer lets "GPL ISC"-style strings through and reports multi-license choices; the registry carries the Copernicus licence document, honest 3DEP datum wording, SPDX expressions, and provenance is mandatory on every result with data.

**2026-10-09: T12 merged (PR #11): DEM region packs.** `tiles --pmtiles` writes a region's Terrarium tiles into one PMTiles archive (Rainier NP region z8–14: 1,404 tiles, 222 MB, 21 s warm), verified tile by tile against the XYZ output (identical grid digests); `pmtiles extract --bbox` cuts a sub-archive without re-encoding, including from the remote planet build. The browser demo now reads hillshade and contours from the archive. This is the "download a drawn area" mechanism from the product principles, working end to end on desktop.

**2026-10-09: T18 merged (PR #12): browser demo tour.** `tools/demo-check.py --views-file tools/demo-tour.txt` drives 14 views across the Rainier region in one headless session and judges each (errors, missing tiles, per-layer counts, screenshots). It found and fixed: contours disappearing one tile in from the data edge (the contour library dropped any tile with a missing neighbour), a seam at the z12→z13 coverage boundary (missing tiles are now built from the nearest ancestor), no contour labels at z12, a solid brown summit at z13 (interval now 80/400 ft there), and a panel that hid a third of a phone screen. Known: contouring runs on the main thread in archive mode (2–4 s per view); the hillshade layer doesn't get the ancestor fallback, so at z9 it stops a little short of the contours.

**2026-10-09: T17 merged (PR #13): world tour.** `devtool tour` renders 22 public landmarks worldwide at z9 and z12 with plausibility checks (expected elevation range, NaN fraction, datum conversion) and a contact sheet; 44/44 pass in 10 s warm, 203 s cold. The highest pixel lands on the true summit for Everest, Denali, Kibo and Batian; no seams at 180°, the equator, 0° or 1° cell corners. It found an out-of-memory crash on open ocean (contour levels from an all-NaN grid), three antimeridian bugs (bounding box, Copernicus cell naming at ±180, tile column 0), and a tilebuild load-time validation that one odd area could break. Recorded: Alaska/Hawaii download 3DEP then discard it for lack of GEOID18 (GEOID12B is the fix), lidar is queried at low zooms where it's useless (Death Valley 28 s cold), `render` crashes on a no-data grid, antimeridian tile metadata bounds, and Copernicus void-fill artifacts (a 230 m low summit near Paine Grande with a step at 51°S; coastal pits of −13 to −75 m). The tour is now the post-wave sanity check.

**2026-10-09: T11a merged (PR #14): rendering and DEM follow-ups.** Labels no longer stack on merged cliff bands (a label is rejected when another index contour passes within 2 px); `--smooth-dem 1` removes most of the band striping on noisy glaciers while keeping ridges sharp (hillshade and slope only; contours stay on the raw grid); the Horn gradient is shared between hillshade and slope; the lidar index is fetched once per 1° cell and tile reads run on a dedicated I/O pool, so a fully cold Rainier z8 tile went from 81 s to 26 s; tile exports pick one overview level per zoom; golden tests now check a label manifest (text, position, angle) so an extra or wrong label can't hide.

**2026-10-09: T13 merged (PR #15): OSM over terrain.** `render --osm` draws our own OpenStreetMap archive on top of the terrain: tints for forest, wetland, rock and glacier under the contours; lakes over them; streams, park boundaries, cased roads, tracks and trails by type; peaks with name and elevation; curved names along trails, roads, rivers and ridges, placed without overlaps and with contour labels yielding to them. The style is declarative JSON in `shared` (ordered layers, filters, zoom-dependent values), so the same style can later drive MapLibre on the phone and the website. Rainier z13 costs ~130 ms of OSM work on top of the terrain render. This is the first complete topographic map the project produces on its own data.

**2026-10-09: T16 merged (PR #16): outdoors profile.** `tilebuild --profile outdoors` builds our own vector schema: OpenMapTiles layer names kept where the data is the same, plus trails from z10 with `sac_scale`, visibility, surface, operator and hiking-route names; peaks from z9 with rank; `outdoor_poi` (trailheads, campsites, shelters, springs, viewpoints, waterfalls) and `natural_line` (cliffs, ridges); glacier, scree and wetland land cover; wilderness boundaries. Archives come out 10–20% smaller than OpenMapTiles (no buildings, house numbers or multilingual names). Verified on a z13 Paradise tile: the Wonderland Trail carries route name, `sac_scale=hiking`, surface and visibility. Gap: the default style doesn't yet draw `outdoor_poi` or `natural_line` and reads ridge names from the OpenMapTiles place; a style update is the follow-up. Wave 3 complete: 16 PRs merged in one day.

**2026-10-09: T22 merged (PR #17): the lidar → 10 m seam on real data.** A new public area, `rainier-lidar-edge` (south-west Mount Rainier NP, where a 2022 lidar project ends at about 46.77°N), finally exercises the lidar-to-10 m transition that was only unit-tested before. At z13 and z14, in hillshade, slope bands and contours, the edge is invisible: no step, no kink, no tone change, and the 60 m feather band can't be seen. Lidar vs 10 m over the covered cells: mean +0.19 m (tree canopy), so no datum bias. The only artifact was in the verifier itself: cells within a feather width of a haloed window edge are compared against a neighbour that can see no-data beyond the window; the check is being narrowed to the exactly-computed overlap (T19).

**2026-10-09: T21 merged (PR #19): datums beyond CONUS.** The geoid grid is now chosen by location: GEOID18 for CONUS and Puerto Rico/USVI (which superseded GEOID12B there), GEOID12B for Alaska (a grid that crosses 180°) and Hawaii (on NAD83(PA11), with its own ellipsoid shift). Sources check geoid coverage before downloading, so areas outside the US no longer fetch 3DEP at all. Checks on bare ground: North Slope tundra agrees with Copernicus to +0.03 m (1.5 m off without the grid), Kona lava plain +0.10 m, Bonneville unchanged at +0.20 m. Denali and Mauna Kea now render from converted 3DEP; the tour is 50/50 with the two new flat areas. Verified against the PROJ grid index and README.

**2026-10-09: T20b merged (PR #18): the browser demo reads the outdoors schema.** POI discs with class glyphs (trailheads, campsites, shelters, springs, viewpoints, waterfalls, parking), cliffs with ticks, named ridges, trails styled by `sac_scale` and faded when hard to follow, wilderness boundaries, lakes drawn over streams; the hillshade now uses the same ancestor fallback as the contours; a collapsible panel for phones. The tour grew to 21 views (phone width, lake views, outdoors views), all clean. The "missing lake names" question had a data answer: the profile only labels lakes about 48 px wide, so names appear from z14.

**2026-10-09: T19 merged (PR #20): sources and formats sweep.** The cache race that crashed cold parallel exports twice is fixed (publish without replace; the loser reads the winner's bytes); open ocean renders and compares without crashing; lidar is skipped where the output is coarser than 20 m per cell (same results, fewer downloads); the lidar index fetch retries with backoff; antimeridian exports write proper bounds; `pmtiles` commands fail with one line on corrupt archives; large extracts stage to disk; and `tiles --verify` no longer compares cells its neighbour computes with more information (T22's false positive). 249 tests; world tour 50/50 at 25 areas.

**2026-10-09: T20a merged (PR #21): renderer style for the outdoors schema.** The devtool renderer now draws everything the outdoors profile emits: POI markers by class, cliffs with ticks on the downhill side (decided from the DEM; 1 of 24 Rainier cliffs flipped from the OSM default), named ridges, trails darker and shorter-dashed as `sac_scale` rises, faded when hard to follow, wilderness boundaries. Trail names can no longer touch. A new golden renders a tiny synthetic outdoors archive built in the test (T5 encoder + T3 writer), so style regressions are caught offline; 255 tests. Wave 4 complete: 21 PRs merged today.

**2026-10-09: T24 merged (PR #23): tracks, waypoints and GPX.** The first phase-2 piece, in `shared` so every platform shares it: waypoint/track/route model with UUID ids, GPX 1.1 import and export on a hand-written XML reader (extensions preserved byte for byte, Garmin heart rate and cadence read), stats with documented rules (3 m turning-point hysteresis for ascent, 0.3 m/s moving threshold), and `devtool gpx info|render|export`. The render draws a track with direction arrows, waypoints and a route over the terrain and OSM layers with an elevation profile strip. The fixture is a hand-written Skyline Trail loop (public trail geometry rounded to 10 m, invented times), never a recording.

**2026-10-09: T23 merged (PR #22): leftover sweep.** Nine recorded follow-ups closed. Notable: one marker table now drives both the renderer and the browser demo, enforced by a test that parses the demo page; cliff ticks are decided per segment from a bilinearly sampled DEM; Mount Rainier Wilderness is a wilderness again in the outdoors profile (OSM tags it `protect_class=1a` plus wilderness tags); no more NaN in tile reports over open ocean. 270 tests, both tours green.

**2026-10-09: T10 merged (PR #24): Android shell.** Phase 1 starts. `androidApp` (Compose + MapLibre Native, min SDK 26) shows a region pack: hillshade from the DEM PMTiles, the outdoors vector PMTiles, and contour tiles generated on the device from the DEM pack by the shared `Contours` + MVT encoder, cached on disk. MapLibre cannot read PMTiles, so a loopback-only HTTP server (port 8686) serves `/dem`, `/osm`, `/contours`, `/glyphs` and `/style.json`; the style is translated from the devtool's `DefaultOsmStyle` by `MapLibreStyle`. Debug endpoints (`/health`, `/screenshot`, `/camera`, `/stats`, `/location`, `/reload`, `/cache/clear`) exist in debug builds only. `shared` gains an `android` target that is only wired when an SDK is present, so SDK-less machines still build; `devtool serve` runs the same routes on the JVM so the phone style can be checked with `demo-check.py` without a device. Verified: 808 JVM tests, lint and license check green (91 Android runtime artifacts, all permissive); debug APK 55.9 MB (MapLibre native libs 9 to 13 MB per ABI, uncompressed); JVM stand-in contour tiles 95 / 183 / 308 ms (min / avg / max) at z13. Android SDK installed under `C:\Users\Dorje\Android\sdk` by `tools/android-sdk.ps1`; the emulator needed Windows Hypervisor Platform, which is now enabled (`emulator -accel-check` passes). First emulator run (Pixel 6 AVD, API 35 x86_64, WHPX, 4 cores, 4 GB): the whole pipeline works on the device. Screenshots at z12, z13.5 and z15 show hillshade, brown contours with index lines, trails, roads, streams, lakes, forest and POI dots; nothing blank or black. Defects: no text renders at all (no names, no peak elevations; only 2 glyph requests were served), `/screenshot?mode=window` returns a white page, the location puck is not visible after a `/location` inject, the attribution shows a literal `&copy;`, and `/health` reports `idle=false` for a minute after load. Footprint: PSS 184 MB total (Java heap 26 MB used of 201 MB max, native 109 MB, mostly MapLibre), APK 58.6 MB, packs 227 MB. Contour tiles: z13 to z15 take 0.2 to 0.5 s, but z12 tiles from DEM z13 take 6.7 to 9.7 s and z7/z8 tiles 7 to 8 s, and generation is serialised on a lock (logcat: monitor contention in `TileService.contourTile`). These are wave 6 tasks. Follow-ups: the contour disk cache is keyed by unit, tile size and z/x/y only, so a replaced DEM pack serves stale contours until `/cache/clear`; the glyph font licence (Noto Sans, OFL 1.1) is only in a script comment, not in `sources.json`; the loopback server has a 4-thread pool with an unbounded queue and no slow-connection guard; contour labels are sparse on the phone.

**2026-10-10: T26 merged (PR #29): contour tile performance and cache identity.** The 7 to 10 s low-zoom contour tiles were three things at once: tiles below z13 traced the DEM at full resolution, one global lock serialised generation, and the map's own rendering competed for the CPU under the software GPU. Now `ContourTiles.plan` picks the DEM zoom per tile zoom (full resolution at z13+, one level coarser at z10 to z12, two below) with slope cut and simplification kept in screen pixels; the DEM pyramid cache is thread-safe with decoding outside the lock; up to cores-1 tiles generate at once with same-tile requests sharing one result; the contour disk cache and the style's tile URLs carry a checksum of the DEM pack (size, mtime, header), so a replaced pack serves fresh contours and old folders are deleted in the background. Verified on the emulator with the host GPU: warm tiles 90 to 480 ms (slowest zoom-in 637 ms), three concurrent, pack swap regenerates without `/cache/clear`; JVM z8 tiles went from 46 to 4 ms average. Cold start after a fresh install still shows 2 to 2.9 s z12 tiles (JIT and first pack reads); Java heap peaks at 82 MB after a zoom-in. Follow-ups: tracing in `render/Contours.kt` dominates at z13+ (170 to 500 ms per tile); about 15% duplicate decodes when threads miss the same tile together; re-pushing identical bytes changes the mtime and regenerates once.

**2026-10-10: T30 merged (PR #30): attribution and sources on the device.** A shared `PackAttribution` composer reads the registry ids that both pack writers already put in PMTiles metadata (the DEM `provenance` array, tilebuild's `dystopo:provenance`), resolves them against `SourceRegistry`, decodes HTML entities and builds per-pack and per-source credits plus a compact line. The app shows the line on the map (tap opens an About screen with pack cards, one card per source with licence and links, the font, and the app licence), and `GET /attribution` returns the same JSON on the device and in `devtool serve`. Noto Sans is registered as a `font` source (OFL-1.1). For the Rainier pack the credits are USGS 3DEP (10 m and 1 m), GEOID18, EGM2008, OSM, OSM water polygons, Natural Earth, OpenMapTiles and Noto Sans; Copernicus is absent because the pack holds no Copernicus pixels (a Glacier/Waterton pack credits both, covered by a test). Follow-ups: friendly names instead of raw ids on pack cards; a short-name field in the registry for the compact line; the `noto-sans` registry url is the glyph template rather than a landing page.

**2026-10-10: T31 merged (PR #28): tracks on the phone.** The first user data on the device, on T24's model. `shared` gains a `TrackStore` interface with an in-memory implementation for tests and a SQLDelight 2.1 implementation (Apache-2.0; schema in `commonMain/sqldelight`, generated code is pure Kotlin, the JDBC driver is test-only and the app uses the Android driver), a recorder (drops fixes worse than 50 m or out of order, new segment after a pause, live T24 stats, closes tracks left recording after a crash) and GPX export. The app records through a foreground service fed by a small `LocationSource` (real GPS, or fixes injected through `POST /location` and `POST /location/replay`, which plays a GPX at a chosen speed); the track is drawn as a magenta line with start and end dots and re-added after style reloads; Compose gives record/stop/waypoint buttons, a live stats card and a tracks list with GPX export to the files dir. Verified: a 100× replay of the Skyline fixture gives identical distance (8141 m), ascent/descent (548/546 m), elevation range and point count in the live `/tracks` stats, the exported GPX and `devtool gpx info`; a force-stop mid-recording keeps both tracks and closes the interrupted one with its points. SQLDelight's migration-verification task is disabled (its SQLite DLL unpack fails under the Gradle daemon on this machine) until the first migration exists. Follow-ups: one unreproduced replay recorded a single point (possible service start race); `GET /tracks` recomputes stats per track; no pause/resume; waypoint dots lack labels; track ids are not kept through GPX.

**2026-10-10: T25 merged (PR #27): labels on the device and text scale.** The missing text was the emulator, not the style: under the software GPU (`-gpu swiftshader_indirect`, which the script used to pass) MapLibre Native draws no symbol layers and no dash patterns at all, while `-gpu host` renders every label, elevation and dashed trail. MapLibre placed the labels in every mode (a new `GET /labels` route counts them per layer), so the symptom was invisible, not absent, text. There was also a real bug: glyph requests queued behind contour tiles on the server's four threads, timed out and were cancelled; glyphs now load straight from `files/glyphs` through a `file://` URL with the HTTP route as fallback, and `/stats` shows zero glyph requests over HTTP. The text-scale setting (the design goal that phone labels are too small) is a multiplier from 0.5 to 3 over the built-in 1.35 applied when the style is built, to text, halos, marker discs, padding and spacing; `AppSettings` persists it, `GET`/`POST /settings` change it live, `devtool serve --text-scale` mirrors it. Verified on the host GPU: peak names with elevations, place names, road names, contour labels and trailheads at z13.5; at 1.6 the text is clearly larger with collision dropping a few labels instead of piling up; the setting survives a restart. Follow-ups: out-of-range values clamp rather than 400; POI discs are circles without collision, so large labels can overlap them (a sprite sheet would fix it); contour labels are still sparse; the emulator script must default to the host GPU (T28).

**2026-10-10: T27 merged (PR #25): debug screenshots, location puck, loopback server hardening.** `mode=window` screenshots were white because MapLibre draws into its own SurfaceView, which a window PixelCopy cannot see; the capture now copies the map surface (snapshot fallback) and composes the Compose overlays on top. MapLibre's own location component never drew (it is symbol layers, see T25), so the puck is now ours: a GeoJSON source with three circle layers fed by `LocationManager` or by injected fixes (an injected fix detaches device fixes until `POST /location?device=1`, so the emulator's default position cannot drag it away). `/health.idle` now means style loaded, no camera transition, no tile in flight or queued, last frame fully rendered, all held for 500 ms, with `notIdleBecause` listing the blockers; measured 5 to 6 s after a camera move. `LocalServer` has a bounded queue (4 workers + 64 queued, then 503), head and per-read deadlines (408), line, header-count and body limits (414/431/413), 400 for non-HTTP input and for tile coordinates that are negative, huge or outside z 0 to 24; `tools/android-probe.py` sends 20 bad requests and checks the server survives (it did, with the refusal counters in `/health`). Attribution text decodes HTML entities. Follow-ups: idle stays false for about a minute after first launch (`frameNotFullyRendered`) until the first camera move; debug routes share the four worker threads with tile requests, so `/health` can wait behind contour tiles; the CLAUDE.md endpoint bullets should mention `notIdleBecause`, `?device=1` and the probe script.

**2026-10-10: T29 merged (PR #26): region packs on the device.** The app manages packs instead of assuming one Rainier pair. A pack is `<name>-dem.pmtiles` plus an optional `<name>-outdoors.pmtiles` (the unnamed pair the push script writes is pack `default`); shared `packs/` code names packs, reads bounds, zoom range, tile counts, size and attribution from the PMTiles header and metadata (checking the file is as long as the header says), fits a zoom to bounds, and defines how a download resumes. The app lists packs, switches the active one (camera fits the bounds, contour cache cleared), deletes them, and downloads a pack pair from a URL with progress, writing `.part` files and persisting state so an interrupted download resumes at startup with a range request. `devtool serve` hosts `out/` with range support and a `?kbps=` throttle as the test host. Verified: a 20 KB/s download force-stopped at 39% resumed from the saved offset with a 206, finished with matching checksums; activating a DEM-only Paradise pack moved the camera and rendered contours without OSM; the static route and pack names reject path traversal. Follow-ups: the cleartext allowance for the emulator host address (10.0.2.2) sits in the main network config rather than a debug-only one; `MainActivity` builds the attribution line and no-pack message once at startup, so they go stale after a pack switch; status-bar icons are white over the light pack screen; the throttle is undocumented in `serve --help`; a missing optional outdoors file shows as `missing` on a `done` job.

**2026-10-10: T32 merged (PR #32): live world mode for the viewer.** The browser demo and `devtool serve` were pack-only, so panning away from Rainier showed nothing. `serve --live` now answers every tile from a pack when one holds it and otherwise live: DEM tiles are rendered on request from the `best` source (3DEP where covered, Copernicus elsewhere, EGM2008) at z6 to z15 the same per-tile way `tiles` does and cached under the shared cache with a record of which sources contributed; contours come from that same pack-then-live DEM with T26's sampling; OSM comes from the pack or from OpenFreeMap (free, no key, ODbL, attribution required; registered in `sources.json`; each tile fetched once with at most four in flight, cached on disk). The page takes `?live=1` (or is opened from serve), shows a source box per visible tile (pack, live-3dep, live-copernicus, openfreemap) and a "Build pack here" helper that prints the `tiles` and `tilebuild` commands for the current view; `/live/sources`, `/live/info`, `/phone` and `/stats` report sources, cache hits and timings. Verified: Mont Blanc, Kilimanjaro, Fiordland and Torres del Paine render from live Copernicus plus OpenFreeMap with contours, labels and peaks (cold views 5 to 14 s, warm under 2.5 s including page load; a cold server renders 96 live DEM tiles at about 580 ms each), Rainier is served from the pack with no live fetches, the Glacier/Waterton border and Fiji across the antimeridian show no seams, repeat views make no new OpenFreeMap requests; pack-mode demo tour 21/21 and world tour 50/50 unchanged. Follow-ups: `CopernicusSource` does not cache missing ocean cells, so live ocean tiles repeat a 404 each time; live DEM tiles pick the source level from each tile's own latitude rather than once per zoom (a row seam is possible in theory, none seen); at a pack edge OSM comes from the pack and stops at its boundary, leaving a strip; live attribution lists every `best` source even outside the US; the Python demo server refuses connections under heavy machine load.

**2026-10-10: T37 merged (PR #33): server pack build from live sources.** "Browse online, download a region" now has its server half. `devtool serve --live` answers `POST /build?name=&bbox=&minZoom=&maxZoom=` with a job queue (one build at a time, four waiting), progress at `GET /build/{id}`, cancel, and limits (20,000 tiles per job, 3° per side, 409 on an existing name unless `overwrite=1`; all env-configurable and reported under `GET /build`). The DEM pack comes from the live per-tile renderer at z6 to z15 with the sources actually used as provenance (Mont Blanc: Copernicus only); the OSM pack is written by the new shared `OsmPackWriter` from OpenFreeMap tiles (fetched once, four in flight, cached) with OpenMapTiles metadata whose profile field says plainly that it is not the outdoors profile and which outdoors layers are absent. Files are staged and renamed atomically, served immediately in live mode and under `/out/` for the phone downloader; the browser's "Build pack here" button has name and zoom fields, progress and cancel. Measured: Mont Blanc z8 to z13 from the browser in 12.7 s (44 + 44 tiles, 7.2 MB + 1.4 MB), a mostly cold z8 to z14 box in 34 s (455 tiles each side, OpenFreeMap at about 20 tiles/s), cancel stops fetches within the four in flight and leaves nothing behind; the built pack renders in pack mode and on the emulator with peaks, glaciers and the cable car. Follow-ups: pack precedence is by the `-outdoors` name, so a plain OpenMapTiles pack could outrank a real outdoors pack where they overlap (rank by `dystopo:profile`); OpenFreeMap's 19 KB `vector_layers` metadata could be trimmed; antimeridian boxes are refused; the DEM pack has no licence field; `maxZoom` above 15 answers 400 rather than 413; devtool stdout is cp1252 on Windows when redirected.

**2026-10-10: T39 merged (PR #34): offline place search.** A search index in `shared` built from a vector pack's names (peaks, places, water, trails, huts, trailheads, parks, from the outdoors or the OpenMapTiles schema), scanning z14, z12 and z10 and merging the same name across tiles; our own versioned, checksummed file format (`<pack>-search.idx`, with a pack fingerprint so a changed pack rebuilds); accent folding with a small table; case-insensitive prefix search where every query word must match, with a one-typo fallback only when a word matches nothing (so `ranier` finds Mount Rainier but short queries are not flooded); ranking by match quality, kind, first-shown zoom, peak elevation and distance to a point. `devtool search build|query`, `GET /search`, and a search box on the map whose results move the camera and drop a marker; the index builds in the background on first open with progress under `search` in `/packs`. Rainier: 77 KB for 2,117 places, 0.9 s to build on the JVM, 1.2 s idle on the emulator (15 s on first open while the map was loading under heavy host load), 0.5 s to load after restart, about 12 ms per query. Skipping layers without names before decoding cut the build six-fold. Follow-ups: `para` does not match Panorama Point (prefix search; `pa` and `pano` do); search covers only the active pack; the result marker has no label; a deleted pack leaves its index file; the Packs and Tracks chips nearly overlap on the map.

**2026-10-10: T35 merged (PR #35): contour tracing speed and warm-up.** The tracer in `render/Contours.kt` no longer builds a HashMap of boxed edge keys and node objects per level: one pass over the cells records which levels each cell crosses, each level is then joined with plain int arrays in scan order (a cell's top and left edges were made by its neighbours), cells entirely above or below every level are skipped with one comparison, and buffers are reused; simplification and the steep-line cut allocate less. Geometry is unchanged up to canonicalisation: an equivalence test keeps the old tracer as a reference, and the four regenerated goldens differ only in label picks and sub-pixel ring start points (viewed); line order is now deterministic (level, open lines, rings, scan order), which also removes a platform-dependent HashMap iteration. Warm-up: after install, pack activation or `/reload` a background-priority daemon thread generates the two lowest pack zooms plus the centre tile at every higher zoom through the same per-tile dedup path, pausing while map requests generate, reported under `/stats` `contourGeneration.warmup`; `DystopoApp.reload()` closes the old service so its warm-up stops. New `devtool contours bench` (28 tiles, z9 to z15, with a trace/cut/simplify/encode split and a checksum). Measured: JVM warm sweep 250 → about 100 to 130 ms, z13 trace 13.8 → 5 ms; emulator fresh install z12 average 2737 → 48 ms with warm-up, worst tile 364 ms (the verifier saw 1184 ms on a loaded host), JIT-warm z13 about 107 to 177 ms. Follow-ups: DEM PNG decode (`BitmapImageDecoder`, `DemPyramid`) is now the main cost at 90 ms warm and up to 950 ms cold; the first z12/z13 view on a cold process can still exceed 1 s; no setting to turn warm-up off; `PackManager` still clears the contour cache on switch (T33); a warm-up tile in progress cannot be pre-empted by a map request.

**2026-10-10: T34 merged (PR #36): desktop leftover sweep.** Fifteen recorded items closed. GPX: malformed files give a one-line usage error, `gpx render` picks its zoom from the fitted bounds (deepest up to 14 that fits about 12 Mpx), `TrackStats.bounds` is padded for single points and straight tracks. Live mode: `CopernicusSource` remembers missing ocean cells in memory and with `.missing` markers (first ocean tile 1.6 s, the next 0.2 s); the live DEM overview level, halo and blend band come from one reference cell size per zoom (cache bumped to `best-v2`); a tile a tilebuild pack only partly covers is the OpenFreeMap tile plus the pack's extra layers merged by name (`pack+openfreemap`); `/live/sources` returns registry ids and an attribution built from what the view used, and the page updates its credit line, so Everest no longer credits 3DEP or geoids; a cold Mont Blanc z12 on an empty cache went idle in 12.8 s (48 live DEM tiles at 1.2 s each), warm 5.5 s. Names: English, else Latin transliteration, else local, with a non-Latin local name on a second line only where MapLibre can shape it (Everest reads "Mount Everest 8849 m"; Devanagari is not drawn). Units: a shared `UnitRegions` defaults to feet inside a coarse US outline and metres elsewhere; live contours take `?units=`, the demo panel has a by-region/feet/metres selector, peak elevations follow the contour unit; pack mode stays in feet. The Python demo server's listen backlog went from 5 to 1024 with daemon threads (a 400-connection burst refused 61 before, none after). Follow-ups: the live page decides units at load, not per pan (the selector overrides); the Android app and static phone style still default to feet and carry the full attribution list (T33/T38); the devtool PNG renderer still labels with the plain `name`; `gpx render` shows a metre profile over feet contours; the old `best-v1` live cache directory is left behind.

**2026-10-10: T28 merged (PR #31): Android tour and multi-emulator scripts.** The device equivalent of the browser tour. `tools/android-check.py` drives the app through the debug server for each view in `tools/android-tour.txt` (10 Rainier views, z8 to z16) and checks style loaded, idle, no tile errors, the contour budget (a warning unless `--strict`), tiles served per covering source, a non-blank screenshot, contour lines drawn, and labels placed *and drawn* (the new `GET /features/text` hides text, snapshots, restores, and counts the pixels that only change when text is hidden, because glyph and feature counts both looked healthy while nothing was drawn under the software GPU); a second "settled" screenshot after the checks fails a view whose pixels change by more than 2%. It writes a JSON summary, per-view PNGs, a contact sheet and the app's logcat warnings. `android-emu.ps1` now runs several instances safely (`-EmuPort`, every adb call scoped to the serial, `-Port` is the host port forwarded to the app's 8686, refuses another AVD's ports and forwards, `-Stop`, `-Delete`, `-AvdHome`, `-Gpu` defaulting to the host GPU when one exists, `-PackName`, writes `local.properties`, reinstalls and re-pushes each run), and `android-push-pack.ps1` targets one device and takes `-Name`. Measured on the host GPU: two instances booted unattended side by side with matching tours and no cross-talk; 10/10 views with legible labels (peaks with elevations, places, lakes, trails, contour labels at z15); under SwiftShader the checker fails 8 to 9 of 10 views, as it should. The settled check found a real app bug: `/screenshot` can return a half-loaded map (no hillshade, no labels) while `/health` says idle, intermittently (2 of 10 views in one run). Follow-ups: that idle definition (T33); `/features` overlaps T25's `/labels`; the summit-z12 view generates no new contour tiles in this tour order; the glyph check alone is weak.

**2026-10-10: T40 merged (PR #37): tracks polish.** Pause and resume (resume starts a new segment), an automatic new segment after a configurable gap (default 5 minutes, `POST /tracks/settings?gapS=`), waypoint labels on the map with a tap-to-rename/delete sheet, per-track stats cached in the store and invalidated on append (`GET /tracks` reports cache misses), an elevation profile panel, GPX import from a body, the files dir or the system picker, and ids kept through GPX via a `dystopo:id` extension (export, delete, re-import gives the same id, points, segments and creation time; re-importing an existing track is skipped). The first real schema migration (`1.sqm`, 20 stats-cache columns, version 2) ships with its own verification: SQLDelight's verifier cannot run here because Gradle starts its workers with an empty environment on Windows, so sqlite-jdbc has nowhere to unpack its DLL; instead `TrackDbMigrationTest` builds every frozen schema (`databases/1.sql`), applies the migrations, compares tables, columns and indexes with a fresh `Track.sq`, and upgrades a version-1 file with rows (removing one ALTER makes it fail). The one-off single-point replay from T31 is explained: replays keep the GPX's old timestamps, and the recorder drops any fix not newer than the last kept one, so a replay into a recording that already holds a fresh GPS or injected fix rejects everything; the replay now warns and ignores GPS for its duration, and `record=` avoids it. Follow-ups: a recorder-level fix for the replay timeline (T33); the profile panel overlaps the attribution strip; the profile draws a vertical step across segment gaps; the on-device version-1 upgrade rests on the author's run.

**2026-10-10: T36 merged (PR #38): sprite sheet, POI collision and contour label density.** The one marker table in `DefaultOsmStyle` now also produces a sprite sheet: `Sprites.kt` lays out one icon per distinct marker (13), and `devtool sprite` draws them with the PNG renderer's own `MarkerShapes` (moved out of `OsmLayer`) so the phone and the desktop renderer match by construction; `devtool serve` and live mode serve the sheet from `out/` (rewriting it when the table changes), the push script copies it to the phone, and the app loads it through a `file://` URL like the glyphs. In `MapLibreStyle` markers are `icon-image` symbols on their label layers, so icons and labels collide properly: the label goes below the icon first, then beside it, `text-optional` follows the devtool layer, and icons scale with the text scale (circle markers remain the fallback without a sheet). Contour labels: spacing 260 → 120 px from z12, `text-max-angle` 45 → 65, label size grows with the square root of the text scale and spacing no longer grows with it (bigger numbers simply fail to fit on bent lines); a `GET /contour-labels` route counts labelled index lines. Cliff tick zoom stops now reach z17. The browser demo uses the sheet when served and a canvas fallback otherwise, and the marker test checks the demo's icon names against the sheet. Measured on the device: no icon/label overlap at text scale 1.6; index lines labelled at z13.5 went from 1 of 7 to 5 or 6 of 7 (2 of 7 at scale 1.6), z14 8 of 8 long lines, z13 south flank 5 → 12 of 16; goldens unchanged. Follow-ups: busy views at large text scales still lose contour labels; a minimum segment length would need a length attribute in the contour tiles; cliff ticks on the phone sit on the OSM right-hand side (only the devtool renderer derives downhill from the DEM); `devtool sprite` and `/contour-labels` are not yet in CLAUDE.md (T33 owns that section); the demo tour run against `serve` fails views that depend on the static demo's layer names.

**2026-10-10: T38 merged (PR #39): online mode on the phone.** "Browse online, download a region" is complete end to end. A server URL setting (`POST /settings?server=`; the debug build on the emulator defaults to the host's live server); tiles outside the active pack's coverage are fetched from the server through a size-capped (256 MB), access-ordered LRU disk cache keyed by server, route and z/x/y, with short timeouts, at most six fetches in flight, one shared fetch per tile, a per-tile negative cache (404 for ten minutes, other failures 30 s) and server backoff from 5 to 60 s with a probe that notices recovery; contours are generated locally (T35's path) over a `DemPyramid` that reads pack tiles where the pack covers and server tiles elsewhere, prefetching the DEM tiles a contour tile needs and not caching a contour tile whose DEM was incomplete; a chip shows online / offline / from cache / pack; "Download this region" posts the view box to the server's `/build`, polls, hands the pair to T29's downloader and activates it; `GET /online`, `/online/download`, `/online/retry`, `/online/cache/clear`. Two latent bugs fixed on the way: MapLibre Native stops every request, including to our loopback server, when Android reports no network, so a pack-only phone in airplane mode would have gone blank (`MapLibre.setConnected(true)` at startup); and glyph ranges fetched while offline were cached empty for the session, so labels stayed missing after reconnect (unreachable glyph fetches answer 503 and the glyph URL carries a generation token). Verified: Mont Blanc with no pack renders fully (42 fetches, then 26 cache hits and no fetches after a restart); offline the app stays alive, cached views render, an unseen area stays blank with no errors, reconnect is noticed in about 4 s; Download this region built and installed an 11.9 MB z8 to z14 pack in about 6 s against a warm server and serves the view offline; the Rainier pack renders in airplane mode. Follow-ups: the download activates the new pack and deactivates the current one (allow several active packs); sprites are not fetched online yet; downloaded packs carry no glyphs; plain-http servers other than the emulator host need a network config entry (T33); `/attribution` does not list online sources; the cache cap is a constant; a 404 outside packs is hidden for ten minutes until `/online/retry`.

**2026-10-10: T33 merged (PR #40): Android leftover sweep.** Sixteen items. The idle bug: `idle` could be true after a complete frame while MapLibre was still drawing follow-up frames (label fade-in, hillshade preparation, placement), so `/screenshot` sometimes captured a half-loaded map; idle now also requires MapLibre's own `isFullyLoaded` (read through guarded reflection, falling back to the old rule if the field is missing) and no frame in the last 500 ms unless MapLibre declared idle after it, `/screenshot` waits for idle and then for two matching snapshots, and `/health` reports how the last screenshot settled; the device tour passed 10/10 nine runs in a row with zero settle change. Units are now the user preference decided above (PLANNING › Units): persisted, unset on install, seeded once from the first pack's centre or else the first fix, never re-seeded, `GET/POST /settings?units=ft|m|unset`, carried in the contour cache key and tile URL and applied through a shared `UnitFormat` to the phone style, online contours, track stats, the profile, the recording notification and search results, with a settings sheet (Units, Text size, Warm-up). Also: debug routes on their own three threads (`/health` answers in 6 to 42 ms during a zoom-in), out-of-range `textScale` is 400, the emulator-host cleartext entry lives only in a debug network config, attribution and the no-pack message refresh on pack switch, no contour-cache clear on switch, a warm-up on/off setting, chips laid out in rows without overlap, dark status-bar icons, `/labels` folded into `/features`, CLAUDE.md endpoint bullets, `__pycache__` ignored, `android-push-pack.ps1 -NoOsm`, and the replay race from T31/T40 fixed properly: a replay into a recording that already holds a newer fix is shifted onto the recording's timeline (108 points kept instead of 1). The "1·3800" contour label was not number formatting: the index line showed through the gap after a narrow "1" because the label halo was 1.6 px; it is 2.6 px now (one faint speck remains in one label). Follow-ups: MapLibre redraws a frame about every half second while the map is still on the emulator (source unknown); switching packs still deletes the other pack's contour cache directory; seeding units from a fix is only unit-tested; the reflection fallback logs nothing if MapLibre renames the field.

**2026-10-10: wave 7 sweeps on main.** Desktop: `check` green, world tour 50/50, browser demo tour 21/21 against `serve --live`, three live views (Everest, Mont Blanc, Fiordland) clean. Device (fresh emulator, host GPU): tour 10/10 twice with zero settle change (one cold z8 contour tile at 2.1 s on the first run, 0.47 s max on the second), online mode renders Mont Blanc with no pack from the live server (38 fetches then 107 cache hits, 0 errors) and switches to metres on request, 20/20 probes pass. Footprint: PSS 193 MB (Java heap 41 MB used of 201, native 93 MB), APK 59.3 MB, Rainier pack 227 MB; warm-up 12 tiles in 0.9 s; warm contour tiles 28 to 145 ms.

**2026-10-10: T43 merged (PR #41): desktop leftover sweep 2.** The viewer's units are now the preference decided above: stored in the browser, seeded once by region while unset (live mode asks `/live/units`, pack mode seeds from the pack), changed only by the Units select (which now has a reset entry), with `?units=` overriding one load; contours, peak elevations and the scale bar follow it in both modes. The devtool PNG renderer prefers `name:en`, then `name_en`, `name:latin`, `name` (an Everest render reads "Mount Everest"); `gpx render` profile labels follow the map units; `serve --live` deletes stale `best-vN` live caches after binding its port (451 MB of `best-v1` freed) and reports it in `/stats`; the demo tour's `req=` expectations are roles mapped to both styles' layer ids, so one tour file passes 21/21 on the static server and on `devtool serve`. T37 items: OpenFreeMap `vector_layers` metadata trimmed from 18.7 to 5.3 KB; an antimeridian build box becomes two packs (`<name>-e`, `<name>-w`, each job naming the other; MapLibre GL JS draws nothing for a pack whose west is greater than its east); DEM packs carry a `license`; `maxZoom` over 15 answers 413 with a hint; devtool stdout is UTF-8 on Windows even when redirected (default JVM args). Cliff ticks in the renderer keep growing by √2 per zoom to z20. CLAUDE.md documents `devtool sprite`, `/contour-labels`, the units preference and the two-target tour. Follow-ups: none recorded; the live server's pack lookup is still not antimeridian-aware (the split works around it).

**2026-10-10: T48 merged (PR #47): continuous integration.** A GitHub Actions job on every push (and manual dispatch; pull requests only from forks, so a branch does not run twice): temurin 21, the Gradle cache, the Android SDK (platform 35) so the app module compiles and its unit tests and lint run, real Arial installed for the golden images, goldens first so a rendering break fails fast, `./gradlew check`, a tiny Rainier pack built in the job (DEM z8 to z10 from USGS, OSM from OpenFreeMap through `/build`, 19 s), `tour --zooms 9 --offline-ok` (a new flag that records any area whose data is not cached as skipped instead of failing; 2 rendered, 23 skipped on the runner, 50/50 locally), and `demo-check.py` with Playwright's Chromium in pack mode; artifacts (tour sheet, screenshots, reports, golden diffs) always uploaded; about 11 minutes; README badge. CI caught a real race: on Linux `Files.move` checks then renames, so two parallel writers of one cache file could both win; `Http.publish` now creates a hard link, which is atomic, and falls back to a move where links are unsupported. Findings: the goldens only match with Arial metrics (DejaVu and Liberation shift labels), so the renderer should bundle an open font later; `gradlew` had no exec bit in git (set now); the CI browser drew far fewer contours at z11 than a local run, likely software rendering speed. Follow-ups: bundle an open font for the renderer; `LiveTiles` calls the HTTP client directly and bypasses the offline hook; bump actions for the Node 20 deprecation.

**2026-10-10: T47 merged (PR #43): map options on the phone.** The "rebuild the map to taste" goal on the device. Settings keys (`GET/POST /settings`, persisted, a Map section in the settings sheet): `hillshade` off/single/multi, `hillshadeIntensity`, `slope` with the CalTopo bands, `contourInterval` auto or a fixed value from a unit-dependent list (applied from contour zoom 11 up; falls back to auto if the units change), `contourLabels` sparse/normal/dense, toggles for trails, water, land cover, POIs and boundaries, and `night`. Every change rebuilds the style live (a contour interval change reopens the tile service); the default style stays byte for byte what it was (unit-tested), and single hillshade at full intensity keeps MapLibre's own hillshade with no shade tiles. Otherwise `/shade/{z}/{x}/{y}.png?v=<pack id>-<options>` serves 256 px overlays built from the DEM with a 2-cell halo so edges match (a test compares a tile straddling the seam with both neighbours), computed at the pack's deepest zoom and scaled beyond it, drawn above land cover and below contours and water, cached on disk like contours with the four most recent option sets per pack, with `/stats` `shadeGeneration`. Two optimisations made it fit: a one-pass gradient/hillshade/slope held equal to the shared `Hillshade` and `Slope` by a test, and a small PNG writer replacing `Bitmap.compress` (40 to 90 ms per tile). Measured: warm shade tiles 34 to 142 ms (median about 80), cold first tiles up to 1 s; device tour 10/10 with defaults. Follow-ups: online contour tiles ignore a fixed interval (`OnlineTiles`); `devtool serve` and the browser phone page do not take the map options; shade tiles pay the DEM decode T42 is removing; shade cache eviction is untested and only bounded when the service starts; the known DEM striping on the upper glaciers shows as noise in the slope bands.

**2026-10-10: T46 merged (PR #42): outdoors packs from the server via Overpass.** Server-built packs no longer have to settle for the plain OpenMapTiles base. `POST /build?profile=outdoors` sends one Overpass request for the box (ways complete, multipolygon and protected-area relations with members, route and admin relations as tags plus member ids; area capped at 0.05 square degrees, 90 s timeout, 128 MiB maxsize, a descriptive User-Agent, the answer cached by box under the shared cache, never retried, a 30 s cool-down after a refusal, endpoint configurable), converts the XML to PBF in `tilebuild` with the protobuf classes Planetiler already bundles (no new dependency), runs the outdoors profile as a subprocess from the installed tilebuild distribution (Planetiler and GeoTools stay out of devtool; cancel kills the process), and publishes `<name>-outdoors.pmtiles` with real provenance and `dystopo:profile=outdoors` (every tilebuild archive now writes that key; Natural Earth is skipped for builds starting at z6 or higher). Oversize boxes get a 413 naming three ways out; a refused or failed Overpass call with `fallback=1` builds from OpenFreeMap with a warning in the job. The browser's build button has a profile select. Measured: the Paradise box builds in 21 to 43 s (Overpass 2 to 3 s for 3 to 4 MB; tilebuild 17 to 39 s, mostly water polygons), a rebuild hits the Overpass cache and takes 13 s; on the phone the pack shows wilderness boundary dots, the Camp Muir route drawn as an alpine route with its name, trails, trailheads, waterfalls and cliff ticks at Mildred Point. Follow-ups: water polygons dominate small builds (skip for inland boxes or cache a pre-clipped copy); the fallback keeps the `-outdoors` file name with profile `openmaptiles`, so precedence must read the profile key (T41 does); the style draws wilderness as a dotted boundary, not a fill; an antimeridian outdoors build makes two Overpass requests; CLAUDE.md should mention `profile=outdoors` and `:tilebuild:installDist`.

**2026-10-10: T44 merged (PR #44): sync core and simulator.** The phase-2 foundation for offline-first user data, in `shared` with no new dependency there. An append-only operation log (put track/waypoint/route/setting, set field, append points, delete) stamped with hybrid logical clocks (wall ms, counter, device id, with the receive rule) and random per-install device ids; our own compact binary format (a recorded fix is 76 bytes, coordinates exact; `DSYNC` v1 bundles carry ops since a version vector, gzipped), with a gap rule keeping each device's ops in order on every replica. Merge is deterministic regardless of arrival order: a delete hides all older ops on the item (a later re-import brings it back), last writer wins per field by HLC, track points are the union of appends ordered by (segment, append HLC, position) with identical appends counted once, so the same GPX imported on two devices is not doubled. Both `TrackStore` implementations are now views of the log, writing the op and the tables in one transaction; migration `2.sqm` (schema 3) adds the sync tables, seeds the log from existing rows on first open and keeps every id (verified by the migration test and on a device). Transports: a `.dsync` file (merge, then rewrite with everything) and HTTP (`/sync/pull`, `/sync/push`) against the devtool server, which stores its replica in a SQLite file under `out/sync/` and runs in both serve modes; the app has "Sync now" and `/sync` routes. The simulator (`devtool sync-sim`) runs in-process peers over a fake network with latency, partitions, drops, duplicates and reordering, deterministic from its seed: 20 seeds converge in memory and with SQLite-backed peers with identical digests; seed 42 (3 peers, 2,000 ops, 5 partitions) delivered 929 of 1,273 messages with 307 cut by partitions and converged in at most 4 rounds after healing; a hostile 5-peer, 20-partition run converges. On two emulators a recorded Skyline track went A → server → B with byte-identical GPX on all three, a concurrent rename 3 s apart resolved to the later name everywhere, and a delete propagated; the same through a file bundle without the server. The design as built is in PLANNING › Sync. Follow-ups: no clock-skew guard (a replica far ahead wins every concurrent edit and a far-future op would pin every clock: reject or clamp remote HLCs more than about a day ahead); bundle parsing has one truncation test and `Hlc`'s `require` throws the wrong exception type (add a truncation sweep); tombstones kept 180 days; a first sync sends the whole log in one request; no authentication; app settings not yet synced; no route screen; CLAUDE.md lacks the sync commands.

**2026-10-10: T45 merged (PR #46): offline route planning over the trail network.** Tap two points, get a route along trails, with no network. A graph per vector pack is built from the z14 tiles (paths, footways, cycleways, bridleways, steps, tracks and roads from service up to primary, in both the outdoors and OpenMapTiles schemas), each line clipped to its own tile so pieces meet at tile borders, points within 1 m merged, junctions added where lines cross except at bridges and tunnels, and elevation taken from the DEM pack at every point plus every 25 m (sampled in DEM tile order so each tile decodes about once); it is stored next to the pack as `<name>-graph.idx` (versioned, checksummed, carrying both packs' fingerprints) and built lazily with progress in `/packs`. Routing is A* with hike and bike cost models (class, surface, difficulty and slope costs) and Naismith time, producing a T24 `Route` with an elevation profile; failures carry a code and a plain reason (start or end off the network, not connected, with the sizes of the two networks). `devtool route` renders a PNG with the profile and prints JSON, `route build` reports sizes, timings and the largest networks; the app has long-press start and end, an orange route line in the T31 overlay style, a card with distance, climb, time and a Hike/Bike switch, save, GPX export and `RouteRoutes`. Measured: the Rainier graph is 652 KB with 8,310 nodes, 9,080 edges and 3,619 km of line in 124 networks (the largest holds 90%), built in 4 to 6 s on the JVM and 6 to 9 s on the emulator, loaded in under 2 s after a restart; Paradise → Panorama Point is 2.9 to 3.2 km, +417 to +455 m, 1:17 to 1:24 hike, found in about 40 ms, following the Skyline Trail past Alta Vista and Glacier Vista, within 10% of the fixture's segment; the OpenMapTiles-schema pack gives the identical route; a Chamonix to Plan de l'Aiguille route works on a Mont Blanc outdoors pack. Follow-ups: the graph file is named from the pack base name, so `rainier-osm` and `rainier-outdoors` overwrite each other's graph; no camera fit to a new route; the 300 m snap limit rejects a start in Chamonix town; the `/route/mode` response is stale until the next read; trail names are not stored, so results list classes rather than trails; the route card and the track profile panel overlap when both are open; deleting a pack leaves its graph file.

**2026-10-10: T41 merged (PR #45): Android leftover sweep 2.** Eleven items. Several packs are on the map at once: a shared `PackPrecedence` ranks archives (outdoors profile ahead of plain OpenMapTiles from `dystopo:profile`, then deeper max zoom, the primary pack, smaller area, name) and for each tile the first pack holding all of it wins; "active" in `/packs` now means the primary pack and `inUse` lists every pack drawn; a region download no longer replaces the current pack. Contour caches are one directory per pack under a single LRU cap, kept across switches and collected only when no pack on the device can use them. Search queries every pack's index (`MultiPackSearch`, duplicates across overlapping packs listed once), the result marker has a name label, and deleting a pack removes its index. The profile panel clears the credit strip and leaves gaps at segment breaks. `/attribution` lists the online sources of the last online view (`/live/sources` ids with per-kind defaults, since the server reports only tiles already served). Cache caps are settings (`onlineCacheMb`, `contourCacheMb`), negative-cached tiles are retried when the camera rests, glyphs and sprites are fetched from the server after a download and once per session. `CliffSides` rewrites cliff lines in served tiles so the ticks sit on the downhill side decided per segment from the DEM, like the desktop renderer. Units seed from the first fix when there is no pack. The half-second redraw on a still map was the location puck: GPS and network each report the same fix every second and every puck update drew a frame; fixes that move it under 0.5 m are skipped (0 frames in 9 s at rest). The rebase onto T43 to T48 kept every hook and added a per-tile DEM lookup to `ShadeService` so shade tiles work over non-primary packs. Verified: three packs in use rendering from their own packs with no online tiles, tour 10/10 three times, per-pack caches survive switches and vanish on delete, Mont Blanc found from the Rainier view, downhill ticks at Russell Cliff, units m from a Mont Blanc fix, both online credits at Kilimanjaro. Follow-ups: a deeper pack outranks the primary within a profile; the credit line covers every installed pack rather than the view; the cliff rewrite is cached in memory only (100 to 400 ms per tile cold); small server-built packs push most edge contour tiles through the online path.

**2026-10-10: T42 merged (PR #48): DEM decode speed.** A pure-Kotlin PNG decoder in `commonMain` (8-bit RGB and RGBA, non-interlaced, filters 0 to 4, zlib through the injected `Decompressor`; anything else throws so the platform decoder takes over) and `TerrariumPngDecoder`, which decodes straight to elevations bit-identically to `Terrarium.elevation`; `DemPyramid` now decodes each tile once into its cached float grid with no Bitmap or ARGB copy and reports fetch and decode time; `ContourTiles.build` checks a cancel flag so a map request can pre-empt a warm-up tile; `TileService` chooses the decoder (`kotlin` by default; `kotlin-zip` and `bitmap` for comparison through `POST /settings?decoder=`), and `GET /dem/decode-bench` times them on the device; `contours bench` gains a decoder flag and a decode split. The surprise was Android's `java.util.zip.Inflater`: about 1 ms alone but 12 to 16 ms per tile with three threads inflating at once, which made the Kotlin decoder lose to `BitmapFactory` until a pure-Kotlin inflate (`Inflate.kt`) replaced it. Results: on the JVM, decode fell from 4.9 to 0.68 ms per 256×256 tile (7×) with identical output, and sampling per zoom from 26 to 58 ms down to 5 to 12 ms; on the emulator the Kotlin path is on par with `BitmapFactory` (2.7 vs 2.3 ms) and 4.5× faster than the zip-backed path, a fresh-install zoom-in peaks at 360 to 700 ms, and warm z13 contour tiles are 90 to 200 ms depending on host load (the decode itself is now 2 to 4 ms; what remains is sampling and tracing under load). Truncated, 16-bit and palette PNGs fail cleanly with the fallback taking over. Follow-ups: `Inflate` verifies no Adler-32 and `PngDecoder` no chunk CRC, so a corrupted IDAT decodes to silently wrong elevations (T50 adds the Adler-32 check; also fall back on any decode error and clamp the IHDR size); the decode bench ignores the decoder setting; warm-up pre-emption could not be triggered on the device (unit-tested only); `RoutePlanner` and devtool still decode with the old paths (T49/T50); about 10% duplicate decodes remain when threads miss the same tile together.

**2026-10-10: wave 8 sweeps on main.** Desktop: `check` green, world tour 50/50, demo tour 21/21 against the live server, three live views clean, the sync simulator converges on seed 42, a Paradise to Panorama Point route gives 3191 m. Device (fresh emulator, host GPU): tour 10/10 twice (one cold 3.7 s contour tile on the first run, none on the second), Rainier and Mont Blanc packs in use together, Kilimanjaro rendered online from the live server with both online credits, slope bands and multidirectional hillshade on request, search across packs, a 107-point replay recorded and synced to the server peer in two pushes (108 ops), 20/20 probes. Footprint: PSS 157 MB (Java heap 42 MB, native 53 MB), APK 59.6 MB, packs 256 MB; warm contour tiles 72 to 206 ms; DEM decode 6 ms average with the Kotlin decoder and no fallbacks. Found: the online-dem attribution entry has an empty attribution string and the compact credit omits online elevation; the Tracks and Record chips can cover a peak label at z14; a sync during a running replay pushes a partial track (expected). These go to T49.

**2026-10-10: T56 merged (PR #50): swissALTI3D elevation source.** The first high-resolution bare-earth source outside the US, proving "best data per region" beyond 3DEP. `SwissProjection` implements the rigorous swisstopo LV95 projection on the Bessel ellipsoid plus the defining CH1903+ → ETRS89 shift in pure Kotlin, matching swisstopo's published test points (Rigi, the Bern origin, the five EUREF points) to 2 mm with heights and under 5 cm without. `SwissAltiSource` finds tiles through the STAC API (index cached per 0.1° cell, all pages followed, the newest survey year wins where two exist), reads the 2 m GeoTIFFs and the 0.5 m ones only where output cells are under 2 m (z16+), is skipped at z11 and below, keeps at most four requests in flight on its own pool, and fetches nothing outside the collection's extent. The heights turned out to be LN02 (EPSG:5728), not LHN95; the PROJ CDN carries the CHGeo2004 grids under CC0, so the conversion is height + CHGeo2004(LN02) − EGM2008 with ETRS89 taken as WGS84 (stated error: centimetres for the geoid and the frame, plus EGM2008's decimetre-level Alpine error that Copernicus shares; measured +0.16 m against Copernicus at the Matterhorn). `best` is now Copernicus, then swissALTI3D, then US, each feathered over 300 m, with `swissSeamStepCm` counters in reports; `GeoTiff` reads striped files (the geoid grids are striped). Registry entries `swissalti3d` (swisstopo free-geodata terms, credit "© swisstopo") and `chgeo2004`; `areas.json` gains `matterhorn` and `jungfrau`; the tour is 54/54. Measured: Matterhorn summit 4475.7 m at z13, 4477.2 at z14, 4477.5 at z15, 4477.8 at z17 (0.5 m data) against 4478; Jungfrau 4156.6 against 4158; border seam step 1.26 m at z12, 0.34 at z13, 0.08 at z14, 0.02 at z15 where a hard cut would step 55 to 60 m, with no visible line or contour kink; `tiles --verify` 0 mismatches. Follow-ups: the z12 seam step comes from `Composite.over` weighting the outermost covered cell at smoothstep(1/feather); starting the ramp half a cell in would halve it; 0.5 m reads are request-heavy (102 range requests for two z17 tiles), so z16 pack builds want a coarser cutoff or whole-file downloads; border tiles are filled across the whole tile, so coverage ends in a 1 km staircase beyond the border (the Matterhorn summit, on the border, is fully Swiss).

**2026-10-10: T52 merged (PR #49): standalone server module.** The always-on peer is now a deployable program. A new `serverlib` JVM module holds the server pieces moved out of devtool with git mv and unchanged package names (live tiles, builds, static files, the sync peer, Overpass, the whole elevation stack including the new Swiss source, caches, DEM PMTiles writing, marker shapes) plus pieces extracted from command files (range sources, sprite sheets, the AWT text measurer, tile-set metadata) and two new files: `PackServer` (the pack-mode body of the old `serve`) and `PackCatalog`. `devtool` depends on it and `serve` is a thin command that behaves as before (demo tour 21/21, live views pass, world tour 54/54).
