The new `server` application reads a JSON config (every key documented in `server.example.json`, unknown keys rejected), writes one JSON log line per request, answers `/healthz`, shuts down gracefully, serves `GET /catalog` (every pack pair in `out/` with name, bounds, zooms, profile, size, build time, provenance, a stable id, per-file SHA-256 and a pack hash, computed in the background and cached next to each pack), gates `/build` and `/sync*` behind an optional bearer token (401 with a `WWW-Authenticate` challenge), applies a CORS allowlist with preflight, and builds style URLs from the request host and forwarded headers so it works behind a TLS reverse proxy; `installDist` plus a Dockerfile (temurin 21 JRE, non-root, volumes for cache and out, port 8080); `docs/SERVER.md` covers local runs, Docker, nginx and Caddy fronting, the config, the catalog, the sync peer, the token, logs and limits. Verified against the installed server: live views from packs and live Copernicus, pack-mode demo tour 21/21, catalog with hashes, token and CORS behaviour, forwarded-header URLs. Follow-ups: the Docker image is unverified on this machine (no Docker) and SIGTERM shutdown is untested on Windows; `serverlib` keeps the `devtool` package name (rename later); `/catalog` can be empty for a couple of seconds after startup while hashes compute; the older `rainier-osm.pmtiles` is served but not catalogued; the browser build button and the phone sync client do not send a token yet; outdoors builds in the image answer 503 because tilebuild is not in it; any devtool file the moved sources reference must live in serverlib (the Swiss source had to follow).

**2026-10-10: T55 merged (PR #51): release build.** A release APK that works. Signing uses a generated, gitignored keystore (`tools/release.keystore` plus `release.properties`, with the keytool command and template in CLAUDE.md) and falls back to the debug key when they are absent so `assembleRelease` runs on any machine; one APK per ABI plus a universal one, switched on only when a release task is named so `check`, CI and the emulator script keep the single debug APK; R8 in full mode with two keep rules for the field and method our MapLibre idle lookup reads by reflection (without them R8 renamed the field and the lookup quietly fell back to the per-frame flag); a `dystopo://map?lat=&lon=&z=` deep link, which is how release builds (no debug routes) are driven; `tools/android-release.ps1` installs the release APK, pushes a pack to the external files dir (release builds cannot use `run-as`), runs a three-view smoke tour through the deep link capturing raw frames, measures `am start -W` and time to first contours, probes the routes and checks logcat. Measured on the emulator: arm64 14.1 MB, armeabi-v7a 10.7 MB, x86 and x86_64 14.4 MB, universal 48.8 MB (four uncompressed MapLibre libraries; debug is 56.9 MB with 25 MB of dex against 2.8 MB in release); cold launch to displayed 1.1 to 1.8 s in release against 4.9 to 8.9 s in debug, first contours 1.6 to 3.7 s against 5.8 to 12 s; tile routes serve and every debug route answers 404 in release; the three smoke views render hillshade, labelled contours, peaks with elevations, icons and trails with no crash in seven launches. Follow-ups: the universal APK exceeds the 40 MB goal (an app bundle would solve distribution); no direct evidence in release that the R8-kept idle lookup is taken rather than the fallback; the release route probe needs a token path once T49 lands (release has no `/health` bootstrap by design); on cold starts the OSM layer lands 4 to 5 s after the contours because contour and OSM tiles share the loopback server's threads.

**2026-10-10: T54 merged (PR #52): phone-to-phone sync on a local network.** The first peer-to-peer step, with no server. A "Share" screen (from the tracks list or `POST /share/start`) starts a second `LocalServer` bound to the LAN (port 8841 or the next free one) that answers only `/share/hello` and the sync routes, every request carrying a 6-digit code (403 without it, 404 for anything else, five wrong codes rotate the code, constant-time compare); it announces the phone through NSD (`_dystopo._tcp`), lists other phones, and shows the code in large type, the phone's addresses and a QR code of `dystopo://share?addr=host:port&code=NNNNNN` drawn by a pure-Kotlin QR encoder (byte mode, level M, versions 1 to 4, matched module for module against python-qrcode). The other phone picks a discovered peer or types the address, enters the code, and runs one pull-merge-push with the existing HTTP transport over a small plain-socket client (the normal client would refuse cleartext to a LAN address in release); closing the screen stops the listener. The shared sync package needed no change. Verified on two emulators: NSD discovery works both ways; through the documented `adb forward` recipe (B reaches A at the host's 10.0.2.2) tracks went both ways with byte-identical GPX, a waypoint rename propagated, a wrong code was refused with nothing moved, the code rotated after five misses, and the listener was gone after `/share/stop` and after the Close chip; the main loopback server was unaffected. Found and fixed on the way: `POST /share/start` set the screen state from a background thread during first draw and crashed. Follow-ups: direct emulator-to-emulator TCP over 10.0.2.x is not reliable on this host (the docs must not promise it; the forward recipe is the tested path); the link is plain HTTP guarded only by the code while the screen is open (a TLS or pre-shared-key step later); no in-app QR scanner and no handler for a tapped `dystopo://share` link; the store-access wrapper duplicates `AppSync`; large first syncs are one request each way until the T51 paging is used here too.

**2026-10-10: T50 merged (PR #55): desktop and server leftover sweep 3.** Ten items. The shared Kotlin DEM decoder now lives in `serverlib` and is used by `PackServer`, `LiveTiles` and `route` (a graph build decodes 512 tiles in 4 s); `LiveTiles` and Overpass go through `Http`, so the offline hook covers every network path (`HttpHookTest`). Map options reach the desktop: `serve` and `serve --live` read hillshade, slope, contour interval, label density, layer toggles and night from the query of `/style.json`, `/phone` and the page (400 on a bad value), serve `/shade/` tiles and interval contours, and the browser demo has a Map options section synced to the URL. Water polygons no longer cost 10 to 15 s per small outdoors build: a one-time 640 KB record index next to the 1.27 GB zip lets inland boxes skip the source (and drop it from provenance) and coastal boxes get a clipped per-box shapefile, so the Paradise build is 4 s warm (Planetiler already filtered features to the bounds; the cost was inflating the whole archive each time). A wilderness fill in both styles and the demo. The renderer bundles Noto Sans Regular and Bold (OFL, 864 KB, with the licence file) loaded with `Font.createFont`, so the goldens match on Linux without installing Arial; all seven were regenerated and viewed and CI lost the font step (green in 8 minutes). `Inflate` verifies Adler-32, the PNG decoder caps the IHDR size at 64 MiB and falls back to the platform decoder on any error. Action bumps for the Node 20 deprecation. Live DEM tiles over Switzerland are labelled `live-swissalti` (28 of 30 tiles at the Matterhorn). CLAUDE.md documents `profile=outdoors`, `:tilebuild:installDist`, serve map options, `route`, `sync-sim`, `sync` and the font. Follow-ups: no coastal box built end to end (the clipped path is unit-tested); goldens contain no wilderness polygon, so the fill shows only in screenshots.

**2026-10-10: T53 merged (PR #53): pack catalog on the phone.** Packs can be browsed and managed from a server instead of built per view. The pack list gains a "From server" section fed by the server's `GET /catalog` (name, area, size, profile, build date, content hash) with download, update when the server's content hash differs from the installed one (only the changed files are fetched, the pack is closed while they are swapped in), and delete; a map overlay draws catalog pack outlines coloured by status (framing all packs only when they lie within 8°, else the nearest); catalog downloads verify each file's SHA-256 against the catalog and keep every file of a pack aside until all verify, so a corrupted download installs nothing (the first version half-installed a pack when the second file failed, now fixed); installed hashes are recorded next to the packs and packs that arrived another way are hashed once in the background; a pure-Kotlin SHA-256 with the standard test vectors; `/packs/catalog`, `/packs/catalog/download`, `GET/POST /packs/update`. The phone's hash formula matches `serverlib`'s `PackCatalog`, enforced by a test that parses the server module's shape. Verified against `serve --live`: a 2.7 MB Paradise pack downloaded with matching hashes, "update available" with an orange outline after a server rebuild with a deeper zoom, the update applied, a flipped byte on the server rejected with a hash mismatch and nothing left on the device, delete removes DEM, vector and search index. Follow-ups: `/packs/catalog/refresh` is 404 (the list refreshes by itself); with the server section open the local pack rows can sit just above the visible top; the online credit chip can cover a catalog label; a stale server `.sha256` sidecar hides server-side corruption from the server itself (the client catches it).

**2026-10-10: T51 merged (PR #56): sync hardening.** T44 made safe to rely on. A received HLC more than one day ahead of local time is still merged but never moves the receiving replica's clock (clamping rather than rejecting, so the simulator can still converge); each replica counts these and shows them in `/sync/status` and the sync sheet; because a local append could no longer assume it sorts last on its track, tracks are rematerialised when a new op is not the newest, and an entity-level receive rule stamps a local change after every op already held on that entity (up to 30 days ahead), at the documented cost that heavy shared editing makes honest peers follow a fast device's clock. All parsing throws only `SyncFormatException`, tested with a truncation at every length, 5,000 byte flips, 4,000 random inputs, HLC range cases and four broken-gzip cases. Pull and push are paged (a continuation vector and a `more` flag; bundle format 2 only when more follows, so files and unpaged replies stay byte-identical to version 1; the server takes `limit` and `maxBytes`): a 50,001-op log syncs in 26 pages of about 52 KB (1.34 MB, 8 s) and a repeat sync is one 73-byte page in 40 ms. Unshipped fixes extend the device's previous append (under 64 points), so 100 fixes are 3 ops instead of 101. Units, text scale and map options are synced settings with per-key last-writer-wins (region-seeded units are not published; received settings are applied without re-sending). A sync status sheet (peer, last sync, pages, pending, conflicts, clock check) and an "edited on 2 devices" note on tracks, with `GET/DELETE /sync/conflicts`; since ops carry no causal history, only one side can see concurrency, so the loser is marked "overwritten" rather than "concurrent". `sync-sim` gains clock skew, crash-restart peers (SQLite peers reopen the same file) and paging; a sync token for the server's bearer gate (`/sync/settings?token=`, `sync with --token`). Verified: 20 seeds converge with 2-day skew and crash-restarts in memory and SQL with identical digests; on two emulators a units change, text scale and slope setting made on A appeared on B after sync, a 107-fix track crossed as four ops with byte-identical GPX, a concurrent rename resolved identically with the conflict shown on the status sheet, and a real 5-day clock skew on one emulator was clamped on the other. Follow-ups: warn the user about skew at sync time by comparing clocks with the peer; per-op causal history would need a format change; a sync token UI; the LAN share link still uses unpaged syncs.

**2026-10-10: T49 merged (PR #54): Android leftover sweep 3.** Fifteen items. The loopback server now requires a random per-launch token on every request (as `?t=` or an `X-Dystopo-Token` header; 401 otherwise), carried by every URL MapLibre loads; debug builds hand the token out only from `GET /health` without a token to loopback callers, which is how the tour, probe and emulator scripts bootstrap, and release builds have no bootstrap and answer 404 to anything but the map routes, so no other app on the device can use the tile or debug routes (the price: MapLibre's own tile cache no longer carries across launches; ours does). Pack precedence puts the primary pack ahead of deeper packs of the same profile; the credit line covers only the packs in view (new `MapViewport`, `mapLine` in `/attribution`), carries the Copernicus or 3DEP text for online views and merges a repeated OSM credit; cliff rewrites are cached on disk under the contour cache; `RoutePlanner` uses the shared Kotlin decoder (512 tiles in 1.6 s on the device); one graph file per vector archive (`rainier-outdoors-graph.idx`), removed with its pack; the camera fits a new route and `/route/mode` answers with the new route already planned; graph format v2 stores trail names (v1 still read), so Paradise to Panorama Point reads "via Skyline Trail, Alta Vista Trail, Skyline Trail"; the route card and profile panel stack above the credit line; shade tiles moved under the contour cache so `contourCacheMb` caps contours, cliffs and shade together while running (16 MB cap held at 16.7 MB with evictions); the Tracks and Record chips fold into one chip (red dot plus distance while recording) that opens the full row, so peak labels stay clear; the three sweep findings (empty online elevation credit, duplicated OSM credit, chips over labels) closed; the release script probes 401 on tiles and 404 on debug routes. Verified with the token: tour 10/10 three times, 28 probes clean, per-view credits at Rainier, Mont Blanc and Kilimanjaro, cliff disk hits after a restart, release deep-link views still render (cold first contours 2.6 s). Follow-ups: the 300 m snap limit still rejects a start in Chamonix town; in debug builds any local app can read the token from `/health`; online contour tiles with a fixed interval already worked after T41/T47 (no change needed).

**2026-10-10: wave 9 sweeps on main.** Desktop: `check` green, world tour 54/54, demo tour 21/21 against the live server, live views at Everest, the Matterhorn (labelled live-swissalti) and Fiordland clean, the sync simulator converges with 2-day skew and crash-restarts, `:server:installDist` builds. Device (fresh emulator, host GPU, with the loopback token): tour 10/10 twice, 28 probes clean including the token probes, the server catalog listed and a pack installed with a matching hash, a sync with the server peer pulled the previous sweep's track and a repeat was a no-op in 16 ms, the Share screen showed its code and QR and closed its port, a planned route with trail names and no overlap, slope bands over a night base, and the release build (15.1 MB) passed its deep-link smoke tour with tiles answering 401 without a token and debug routes 404, cold start 1.1 s to displayed and 2.6 s to first contours. Footprint (debug): PSS 228 MB, Java heap 42 MB, native 117 MB; contour tiles 166 to 172 ms warm at z13; DEM decode 4.9 ms average. Oddities: a `/tracks/record` before a replay leaves an empty track beside the replay's own recording; `/stats` counters reset after a catalog install and sync (the service reopened). Both go to T57.

**2026-10-10: T62 merged (PR #58): public release preparation.** Everything short of flipping the repo public. The README is rewritten for newcomers with seven screenshots of public places under `docs/images/` (browser views of Rainier, Mont Blanc, Everest and the Matterhorn, two phone views, the device tour sheet; 2.3 MB, the only non-golden images allowed), a feature list, build and run steps for desktop, server and phone (walked through by the verifier) and the licensing summary. `CONTRIBUTING.md` says issues are welcome and code contributions wait for a CLA or licence-exception policy (hard rule 3), then explains the task, worktree and verifier workflow, debug before you push, the tours, the privacy hooks, the licence policy and releases. `CHANGELOG.md` is seeded from this log. `tools/notices.py` generates `NOTICES.md` from the licence reports of all six modules (234 artifacts under 17 licences, with the three browser libraries, the bundled Noto Sans and the 19 data sources in their own sections) and checks it is current. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds, on a `v*` tag, the per-ABI release APKs (signed from four `RELEASE_*` secrets when present, otherwise debug-signed with a `-debugsigned` suffix and a warning in the notes), the server and devtool distributions, NOTICES, SHA256SUMS and the tour sheets as a draft release; two throwaway tags produced complete drafts and were deleted. A privacy pass with a new counts-only scan mode of `PrivacyCheck.java` over the full history (112,000 lines), the tracked tree and the text of all 59 pull requests found zero hits in commits and files and one hit in a PR description (an implementer named the Colorado summit from the owner's screenshot request); that description was scrubbed, but GitHub keeps PR-description edit history visible to anyone with access. Owner actions before going public: decide whether to recreate the repository from the git history so the old description text is gone, add the release secrets, push the first tag. Follow-ups: the README status line goes stale at the first release; the live browser view shows OpenFreeMap glacier polygons cut at low-zoom tile edges over Everest; the Mont Blanc summit label is dropped by collision at z12.4 in the browser.

**2026-10-10: T59 merged (PR #57): Norway and Austria bare-earth elevation.** Two more national sources on the Swiss pattern, each with a surprise. Norway: Kartverket publishes no range-readable tiles (the download service is zipped project blocks), but its coverage service cuts any box at any resolution, so `NorwaySource` requests fixed 256×256 blocks on a UTM 33 grid at 1, 2, 4, 8 or 16 m (about 260 KB each, cached on disk for good, four in flight on a per-host pool, georeference and error pages checked); the service returns 0 for sea and land beyond its coverage, which becomes no-data so Copernicus fills there; heights are NN2000, converted through the HREF2018B grid from the PROJ CDN (CC BY 4.0; mean offset +15 cm at Galdhøpiggen). Austria: the BEV ALS DTM is 55 cloud-optimised BigTIFFs of about 7.7 GB each with overviews, read by range (`GeoTiff` gained BigTIFF support), on 50 km squares of EPSG:3035 rather than Austria Lambert, so a pure-Kotlin Lambert azimuthal equal-area (matching the IOGP example within 1 cm) replaced the planned projection; editions from 2022 are EVRF2000 Austria, not GHA, so the BEV geoid grid is used (mean offset −12 cm at the Grossglockner); the 20250915 edition and tile table are pinned constants copied from BEV's ATOM feed. Both are CC BY 4.0, registered (`kartverket-dtm`, `href2018b`, `bev-als-dtm`, `bev-geoid`), inside `best` with 300 m feathering, labelled `live-kartverket` and `live-bev` in live mode; areas `galdhopiggen` and `grossglockner`; the tour is 58/58. Measured: Galdhøpiggen 2466.9 m at z13 and 2468.6 at z15 against 2469.15; Grossglockner 3794.5 and 3797.2 against 3798 (the full-resolution tile maximum is 3797.76); Austria/Italy seam step 1.24 m at z12, 0.27 at z13, 0.02 at z15 where a hard cut would step 48 to 58 m, no visible line; Norway/Sweden seam 0.10 m at z12; `tiles --verify` 0 mismatches. Follow-ups: a true 0 m Norwegian coastline cell is lost to no-data (one cell widened by bilinear propagation); Kartverket's data continues 10 km into Sweden as coarser bare earth before Copernicus takes over (a border clip would need a polygon); the Austrian edition needs a manual bump or an ATOM read; at z16+ Norway costs about one request per 256 m block cold; the geoid registry licence URLs point at PROJ-data READMEs.

**2026-10-10: T64 merged (PR #59): outdoors packs from extracts and from the phone.** Whole regions with the outdoors layers, not just small Overpass boxes. `Extracts` finds Geofabrik `.osm.pbf` files from the server config (`extracts`, `extractsDir`) or the cache's `extracts/` folder, taking each file's bbox from the config, a sidecar JSON or the PBF header and its date from the header; `POST /build?profile=outdoors` picks the smallest covering extract, else Overpass under the area cap, else OpenFreeMap with `fallback=1` (`source=auto|extract|overpass` forces one); jobs report `osmSource` and `provenance.osmExtract` (name, file, date) and the pack metadata carries `dystopo:extract` and `dystopo:clip_bbox`; `GET /build/sources?bbox=` says what a box would use. The phone's "Download this region" now asks for the outdoors profile with fallback and shows the profile and source it got; the browser build button has a source select and a "What would it use?" button; SERVER.md documents the setup and the caveat that a Geofabrik bbox is the region polygon's bounding box, so state-edge boxes may be counted as covered. Measured: the whole Rainier park (−122.05 to −121.4, 46.6 to 47.1) at z8 to z14 built from the 364 MB Washington extract in 42 s cold (Planetiler 15 s with `--bounds`) and 24 s warm, 1,404 tiles per side; a Sunrise-sized box requested from the phone arrived as an outdoors pack in 22 to 28 s; on the device the pack shows wilderness fill and boundary, campgrounds, trail names and cliff ticks at The Ramparts. Found on the way: `BuildRoutes` must default to no extracts (only the serve and server factories scan the folder), or the existing Overpass tests silently built from the state extract. Follow-ups: no automatic extract download (SERVER.md gives the curl line); extract coverage by bbox rather than the Geofabrik `.poly`; the phone does not pre-check `/build/sources`; 413 is an odd status for "no extract covers this box"; the phone's profile retry matches on error text.

**2026-10-10: T60 merged (PR #60): trips with editable routes.** Planning before the trip. Routes are synced entities without any new op type: a `PutRoute` plus two last-writer-wins fields, `path` (one versioned base64url value holding the mode, the via points and every leg with its kind, so two concurrent drags cannot mix one device's via points with another's legs; older builds ignore unknown fields and a pre-T60 route shows as one fixed leg) and `waypoints` (one list). Schema 4 (`3.sqm`, frozen `databases/3.sql`) adds a materialised `route` table with the summary numbers, filled from the log once on first open and verified by a migration test holding a legacy and a planned route in a version-3 file. `RoutePlan` and `RouteEditor` add, insert, move and remove via points, rerouting only the legs next to the change through the T45 router (a via point near a trail snaps to it; a leg with no trail is straight, dashed, with DEM elevation every 25 m); `RouteSummary` gives distance and climb by the T24 rules, time by the T45 hike model, the profile and where each waypoint sits; GPX export writes one `rte` with every point as `rtept` (via points marked with the leg kind) plus `wpt` and our ids, and importing our own export gives back the identical plan while a route from another app becomes via points joined by fixed legs. On the phone: a Routes chip and list sheet, a trip card (totals, distance off trail, profile with waypoint flags, Drag / +Via / +Waypoint, Hike/Bike, remove via, GPX, Delete), a map overlay with numbered via points dragged by touch without panning the map, "Keep" on the long-press route, and debug routes including `/routes/{id}/drag` with real touch events. The simulator creates planned routes with waypoints and drags, adds and removes via points; 20 seeds with skew and crashes converge in memory and SQL. Verified: a four-point Paradise route (trail, trail, straight to a snowfield; 6.9 km, +760 m, 2:39), via drag and two waypoints, GPX byte-identical on A, on B after sync and after delete and re-import, a concurrent rename on A and drag on B both surviving everywhere. Follow-ups: no size cap or compaction for `path` values (about 2.5 kB per edit stays in the log); waypoints are one list, so concurrent waypoint edits keep the later list; no in-app file picker for route import; the Routes chip sits in a third row and can be covered by the tracks list; the camera-fit padding under the trip card was not re-checked on a device.

**2026-10-10: T58 merged (PR #62): desktop and server leftover sweep 4.** Twelve items. The `serverlib` package is now `io.github.jasondorjeshort.dystopo.server` (the mechanical commit was landed on main early so T59 and T64 could merge). The browser's build button sends the server's bearer token (a Token field; `?token=` is stored in the browser and removed from the address bar; build, progress polls, cancel and "What would it use?" all carry it; without one the page explains the 401). The Dockerfile is two-stage from the repo root with tilebuild in the image, and a CI `image` job builds and runs it and checks `/healthz`, the token, the catalog, tilebuild and a clean `docker stop` (SIGTERM shutdown, untested since T52, now proven in CI; Docker is still absent on this machine). `/healthz` reports the catalog warm-up state; single-file packs are catalogued with `single: true`; a hash sidecar counts only with an exact mtime match, `/build` deletes old sidecars, and `POST /catalog/refresh` (token-gated on the server, also on `serve`) rehashes everything and lists what changed, catching a byte flip the sidecar had hidden. `Composite.over` starts its feather ramp half a cell in: the Matterhorn seam step fell from 126 to 32 cm at z12 and 34 to 9 cm at z13 with goldens unchanged. Pack exports read the Swiss 0.5 m product only from z17 (interactive views still from z16). A coastal outdoors build at Cape Flattery went through the installed server with clipped water polygons in 33 s cold and 5.6 s warm and renders water in the browser. The Lake Cliff golden already contained a wilderness polygon, so the T50 note was wrong; a test now asserts the fill. `route --help` names the per-archive graph file. Follow-ups: no local Docker run and no outdoors build inside the container (it would download the aux data per run); the phone's catalog parser skips vector-only single-file packs.

**2026-10-10: T61 merged (PR #61): navigation mode.** Following a route or track in the field. Navigation starts from the current planned route, a saved route by id, a stored track, a file or a GPX body (`POST /nav/start`, or Go on the route card and on each track); the camera has follow, heading-up and free modes (a manual pan drops to free with a Recenter button); the compass comes from the rotation vector sensor plus declination from a pure-Kotlin WMM2025 (the NOAA coefficients embedded unchanged with source and date; all 100 published test values match within 0.006° declination and 0.1 nT per component); a large-type stats bar shows distance and ETA to the next waypoint and the end by the hike model (a planned route's ETA matches its plan within 0.4%); off-route detection (50 m for 10 s by default) raises a red banner with a heading hint and distance to rejoin, a tone, optional vibration and a line to the nearest route point, and clears when back; waypoint and arrival events are listed in `/nav/status`; the screen stays on; a simulated heading comes through `POST /location?bearing=`. Verified on the emulator with a synthetic off-route fixture (the public Skyline loop with a middle section pushed 50 to 150 m east and resampled to 10 s): off-route fired at 72 m off and 4,065 m along, peaked at 150 m, cleared at 5,182 m, then waypoints and arrival, with the alert counts recorded; device tour 10/10. Found on the way: on a loop the first fix matched the end point and reported arrival at once (first fix now matches from the start and arrival needs prior progress); the emulator's own fix is stamped today while replay fixes carry 2025 times, so an older fix now restarts the off-route timer. Follow-ups: the T45 route card stays under the nav bar; the orange planned-route line draws over the purple navigation line; no snooze for the alert; a track's segments are joined by straight lines across gaps; a stale far-away GPS fix is accepted before injected fixes with no "GPS stale" handling; `etaVsPlan` needs a documented unit; no devtool command (the debug routes are the testable surface).

**2026-10-10: T57 merged (PR #63): Android leftover sweep 4.** Fifteen items. In debug builds the debug routes now run on a second loopback server on a random port per launch, announced in logcat (`Dystopo: debug server on port N token T`) and read by a shared `tools/android_debug.py`, so port 8686 serves map routes only behind the token like release and no other app can learn the token from a fixed port (`debugFixedPort=1` restores the old layout; `android-emu.ps1 -Forward` re-forwards after a restart). A `routeSnapM` setting (50 to 1000 m, default 300, in the settings sheet) and off-network messages that name the nearest trail, its distance and direction and the snap value that would reach it; `/route/status` reports the decoder. A clock check at sync time compares the phone's clock with the peer (the server's `Date` header or a `now` field in the share hello) and warns on the sync sheet (a real 3 h skew on the emulator warned and cleared); peer and token fields in the sync sheet, the token never echoed. The LAN share link uses `AppSync.replica` and `AppSync.run` instead of its own store wrapper, syncs in pages, has a `dystopo://share` link handler that prefills the Share screen and never syncs until the user taps Sync, and a QR scanner: CameraX for the preview and frames (Apache-2.0, no ML Kit) with our own pure-Kotlin decoder (versions 1 to 10 at every error-correction level, matched against 13 python-qrcode matrices and tested on damaged, rotated, skewed and noisy images; it reads real screen captures in 32 to 194 ms through `POST /share/scan`). `POST /packs/catalog/refresh`; local pack rows stay visible with the server section open; catalog labels stay clear of the online chips; a replay with `record=` while a track is recording answers 409 (a replay without it adds fixes to the running track); `/stats` counters count from app start and survive tile-service reopens. Not done here: "Download this region" offering outdoors, which T64 delivered. Follow-ups: no unit tests for the 409 and the counters (device-tested); the camera path reading a live QR could not be shown on the emulator (screen captures were decoded instead); shade and cliff counters still reset on reopen.

**2026-10-10: T63 merged (PR #64): cold start and tile scheduling.** The loopback server now has three pools: eight slightly favoured threads for cheap reads (DEM, OSM, glyphs, sprites, the style), three for generation (contours, shade, and cliff rewrites, which run inside OSM requests at generation priority), and the debug pool from T33; a generation request whose client has gone before a worker starts it is abandoned, while started work is always finished and cached (cancelling mid-tile backfired: under load MapLibre gives up on slow requests and asks again, so each retry threw the work away and a debug cold start took 30 s); warm-up still starts at launch (delaying it made the first view slower because it warms the code paths) but waits a second after the map's last contour request; `/stats` `server.pools` reports queue waits and run times per pool; `DemPyramid` shares one fetch and decode between threads that miss the same tile (duplicate decodes 0, the T42 leftover); the release script measures first OSM and OSM lag by colour, has `-ColdCache`, and reports medians. The honest finding: the server was never the bottleneck. Separating the pools did not move the medians (release cold start to a settled first view is 3.1 s warm and 3.7 to 4.2 s cold, under the 5 s target, with tile-pool waits of a few milliseconds), and the OSM layer still lands 1.0 to 1.6 s after the contours, which is MapLibre parsing and laying out the larger outdoors tiles; the 4 to 5 s lag reported in T55 only appears under heavy host load. Follow-ups: the OSM lag target (lighter low-zoom outdoors tiles or fewer symbol layers on the first frame); cliff rewriting still inside the OSM request; generation niceness inconclusive; the pools section is not yet in CLAUDE.md.

**2026-10-10: wave 10 sweeps on main.** Desktop: `check` green, world tour 58/58, demo tour 21/21 on both targets, live views at the four national sources clean (live-swissalti, live-kartverket, live-bev labels present), the sync simulator converges with skew, crash-restarts and paging, `:server:installDist` builds; `tools/notices.py --check` was stale (T57 added CameraX, T59 four data sources), so `NOTICES.md` was regenerated and the notices check joined the sweep list in CLAUDE.md. Device (fresh emulator, host GPU): tour 10/10 twice with contour tiles under 250 ms and the contact sheet clean at every zoom, probe clean, the server catalog listed five packs, a planned route Paradise to Panorama (3.2 km, five trail names) kept and navigated with `/nav/status` 0.7 m off the line, slope bands over multi-direction hillshade, a Skyline replay recorded 8.1 km, a sync with the server peer pulled 111 ops and pushed 13 with no conflicts, Share opened and closed its port, search ranked Panorama Point first; release build (x86_64, 15.8 MB, three runs, medians) start 0.7 s, first contours 1.6 s, first OSM 2.6 s, settled 3.6 s, tiles 401 and debug routes 404 without a token, no crashes. Footprint (debug): PSS 167 to 232 MB, Java heap 42 to 51 MB, native 51 to 119 MB; DEM decode 3.7 ms average with 0 duplicate decodes (T63 confirmed). **2026-10-10: T68 merged (PR #65): route planning in the browser.** `GET /route` and `POST /route/edit` on the live server plan and edit with the phone's shared `RouteEditor`, `Router` and `RouteSummary`; each catalog pack's trail graph is built on first use (Rainier 8310 nodes in about 2 s) as the same `<vector>-graph.idx` file the phone and `devtool route` use, written through a temp file, fingerprinted against the pack and DEM, and guarded per pack; `/route/gpx`, `/route/bundle` (browser route ops to a gzipped sync bundle, validated: one device per bundle, no duplicate HLCs, route fields only), `/route/packs` and `/route/status` complete the surface. `tools/demo/trips.js`, loaded by `index.html` only in live mode, gives click-to-set start and end, click-to-add and drag-to-move via points, dashed straight legs where no trail exists, a card with distance, climb, descent, hike time and an elevation profile, GPX export and "Save as trip". Saving keeps a browser-side replica in localStorage (device id, HLC clock, the same put, path and waypoints ops the phone writes) and pushes it to `/sync/push` with the Token field as the bearer token, so T67's per-user replicas need no change here; a refused push leaves the trip in the browser and says so. `demo-check.py --route` drives the flow with the real mouse. Results: the browser and the phone's trip planner agree exactly on Paradise to Panorama Point (2953.3 m, +401.6 m, 1:16, 223 points); dragging a via point 846 m rerouted to 4493.5 m; a trip saved in the browser synced to an emulator with byte-identical GPX; the verifier's three runs matched. Follow-ups: the browser replica only pushes (phone edits do not come back); no waypoint editing in the browser; `/route*` is outside the bearer gate (cost-bounded by caps, but T67 should put it behind the token); the phone's long-press planner counts climb differently from the trip planner (+416 vs +402 m, to T65); the standalone server must copy `trips.js` beside `index.html` (asked of T67); the demo page's sources box covers the panel at phone width; on the phone a synced trip opened over a long-press route card overlaps it (T65).

**2026-10-10: T67 merged (PR #66): users on the server and trip share links.** The server config takes `users: [{id, token}]` and an `adminToken` (`DYSTOPO_ADMIN_TOKEN` overrides); each user's `/sync*` runs on its own replica `out/sync/<id>.db`, the legacy single `token` still reaches `peer.db`, and the admin token opens only `/admin/users` (list, add, remove, written back to the config through a temp file and rename) and `/sync*` with an explicit `?user=`; tokens are compared with `MessageDigest.isEqual` across every configured token, must differ and be at least 16 characters, and are generated as 32 random bytes by `server --add-user`, `--remove-user`, `--list-users` and `--new-admin-token` (printed once, masked by `--print-config`); the per-request user lives on the gate's own wrapper, not the JDK HttpServer's context-wide attribute map. Share links: `POST /sync/share?route=` makes a 128-bit token, `GET /sync/share` lists, `DELETE /sync/share/<token>` is owner-only; the open `/s/<token>` page draws the route on the live map (map credit collapsed, inline icon) with `.gpx` and `.json` beside it, `no-store`, `no-referrer` and `noindex` headers, served live from the owner's replica so a deleted route gives 404; links persist in `out/sync/shares.json`. The phone's trip card has "Share link" (sync first, ask the server peer, copy) with `/routes/{id}/share` debug routes; devtool has `sync import-route`, `share`, `links`, `unshare`; `/healthz` reports users, replicas and share links; the standalone server now ships `trips.js` beside `index.html`. Verified with two emulators as two users against a private server instance (each replica held only its user's data, the shared link's GPX matched the phone's byte for byte, revocation gave 404 on page, GPX and JSON) and by the verifier on the JVM (400 parallel requests, 0 crossed; non-owner revoke 404; admin on `/sync` without `?user=` 400). Follow-ups: share-link tokens appear in request logs (redact the path or document it); `config.json` holds plaintext tokens with default permissions (document chmod 600); `/route*` from T68 is still outside the gate; the Sync section of PLANNING, ARCHITECTURE and CLAUDE.md need the users model (next sweep); `android-emu.ps1` hung once after pushing packs to a second emulator (T65 area); a phone's default peer is the coordinator's 8765 server, so tests must set their peer first.

**2026-10-10: T69 merged (PR #67): Canada HRDEM and New Zealand LINZ elevation.** Two national lidar sources read straight from the AWS open data registry, checked before coding: NRCan's HRDEM (one Cloud-Optimized BigTIFF per 500 km square of EPSG:3979 Canada Atlas Lambert at 1 m and 2 m, LZW, no-data -32767, Open Government Licence Canada 2.0; 56 and 64 tiles pinned from the STAC) and LINZ's 1 m DEM (one COG per NZTopo50 sheet in NZTM2000, 424 sheets pinned, no-data -9999, CC BY 4.0), the latter LERC-compressed, so a pure-Kotlin LERC2 decoder (versions 3 to 5, Fletcher-32, bit-stuffing and lookup tables; Huffman and multi-depth refused) now lives beside the source with a real LINZ tile as a fixture. `CanadaLambert` implements EPSG method 9802 and reproduces the IOGP worked example to 1 cm; vertical datums CGVD2013 (CGG2013a, relative to NAD83(CSRS), so the NAD83 height shift is added) and NZVD2016 come from the PROJ CDN grids; `GeoTiff` learned int32 samples with GDAL scale and offset for the Canadian grid. In `best`, HRDEM sits above 3DEP because the US 10 m base reaches into southern British Columbia at coarser resolution, and both sources are gated to their country boxes, so Rainier opens no tile (counters confirm). Results: Aoraki 1 m summit 3719.8 m against the 3724 m survey (the sheet's own maximum plus the datum offset, so exact); Mount Robson has no HRDEM lidar (nearest 25 km west), so it stays Copernicus at -39 m and the board's 5 m criterion is met only for New Zealand; a new `garibaldi` area shows HRDEM over 55% of its box with a 4 cm feathered seam step against 30.8 m for a hard cut and no visible seam line; tour 64/64 (three new areas); `tiles --verify` 0 mismatches; live views labelled `live-linz` and `live-hrdem`; NOTICES.md regenerated. Follow-ups: cap LERC allocations against the expected size and bound the mask and lookup loops on corrupt input; move `Lerc2` into shared tiff; lazy tile-index read in `GeoTiff` so the 15 MB HRDEM 1 m index is not re-read per fetch (1 m is used only for cells of 1 m or less until then); a refresh tool and a test for the pinned tile and sheet lists; the Canada box also opens empty tiles over the US border band north of 48.2; the 3DEP 10 m base is blocky north of 49 near Garibaldi; `notices.py --check` reports false when the Android SDK is not configured.

**2026-10-10: T70 merged (PR #68): map quality sweep.** Contour labels at large text scales: above scale 1.0 the label text grows with the fourth root of the scale, anchors sit closer together up to z13.5 and open up again by z15, the angle limit is 75 degrees and padding fixed, so long index lines labelled went from 3 to 9 of 24 at scale 2.0 (and the summit at z15 reads 11 of 12) while scale 1.0 is pinned unchanged by a test (spacing 120, padding 4, angle 65); a first version repeated a label every 140 px along the summit contours and was fixed before the screenshots. Label priority was already places and peaks before POIs with contour labels last in both the phone style and the PNG renderer; tests now pin the order and the exact sort-key JSON. The Mont Blanc summit label lost at z12 was the demo page's own peaks layer lacking a sort key (now sorted by elevation); the phone style already drew it. Local-script second line on the phone: the name rule also uses `name` when it starts outside the Latin blocks (outdoors packs have no `name:nonlatin`), glyph ranges grow from 3 to 12 (Greek, Cyrillic, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai, Georgian, Vietnamese; `GlyphRangesTest` keeps the push script's list equal to the style's); seen on the emulator at the Dead Sea and Everest. Sprite icons render at 4x so they stay sharp to text scale about 3. A new `StyleParityTest` renders the Lake Cliff golden scene and checks the phone style against every feature: 1 difference, 0 unexplained (the cliff tick side: the renderer draws ticks downhill from the DEM, the phone right of the line with the server rewriting directions). The 3DEP striping on Rainier's upper glaciers is in the 10 m product (no 1 m lidar there): a directional filter (`DYSTOPO_3DEP_DESTRIPE=1`, off by default, level 0 reads only) cuts the striping signature in rows from 2.15% to 0.89% of blocks while moving 0.56% of cells by more than 10 cm (max 1.15 m, RMS 2.4 cm, summit unchanged); most glacier texture is real crevasse relief, so it stays off. Verified on an emulator at scales 1.6 and 2.0 at z13.5 and z15 (no overlaps, contour labels present), demo tour 21/21, no golden changed. Follow-ups: a phone that already holds the 2x sprite sheet or the three old glyph ranges keeps them (the online install skips existing files), so icons draw at half size and non-Latin second lines are blank until sprites and glyphs are re-pushed or a version stamp forces a refetch; the PNG renderer still draws one name line (bundled font is Latin-only); finer detection of beading along crevasse lines; one online-mode tile at the Dead Sea stayed paper-coloured over the sea; the demo tour's z9 Rainier view fails its source check against a live server by design and the message should say so.

**2026-10-10: T65 merged (PR #69): Android leftover sweep 5.** The wave 10 device findings: one ascent and descent figure per route, computed once from the path and shown on the route card, in `/route`, on the kept route, the trip card and navigation (Paradise to Panorama Point +401.6 m everywhere; the router's own +416.4/-16.5 m stays under `route.graph`); `/nav/start?route=<kept id>` navigates a kept route from the trip store (`etaVsPlan` 1.0 at the first fix, now documented as a pace ratio) and the trip card has Go; starting navigation closes the route editor and hides the route card, and the Tracks and Routes chips stack below the stats card (`layout` reports it); the two-segment replay was the Skyline fixture's 15-minute stop with no fixes split by the 5-minute gap rule, which now also requires more than 50 m of movement (loop recorded as 1 segment, 107 points, 8141 m); `/route/clear` closes the editor so the kept line goes while the route stays stored; `android-emu.ps1` prints its progress when piped and Gradle's stderr no longer aborts `-Build`. Earlier items: the navigation line draws above planned, kept and track lines; `POST /nav/snooze` and two banner buttons (a snoozed replay made 0 tones with 2 alerts held, 2 tones unsnoozed); a fix over 30 s old is dropped, a last-known fix over 2 km from the line does not start navigation, and the bar says how long GPS has been silent; per-waypoint route writes (`wpt:<id>`) so concurrent waypoint edits merge, compaction keeps one `path` per route but never drops waypoint writes (a first version did and diverged on simulator seed 59, now a regression test), paths over 256 KiB are refused with a clear message; an Import button opens the system file picker for GPX; vector-only single-file packs appear in the phone catalog as map-only packs that can never become primary; unit tests for the replay 409 and the cumulative counters; shade and cliff counters survive `/reload`; `devtool nav replay` runs the off-route logic on the JVM (Skyline off-route walk: 1 event, 149.8 m max off the line, arrived). Verified: `check` green, sync-sim 20/20 converged in memory and with the SQL store paged at 50 with waypoint ops, device tour 10/10 three runs by the implementer and once by the verifier (contact sheet clean), probe clean. Follow-ups: `Trips.refresh` can close a route that was just opened (worked around in the import picker; the fix belongs in trips/); the stale-fix thresholds and snooze length are constants, not settings; remote ops over sync are not size-capped; PLANNING's Sync section should describe `wpt:` fields and the compaction rule (documented in CLAUDE.md and `RouteOps.kt` for now); replay recording at rates 50 to 100 dropped fixes in another agent's run; emulators launched by `android-emu.ps1` inside a background tool call die when the call ends; agents sharing one scratchpad directory overwrote each other's files (use unique names).

**2026-10-10: T72 merged (PR #71): encrypted relay sync and an encrypted LAN link.** Pure-Kotlin ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439), HMAC-SHA256 (RFC 4231) and HKDF (RFC 5869) in `shared/.../sync/crypto/`, all vectors passing plus a byte-for-byte cross-check against the JDK's own ChaCha20-Poly1305 and a BigInteger Poly1305 on random inputs; no new dependency and no existing sync file edited (the frame lives in the new `EncryptedBundle.kt`). Every message derives a fresh key and nonce by HKDF from the canonical share code and a random 16-byte salt sent in the clear; the salt and a purpose id are bound into the authenticated data, so a frame cannot be moved between purposes or messages, LAN replies carry the request's salt as context, and the LAN listener (`POST /share/sealed`) authenticates before recording a salt in a 50k-entry replay guard (replay 409, wrong code 403, the old plain routes 426). The share code is now 16 Crockford base32 characters (80 bits) for both the LAN link and the relay, replacing T54's 6 digits, because a 6-digit code falls to an offline search of one recorded message in about a second. The relay (`RelayRoutes.kt`, `relay` config block) keeps ciphertext blobs in memory under a slot that is a separate HKDF of the code (the server never sees code or plaintext), for 15 minutes after the last write, with 4 sides per slot, 16 MiB per blob and 120 requests a minute per address, open without the bearer token (the gate exempts exactly `/sync/relay` and `/sync/relay/*`, tested against `/sync/relayanything`); the phone's Share screen gains "via server" (New code, Join) with `/share/relay` debug routes, and the QR encoder goes to version 6 for the longer link. The threat model in the Sync section states what the server sees (sizes, timing, addresses, which sides share a slot), that a malicious server can withhold or roll back blobs (harmless, the merge is idempotent), and what is not provided (no forward secrecy, no device identity; a PAKE would give both). Verified: two emulators with no connection to each other exchanged up to four tracks through a private relay with byte-identical GPX while the server's own replica held nothing; a JDK-only check opened the phones' stored blobs with the code and was refused under wrong codes; a polling bug that re-posted identical logs (16 blobs for one exchange) was found on device and fixed so the relay goes quiet once both sides are caught up (converged 5.8 s after the second phone joined, 2 blobs); the verifier's server run gave 401 on `/sync/pull` without a token, 413 over the blob cap and 429 past the rate limit, with no slot ids in request logs. Follow-ups: `dystopo://share?relay=` links need the same tap-to-confirm care as LAN links; a PAKE; the pack-mode `devtool serve` has no relay; relay blobs are memory-only across restarts; the "via server" section sits below the fold; an open relay can be filled within the rate limit and TTL (accepted); emulators launched inside a background tool call die with the call (use a foreground launch or `Win32_Process.Create`).

**2026-10-10: T66 merged (PR #70): desktop and server leftover sweep 5.** Norway: a Kartverket block that is 0 everywhere is still no-data, but a 0 in a block with land is sea level and is held at exactly 0 through the datum shift (a first version shifted those zeros and the 0 m contour traced every fill block; a second kept zeros only near the shore and Copernicus blended 300 m inland along every coast; both caught in the PNGs), and cells in Sweden, Finland and Russia are masked with Natural Earth 1:10m admin-0 rings stored as a 149 kB resource written by the new `devtool borders` command (5.19 M cells masked on a border box at z13; Kartverket west of the line, Copernicus east, no artefact). Austria: the BEV edition and tile table come from BEV's ATOM feed, cached a week with the stale copy used when BEV is down and the pinned 20250915 table as last fallback (Grossglockner edition read from the feed, 3797.2 m). The ten geoid `licenseUrl` entries (including T69's two, fixed during the rebase) point at the licences themselves, not PROJ-data READMEs, with a test that keeps it so. Extracts on demand: an `extractDownloads` config key (`DYSTOPO_EXTRACT_DOWNLOADS` for `serve`) lists Geofabrik regions whose `.poly` polygon decides coverage (holes handled; corners, bbox and edge crossings checked), and any extract with a `.poly` beside it is covered by the polygon rather than its bbox; Liechtenstein downloaded in 2.1 s and built in 6.9 s. A box no extract covers answers 422; 413 is kept only for a forced `source=overpass` over the area cap; the phone's `fallback=1` path is unaffected (202). Glacier polygons cut at tile seams were real only at z7 to z9 (15 to 40% of seam samples mismatched against 2 to 5% from z10), so live `/osm` drops OpenFreeMap glaciers below z10. `demo-check` counts idle only after two polls in a row and retries a missed view once (`--retries`, reported under `notIdle` so a masked stall stays visible). CI's image job builds an outdoors pack inside the container from a Geofabrik Liechtenstein extract the server downloads itself, with the 1.45 GB aux data cached between runs (cold 65.7 s, warm 13.5 s). Verified twice: `check` green, notices up to date, tour 64/64, demo tour 21/21 three runs on both targets, live views as above, 422 and 413 paths, glacier features absent at z8 and z9 and present at z10 and z11, all geoid licence links HTTP 200, CI green. **2026-10-10: testing gaps found at the first alpha (and what closes them).** The first install on a real phone (release build, empty, UI only) found a dead end no sweep could see, because every device check shared four assumptions: an emulator (whose debug network config allows plain HTTP to the host), the debug build driven through debug routes, a pack already pushed, and the server set through the debug port. The pattern generalises: everything ran in the one environment the agents have, on one API 35 image with warm caches. Gaps listed and scheduled: the release build beyond the map (R8 has never seen tracks, trips, routing, sync, share or search); upgrade in place over a published build; permission prompts; background recording through doze; private strings in the built APK; a device matrix (minimum API, tablet, font scale 2.0, dark mode, a non-English locale); throttled and failing networks; routing and search tours beside the terrain tour (T81); the server as a long-lived service with a soak, a server probe and an operations section (T82); a fresh Windows clone with an empty cache (checklist); and what only the owner can do: a real walk with GPS on and the screen off on mobile data. Process changes the same day: the `first-run` sweep part (T79), `ScreenNamesTest`, the rule that an agent installs the APK from the draft release itself before anyone publishes, a Haiku sweep runner with Sonnet escalation, and the owner's publishing policy (an alpha after every green wave sweep on GitHub Releases; beta on a public channel later). Also that day: the phone was found to need a server or pack files because the elevation readers live in `serverlib`; T80 moves them to `shared` and leads wave 12.

**2026-10-10: T76 merged (PR #74): tiled routing graph and cross-pack routes.** The graph file is `DYSG` version 3: a tile directory and one checksummed body per z11 tile, read on demand (a version 2 file is refused with a message and rebuilt; that is the migration; devtool, phone and server now read and write the same version). `Router` is an interface; `TileRouter` implements it as a bidirectional A* with average potentials (Goldberg; termination `topF + topB >= mu`; the heuristic stays admissible because surface, SAC and flag multipliers are at least 1 and the slope penalty is additive), loading tiles as it reaches them through a shared LRU capped at 32 MB (hits re-insert, reads and decodes run outside the lock, a search holds its tiles through a `View`). Nodes within 4 m of a z14 border join matching nodes in neighbouring tiles and in other packs within 2 m at zero cost, which is how a route crosses a pack border, including overlapping packs; within one file joins are exact. The phone and the server plan over every pack whose box comes within 5 km of the route's points, the reported or primary pack first; `RoutePlanner.ensure` no longer keys on the primary pack. Measured (written into PLANNING › Routing): the whole Rainier graph is 3.45 MB in memory; at z11 the Paradise route reads 2 of 20 tiles, 0.61 MB plus 57 KB of search state (z10 2.2 MB, z12 0.35 MB, so z11); the phone built the tiled graph in 2.5 s. Results: Paradise to Panorama Point is identical to the old router in distance, ascent and cost (2953.3 m, +416.4 m, cost 6284.5; one extra point where a z11 border cuts an edge), the phone reports the T68 numbers (+401.6 m, 1:16); Rainier cut in two at 46.8000 N routes across the border with the same points on the phone, in the trip editor and in the browser (one continuous line, no straight leg; climb +404.6 m instead of +401.6 m because each half's DEM ends at the border); the two-pack stitch test matches a single union pack within 0.1% for overlapping, abutting and reversed packs; device tour 10/10 (with the server setting cleared, see the glyph defect under T74). Follow-ups: when two packs are not connected the search runs until one side is exhausted, bounded by the smaller component and not by a cost cap, and the `View`'s strong references put that memory outside the cache cap (a per-tile component summary before T77 or large packs); a corrupt tile is treated as absent with no negative cache and no rebuild trigger; a pack still building or failed is silently omitted once the wait expires; the cross-pack join does not check class, bridge, tunnel or layer; adjacent packs show a hillshade seam where their DEMs end; `devtool route` takes one `--pack` and reads the whole graph; `devtool serve` keeps serving a pack moved out of `out/` and answers 500 until restarted; one-ways (T73) and long distance (T77) are designed for, not built.

**2026-10-10: T82 merged (PR #75): server soak and operations.** The standalone server had only ever run for seconds. Now: `/metrics` (JSON, or Prometheus text with `?format=prometheus`: per-route counts with a bounded label set, status classes, latency percentiles, heap, threads, disk under `cache/` and `out/`; unauthenticated like `/healthz`, so restrict it at the proxy or set `metrics.enabled: false`); a log cap (string fields cut at 512 characters, `log.file` with size rotation by `log.maxMb` and `log.keep`, every line still JSON; share-link tokens redacted as `/s/…` and `/sync/share/…`, closing the T67 flag); body caps in the gate for declared and chunked bodies (256 MiB for sync, the relay's own blob cap, 16 MiB elsewhere; 413 with `Connection: close`) and HTTP limits (`http.maxRequestSeconds` 60, `idleSeconds` 30, `maxConnections` 1000; before this, 64 slow clients stalled `/healthz` indefinitely); `tools/server-probe.py` (73 probes over every route family and the HTTP layer, with a `lenient` list for answers accepted but wrong: the empty 200 for a missing glyph range, silent `units=` fallback, the gzip bomb answering 400 only because the heap is large); `tools/server-soak.ps1` (own server under mixed load, samples every 10 minutes, SVG chart, stops only its own PID; works in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and pwsh); a 20-minute `server-soak` job in the nightly workflow (Linux: 2,683 requests, 0 failures, live heap flat at 158 MB, probe 73/73); and an Operations section in SERVER.md (systemd and compose with Caddy, why the reverse proxy matters, backups of `out/sync`, Prometheus monitoring, the log cap, how to run the soak and probe). Docker is not installed on this machine, so the soaks run the `installDist` distribution directly; CI still builds and checks the image. Findings from the first 40 minutes of two 12-hour soaks (final charts follow as a PR comment): (1) a memory leak in serverlib `Geoids.offsetCache`, which keeps the geoid offsets of every US live window ever rendered (2.19 GB of 2.27 GB of float arrays after 25 minutes, 3,023 entries of 0.73 MB, keys hashing into tree bins; with a 1 GB heap the server died in two minutes on Rainier edge tiles), assigned to T80 which is moving the readers to shared; (2) `RemoteTiff.prefetch` blocks on the shared ForkJoin pool, which grows to its 287-thread cap (also T80); (3) `/sync/push` has no cap on the inflated bundle size (T74); (4) the live cache grew 1.45 GB in 40 minutes with no cap (T74); the packs-only soak stayed flat at 145 MB live heap with p95 35 ms for warm tiles. Verified by the verifier on its own server: probe 72/72 plus one skipped, 413s with the server alive, slow clients held off, 9 rotations under a 14,000-request burst with every line parseable, the soak SVG viewed. Follow-ups: contour and sync routes answer 500 without a `request_failed` event; `/healthz` logs `connectionLost` at info during idle tests (noise).

**2026-10-10: T80 merged (PR #76): the phone reads the elevation sources itself.** The owner expected the phone to download data and build maps itself; the alpha needed a server or pack files because the readers lived in `serverlib`. Now every elevation reader (3DEP, Copernicus, swissALTI3D, Kartverket, BEV, HRDEM, LINZ, `BestSource`, `RemoteTiff`, the geoid grids, `OverviewLevels`, the Norway border mask) lives in `shared/.../sources/elevation/` in commonMain, with `expect`/`actual` for the HTTP client, the file cache, thread pools, zlib and the image decoder in a new `jvmCommonMain` source set; `serverlib` keeps every old name as an alias and its cache paths and formats, and the verifier found the server byte-identical to main on the Rainier, Matterhorn and Galdhøpiggen renders (same maxima, same provenance), tour 64/64, demo tour 21/21, live views 6/6. On the phone `online/DirectSources.kt` is a second, additive target behind `OnlineFetcher`: with no server set, DEM tiles are rendered on the phone the way `LiveTiles.generateBest` does and vector tiles come from OpenFreeMap; a configured server takes precedence (the source label flips and the direct counters stop). "Download the map around me" builds the pack on the phone (`LocalPackBuild.kt`) and installs it through the import path, so the box is a pinned pack; the card gained "Show the map around me". Every BMP glyph range of the three fonts and the sprite sheet are bundled in the APK (+6 MB per ABI; a range the fonts lack is a valid empty PBF; `BundledAssetsTest`), the loopback glyph route answers a valid empty PBF instead of zero bytes and an empty file counts as a miss (T76's finding), and the About screen lists every source with its licence. From the T82 soak: `Geoids.offsetCache` is an LRU capped at 32 MB with a proper key (`GeoidOffsetCacheTest` renders 3,000 soak-sized windows), and the readers' parallel block reads run on a fixed 16-thread pool instead of the common ForkJoin pool. `BestSource` skips Copernicus when a regional layer covers the whole window and nothing above it has holes (identical output, half the bytes of a US view). Caps on the phone: 6 renders and 16 source requests at once, a session limit (`directSessionMb`, default 300, choices 100 MB, 300 MB, 1 GB in Settings › Sources) with a message on the map and the card when reached, Wi-Fi only by default (`directOnMobileData`, default off, with a message on mobile data), a 512 MB source cache trimmed in the background, DEM rendered to z12 and synthesized deeper, and a tile not ready in 8 s answers 503 with one style reload when rendering finishes (MapLibre never retries a timed-out tile but does retry a 503). Measured on the emulator, release build, by taps only: a cold Rainier or Mont Blanc view settles with contours in 39 to 69 s (network-bound; the 15 s target is missed), the download takes 7 to 22 s, and the same place offline renders in 12 to 20 s; a cold z12 view costs 66 to 103 MB of source bytes in about 150 requests (the readers already pick a 20 m overview for z12; by zoom over a 4 by 5 tile Rainier area: z12 25.1 MB, z11 10.0, z10 7.0, z9 5.5; most of the total is the z8 to z11 ancestors read for contour sampling, which share no blocks with the z12 tiles), 0.2 MB warm. Follow-ups: sample contours from the hillshade's own zoom on the phone (the biggest saving), then a coarser overview for interactive views; the 15 s cold-view target; the first-view contours are coarse until the pack is built; the online indicator still shows the green dot on mobile data with direct fetching off; the debug build's `/health` map.idle stayed false for minutes while fully rendered (look before the sweep relies on debug idle); OpenFreeMap tiles below z10 reach the phone with glacier polygons the server strips; geoid offset computation is serialised under one lock (server contention); the sweep's `--direct` step is wired by T81's files; the About compact line truncates.

**2026-10-11: T81 merged (PR #77): release realism sweep.** The sweep now sees what a user sees. New parts, each on the release APK driven by `tools/android-ui.py` (uiautomator text, taps, screencaps; other apps' permission dialogs are refused, never granted): `features` (search, a route kept as a trip, the Routes list, a recorded track, GPX export, sync with the sweep's server, the sync sheet, the Share code, Settings: 11 steps, the first time R8 saw those paths); `upgrade` (install the latest published release from GitHub, make a track, a trip, metric units and a pack, install the new build with its commit-count version code, check all survive: 203 to 229); `permissions` (location and notifications, allowed and denied); `background` (recording under forced doze: 40 fixes fed, 57 points kept); `network` (a download killed at 9.5 MB of 144 MB and a sync cut by a stall proxy both fail cleanly and succeed on retry); `apk-strings` (658,529 strings from the APK's dex through `PrivacyCheck`: 0 term hits); `routes` (7 public routes within 0.1% of pre-T76 numbers) and `search` (10 expected top hits); and an opt-in `-Matrix` (API 26 phone, font scale 2.0, dark mode, a German tablet) with new `android-emu.ps1` flags (`-Api`, `-Device`, `-Locale`, `-FontScale`, `-Dark`, `-NetSpeed`/`-NetDelay`, `-Feature`, `-BootOnly`; images before API 30 need `-Feature GLESDynamicVersion` or MapLibre gets GLES 2 and crashes). The sweep builds the release once with the commit-count version code, like `release.yml`, so a sweep build installs over a published one. Defects the new parts found and fixed in the same PR: (1) the app crashed on Android 8 to 10 whenever GPS started or stopped, because the three location listeners were lambdas and `LocationListener`'s other methods only gained defaults in API 30 (`AbstractMethodError`; minSdk is 26, so every published build so far crashed there): a `tracking.locationListener {}` helper implements all four methods, `LocationListenerTest` scans the sources and fails on a lambda, and the API 26 matrix row guards it (the verifier toggled location and navigated on API 26 with the release build, no crash); (2) `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` was declared but never requested, so on Android 13+ the recording notification never appeared: `tracking/NotificationPrompt.kt` asks once when Record is tapped, posts the notification when allowed, keeps recording when refused. Filed: at font scale 2.0 the Settings chip covers the search box; no GLES 3 declaration in the manifest; a cut download shows only "Connection reset"; "1 packs"; the Routes list's empty text names a debug route; `android-release.ps1`'s final chmod fails on API 26 (harmless); the upgrade part downloads the latest release by tag without checking SHA256SUMS.txt or the certificate (harden); `LocationListenerTest` is line-based; the sweep's first-run part does not yet call T80's `--direct` step; the USB phone path is documented but untested; `TrackControls.kt` was touched for the prompt (not in the declared list). CLAUDE.md: the sweep bullet, a release-realism paragraph, and the release checklist now says one agent installs the APKs from the draft release itself, on a USB phone with `-Serial` when one is connected.

**2026-10-11: T83 merged (PR #78): in-app updates from GitHub Releases, no Wi-Fi gates.** The owner asked how users get updates and past the "Google doesn't know this publisher" block; the durable answer to the second (developer verification under the owner's name) is on the owner list, and the first is now in the app. Release builds check the public releases API 15 s after start and then daily, on any network, and on "Check for updates" in Settings › Updates; a release is newer when its `version.json` versionCode (a new release asset: tag, versionCode, versionName, apk, sha256 of the universal APK) is higher, or by tag numbers for releases without one; the universal APK is downloaded to app-private cache as `<name>.part` and offered only when its SHA-256 matches `SHA256SUMS.txt` (and `version.json`), it is the same package with a strictly higher versionCode, and its signing certificates equal the running app's exactly (full certificate bytes, sorted list, so multi-signer or rotated keys are refused); with the default feed every asset URL and redirect must be https on github.com, api.github.com or `*.githubusercontent.com`; a verified update shows "Update to <version>" on the map and as a notification, Install hashes the bytes as it writes them into the `PackageInstaller` session and abandons it on a mismatch, the result receiver is not exported, "Not now" or a cancelled installer declines that version for the daily check, refusals show their reason (both hashes, both certificate prefixes). Debug builds never check on their own and alone honour `?api=`; release builds honour an `update-api.txt` override only for private hosts (the sweep's mock). `UpdateFeedTest` covers feed parsing, draft skipping, version comparison, the sums file, the allowlist (look-alike hosts, http, credentials) and the verify decision with fake package facts. No Wi-Fi gates (owner's rule): `directOnMobileData` defaults to on, the "needs Wi-Fi" messages are gone, the session limit is the only brake; the update check never consumes the direct-sources session limit. Tools: `android-ui.py update` and a sweep `update` part (a second release of the same commit with versionCode +1 and a mock feed), the sweep's `upgrade` part falls back to the universal APK, a `mobile-data` step in `android-firstrun.py --direct`. Verified by taps on release builds (implementer and verifier): a lower version stays quiet, a wrong checksum and a debug-signed APK are refused with their messages, a good build is offered and installed through the system installer (Settings, Allow from this source, Update) with the track, units and pack intact; a real API check followed the asset redirect through the allowlist, matched the published sums and refused the APK on a debug key; the verifier's emulator did reach the mobile-data step on a cellular-only network (contours at 5.9 s). Follow-ups: the first real mobile-data test is the owner's phone; `Signing` itself has no unit test (covered on device); the sweep's `first-run` part still does not call `--direct`.
