**2026-10-11: T82's 12-hour soaks (pre-T80 build, started the evening before).** Soak A (Rainier pack plus a fresh live view every 2 minutes, port 9090): 102,435 requests, 4 failures (the cold live contour 500s seen in the first hour), live heap after full GC 6 MB to 2,733 MB and still climbing 21 MB per hour in the second half, working set 6.5 GB and flat in the second half, server alive at the end, final probe 73/73, no OutOfMemory lines; this is the `Geoids.offsetCache` leak T82 found at 25 minutes and T80 fixed (bounded LRU, 32 MB), so the chart is the before picture. Soak B (Mont Blanc pack only, port 9091): 103,856 requests, 0 failures, live heap 6 MB to 149 MB (0.4 MB per hour in the second half), working set peaked at 2.0 GB and ended at 1.0 GB, alive, probe 73/73. The T82 agent did not post the charts as planned; the numbers come from `summary.json` and `soak.jsonl` under `E:\dystopo-soak`. Follow-up: rerun soak A on the current main to confirm the fix (memory flat after warm-up is the bar the T82 Done-when set).

**2026-10-11: process base and what differs (Hatchery).** The owner published Hatchery, a base repository distilled from this project's working method; this session co-authors it. Adopted here today: `docs/HANDOFF.md` (Now, Next, Waiting on user, Open findings; 40-line cap) imported into `CLAUDE.md` so every session and subagent sees where work stands, and a PreToolUse hook (`.claude/hooks/usage-gate.js`, from the base) that refuses new Agent launches at 75% of either meter, with `DYSTOPO_GATE_OVERRIDE=1` for a verifier of work already in flight. Recorded differences, each with its reason: (1) Dystopo runs waves of owned tasks in worktrees with a verifier per PR and implementers that push their own `task/*` branches, so the base's "only the pusher pushes, only the runner commits" rules do not apply here (proposal 0003 in Hatchery makes them phase-dependent); (2) `docs/PLANNING.md` is a dated development log far over the base's 300-line cap, because the measurements and findings behind each merge are the record this project relies on, and the base's `docs/findings/` plus `docs/reference/` split is the better home for the environment facts now in `CLAUDE.md` (T84 moves them); (3) the gate threshold is 75% not 50%, and in-flight verification finishes past it (owner, 2026-10-10); (4) `docs/TASKS.md` is the board and `CHANGELOG.md` exists because releases are published, both absent from the base by design. Note on commit 44b2d0f: its message names the Hatchery proposal, but it contains this repository's handoff, planning and board edits; a `cd` into the Hatchery clone inside the Bash call did not take effect, the commit landed here, and the coordinator reported a Hatchery push that had not happened until the owner asked (rule above: report from the remote, use `git -C`). Contributed to Hatchery today: proposal 0003 (wave workflow), three resolved findings and a Windows reference file, and backlog items for the private-terms guard, the verify agent, the release and sweep skills, and the first-real-device lesson.

**2026-10-12: the post-fix 12-hour soak (current main, after the T80 geoid cache fix).** Same live-column load as T82 soak A (Rainier pack plus a fresh live view every 2 minutes), 2 GB heap, root `E:\dystopo-soak\T82c`: 102,792 requests, 1 failure (one 500), live heap after full GC 6 MB to 174 MB and +0.2 MB per hour in the second half (before the fix: 2,733 MB and +21 MB per hour), threads 114 at the end (before: 375 to 407, the ForkJoin fix), handles 1,371 flat, working set about 1 GB, warm pack tile p95 37 ms, live p95 1.9 s, alive at the end, final probe 73/73 with the gzip bomb listed as known. The T82 bar (memory flat after warm-up) is met. What still grows is the uncapped live cache, 2.8 GB and 28k files in 12 hours, which is T74. Numbers posted on PR #75. Follow-up: the soak script exits 1 on this run although every check passed; make its verdict print the reason.

**2026-10-12: T73 merged (PR #79): drive mode, to the trailhead and between trailheads.** A third routing mode, scoped to what a pack covers and labelled in all three editors as not a road navigator. The outdoors profile is version 2 (`dystopo:profile_version` in the metadata): roads and tracks carry `oneway` (including `-1` and roundabouts), `motor_access` (`motor_vehicle`, `motorcar`, `access`; `private` and `no` closed, `destination` allowed only as a route's first or last leg) and `maxspeed` from z12, and road `name`/`ref` at z14 so a drive can name its roads. Graph tiles are DYSG v4: a direction bit, a car-access bit, a speed byte and a drive-capability bit per edge; v3 files are refused and rebuilt. Drive cost is travel time (`maxspeed` where tagged, else class defaults); motorways and trunks are in the graph and closed to hiking and cycling, and a trail that crosses a trunk stays unjoined. The phone's route card has a Drive chip on its own row, the trip card and the browser editor offer the mode, GPX marks it (`<dystopo:mode>drive</dystopo:mode>` in trips, `<type>drive</type>` in route files), and a pack built before profile 2 answers `drive_unsupported` with a message naming the file and saying to build or download it again while hike and bike work as before; OpenMapTiles-schema packs count as drivable (they carry `oneway` and `access`) but no such pack was on hand to check. Verified by implementer and verifier on separate emulators and rebuilt packs: Paradise to the Sunrise visitor centre in drive mode is 81,937 m on Longmire-to-Paradise Road, Stevens Canyon Road, SR 123, Mather Memorial Parkway and Sunrise Park Road (secondary 48.0 km, tertiary 27.7 km, primary 5.8 km, service 0.4 km; no path or footway), and the same pair on foot is 47.9 km of footway on the Wonderland Trail with no trunk; Paradise to Panorama Point is unchanged on the new pack and the new code (hike 2,953.3 m, +416.4 m, cost 6,284.5; bike 3,006.7 m, cost 13,997.5), the fixture test pins those numbers with drive data present; routing tour 8/8 (the seven public routes plus the drive line), device tour 10/10, the browser editor plans the drive against `serve --live` with `demo-check.py --route-mode drive`; `DriveRoutingTest` covers one-way loops, private roads, destination-only legs, trunks and speed limits, the unjoined crossing, the old-pack refusal and the graph round trip. Out of scope, written in PLANNING › Routing: turn restrictions, seasonal closures, traffic, routing across packs (T77). Noted: the trip card's drive time comes from `RouteSummary` at a flat 45 km/h (1:49) while the router's own time uses its per-class speeds (1:26); the verifier judged the card's figure the more realistic and the router's a routing cost, so the two stay until unified. Side effect: hike and bike `via` lists now name a road a trail follows for 30 m or more (reflection-pinnacle reads Wonderland Trail, Stevens Canyon Road, Pinnacle Peak Trail); accurate, sometimes noisy. First task merged from the owner's second account on the development machine, with the SDK, cache and packs set up fresh that afternoon by a chore agent.

**2026-10-12: T74 merged (PR #80): cache coherence and storage budgets.** Every cache now names its inputs and is bounded (Design goals, principles 2 and 3). Server: `serverlib/.../BoundedCache.kt` puts a byte cap with least-recently-used eviction on `live/`, `openfreemap/` and a new `derived/` store that holds server contour and shade tiles keyed like the phone's (version, options, DEM content version); caps come from the `cache` config block (`DYSTOPO_CACHE_CAPS` and `DYSTOPO_TILE_CACHE` for `devtool serve`) and `/stats` has a `caches` section. `GET /version` is new and `/dem` and `/osm` tiles carry `X-Dystopo-Version`; `GET /assets/manifest.json` lists glyphs and sprites with SHA-256 hashes (format in shared `style/AssetManifest.kt`). A glyph range is the file, a valid empty range for a font the style uses, or a 404, never an empty 200 (T76's finding, closed); an unknown `units=` is a 400; `/sync/push` inflates at most 64 MiB and takes at most 100,000 operations, else 413 (the T82 soak's gzip bomb, closed). Phone: `OnlineFetcher` takes a list of sources (for T78); cache keys carry each route's content version, checked at most every 15 s and read from the tile header, and a change drops the old version's tiles and the contours and shade made from them and reloads the style; an empty glyph 200 counts as a miss; online shade tiles are disk-cached like online contours; glyph and sprite installs follow the server's manifest hashes, so the T70 sprite change reaches existing installs. A Storage screen (Settings › Storage, `GET/POST /storage`, `/storage/clear`, `/storage/ui`) shows packs, online tiles and derived caches with bytes used, the budgets as chips and a Clear per store; `contourCacheMb` and `onlineCacheMb` stay the underlying settings. Verified by implementer and verifier on separate emulators and servers: `server-probe.py` 73/73 with 0 unexpected and the units, empty-glyph and gzip-bomb entries strict (400, 404 or a 29-byte empty PBF, 413 with the server alive); under caps of 40/20/10 MB and about 1,600 tile requests over 14 public areas no `/stats` sample exceeded a cap (live peak 41,942,962 of 41,943,040 bytes with 1,362 evictions, derived 10,485,746 of 10,485,760); with two standalone servers holding different DEMs behind one phone URL the phone's `/dem/12/662/1444.png` bytes changed to the second server's after one request without `POST /cache/clear` (`versionChanges` 1, the old `dem@` and `contours@` directories left with 0 files), reached only once the first server's process was stopped because the phone's keep-alive connection was pooled (not a user-facing defect: a different URL is a different key and a restarted server closes its connections); a recoloured sprite sheet replaced `sprite.png` and `sprite@2x.png` on the device within about 75 s with matching hashes and the icons recoloured on the map; the Storage screen's apparent bytes equal the file sums exactly and `du` differs by 8 KB per directory; device tour 10/10 twice. Found on main and left for the sweep task: `/contours/9/33/227.pbf` and `9/34/227.pbf` (Hawaii, z9) answer 500 with an `IndexOutOfBoundsException` in contour generation; Flevoland is a flat polder with no contours and is no use as a contour check in the demo tour. Not done: the server's source downloads (`3dep13/`, `copernicus30/`, the national readers) are still uncapped, stated in SERVER.md; the Storage screen's last budget chip (2048 MB) is clipped at the right edge; pinned tiles (T75) count against `onlineCacheMb` and are not budgeted separately.

**2026-10-12: T75 merged (PR #81): areas of interest, manifests, corridors, pinning, prefetch.** "Tell us where you are going" (Design goals, principle 4). `shared/.../areas/` has `AreaOfInterest` (a box or a route corridor with a buffer, plus a zoom range), the corridor tile cover, line slicing, pins, and the manifest format, written field by field in SERVER.md › Areas of interest because a peer phone will publish the same thing in T78; the proposal and the rejected alternatives are in the Design goals section for T84's ADR. The server answers `POST /area` (behind the token like `/build`): for a box the pack is built through the same `/build` job and the manifest carries a SHA-256 per archive, so the phone verifies the download like a catalog download; for a corridor it is the list of DEM and vector tiles with sizes. On the phone a trip's new Offline button (`POST /areas/download?trip=`) fetches the corridor into the online tile cache and pins it; with no server set the phone makes the same lists itself and fetches through its direct sources; "Download this region" is the box case and the pack it installs is recorded as a pinned area; while navigating online the next 3 km of the line are fetched ahead in 1 km segments with a 300 m buffer at z12 to z15 and pinned until navigation stops; an Offline areas sheet (from the pack list; `/areas`, `/areas/{id}[/manifest|/pin]`, `/areas/trim`, `/areas/prefetch`, `/areas/ui`) shows each area's bytes, coverage, Pin/Unpin (corridors; pack-backed areas stay until removed), Show (outline on the map) and Remove. `TileCache` gained a `keep` predicate (default `Areas.isPinned`) that the eviction loop honours, four lines, the hook T74 was told about; after T74 merged the area code was rebased onto its versioned cache keys (`<source>/dem@<version>/z/x/y`) and checks every fetcher source. Verified by implementer and verifier on separate emulators against their own `serve --live`: the Skyline loop's route imported as a trip ("Paradise to Panorama Point", 2.5 km) has a corridor of 236 tiles (143 DEM z6 to z15, 93 vector z8 to z14, estimated 29.6 MB), downloaded in 3.6 to 19 s (16 to 18 MB fetched, the rest already cached, 0 failed); with airplane mode on and the app restarted, navigation on it drew hillshade, labelled contours, named trails and the nav line at z8, z10, z12, z13 and z15 with 0 fetches (`/nav/status` active, 23 m off the line); `POST /areas/trim?mb=1` evicted 99 to 101 unpinned tiles and kept 236 of 236 corridor tiles, and after Unpin the same trim left 4 (`PinnedTrimTest` covers both); prefetch on a cold cache with the track replayed at 20x had every segment complete before arrival (3 of 3 on the trip, 9 of 9 on the 8.1 km loop; segments fetched 1, 2 and 3 km ahead); a box download through `/area` at Paradise took about 21 s with both archives' hashes matching the server's files and the pack rendering 330 minor contours at z13; device tour 10/10 (7/10 once on a cold boot when z8 to z11 never went idle; the rerun passed). Found for the sweep task: on the verifier's first launch after install the app crashed in `NavSession.getUi` with "Reading a state that was created after the snapshot was taken" (Compose, `NavOverlay.kt`; a relaunch worked; not T75's code). Limits stated in the PR: a corridor has no trail graph or search index (those come from vector packs; the manifest says so); pinned bytes count against `onlineCacheMb` and are not budgeted separately (T74 follow-up); contour pre-generation for a corridor is wired but unmeasured; `tools/android-push-pack.ps1` hung once after pushing. Docs: SERVER.md's `/area` response was checked field by field against the live JSON; the trip import route `POST /routes/import` is used by the checks but not named in the area docs.

**2026-10-13: T77 merged (PR #83): long-distance routing, a drive overlay over the graph tiles.** The comparison was written into PLANNING › Routing before building. GraphHopper 9.1 was measured on the Washington extract (import plus car contraction hierarchies in 88 s, about 2 GB peak heap, 327 MB on disk, warm queries 3 to 15 ms; it agrees with our router on Paradise to Sunrise, 81.936 km against 81,937 m, and on Seattle to Yakima, 229.3 against 229.4 km) and rejected because it pulls in `osmosis-osm-binary` (LGPL-3.0, never in `shared` or the app) and because it is a second network imported from the PBF, separate from the pack graphs; Valhalla was not measured (no Docker here) and has the same second-network objection; per-pack contraction hierarchies do not compose across packs without exactly the border overlay that was built instead. Built: `shared/.../routing/DriveOverlay.kt`, a partition overlay over our own z11 graph tiles, used by the server and the phone alike: each cell is one tile of one pack and keeps the tile's border nodes where a tertiary or larger road crosses, with the exact in-tile drive cost between each pair; drives of 40 km or more (`MIN_DISTANCE_M`) search the tiles within 5 km of their ends in full and everything else through the cells, and search again in full if that finds nothing; the server builds every cell of a pack in the background once its graph opens (in memory, rebuilt after a restart; 65,536 cells on the server, 4,096 on the phone); `overlay=0` turns it off per request. The phone plans long drives through the server as a remote router (`RemoteRouter.kt` behind `FallbackRouter`), keeps the answer as a trip, and T75's corridor download makes it navigable offline; the browser demo has a long-route check (`demo-check.py --long-route`, `--long-route-packs`, `--long-route-ms`). Also fixed: `TileRouter` tells graph files apart by `GraphSource` rather than by `GraphPiece.archive`, an index that came from whichever router first loaded the piece into the shared tile cache, so a later router with its packs in another order could mistake two packs for one (the T76 cross-pack join depended on it; no new test for the other-order case). Verified by implementer and verifier on separate emulators and servers with three Washington packs (`rainier-north`, `rainier`, `rainier-east`, built from the extract, plus a statewide pack and a Grand Canyon pack): Seattle to Yakima in drive mode is 231,994 m across the three packs, 18.6 s cold with the graphs built inside the request, 1.4 s cold with the graphs on disk (0.66 s for the implementer), 0.23 to 0.41 s warm (48 overlay tiles), and the same metre with the overlay off; Paradise to Yakima 147,978 m across two packs, 13 to 42 ms warm; on the statewide pack Bellingham to Walla Walla (570 km) takes 0.23 to 0.30 s with the overlay (47k nodes settled, 14 MB) against 0.8 to 1.25 s without (1.17M nodes, 197 MB), and all six Washington drives gave the same route either way (a synthetic worst-case grid costs 1 to 5% more on two of five drives); on the phone with only the Rainier pack, Paradise to Yakima came back through the server in 0.7 s (`memory.remote`, `remoteMs 62`) and was kept as a trip, its corridor downloaded (633 tiles and 35 MB at z8 to z13 for the implementer; 2,101 tiles and 134 MB at the default z8 to z15 for the verifier), and with airplane mode on and the app restarted navigation was active 83.9 km along, in `rainier-east` which the phone never had, with hillshade, contours, roads, the lake and the nav line at z10, z12 and z13; Ellensburg to Yakima by bike planned offline with `rainier-east` pushed at 54,827 m (49.5 s including the graph build on the device); the Grand Canyon walk South Kaibab trailhead to the river to Bright Angel trailhead planned offline at 25.2 km (implementer) and 27,961 m with the verifier's own points, server and phone identical; the true rim to rim does not route because the extract has no `highway` way for the North Kaibab Trail between Phantom Ranch and Ribbon Falls; routing tour 8/8, device tour 10/10, golden routes unchanged (hike 2,953.3 m, +416.4 m; bike 3,006.7 m; drive 81,937 m), `licenseCheck` green with no GraphHopper or osmosis artifact anywhere; the browser long-route check planned Seattle to Yakima in 362 ms across the three packs. New York to Los Angeles is an extrapolation in PLANNING, not a measurement. Not done: no second overlay level (z8); overlay cells are not kept on disk; no test for two packs loaded in another order. Seen again: the first-launch crash in `NavSession.getUi` (T86), twice on the implementer's emulator, not on the verifier's; the trip card can draw over a previous route card.

**2026-10-13: T85 merged (PR #82): contours from the hillshade zoom on the phone (the T80 follow-ups).** A cold direct-sources view now costs a third of T80's bytes. `ContourTilePlan` gained a `sampleAtDemZoom` option (`shared/.../tiles/ContourTiles.kt`, with tests; packs, the server and the JVM stand-in keep their plans and the goldens are untouched): when the phone renders terrain itself, a contour plan samples the DEM at the zoom the hillshade is rendered at and averages the grid down, so a view reads each source block once instead of the z8 to z11 ancestors. The biggest single saving was not in the brief: each contour tile's 4-cell halo was rendering a whole ring of neighbouring DEM tiles (14 of a z12 view's 20 renders); direct contours now leave the out-of-view halo empty, render only tiles in view, and drop work for zooms the camera has left. DEM tiles below z12 read a coarser source overview (`COARSER_OVERVIEW_BELOW`), measured and kept: about half the bytes on a z10 view with no visible hillshade loss. A T80 bug found on the way: the "reload after rendering" never reloaded anything, because MapLibre keeps a tile it got a 503 for when the reloaded style is identical, which is why first views only ever showed the coarse fallback; tile URLs now carry the reload count. The verifier's FIX round: with a DEM pack installed, MapLibre's z7 contour request (below the pack's minimum zoom) fell through to the direct path, which answered 503 for good, so tour views at z8 and z10 never reached idle (155 s each); now any contour tile a DEM pack's bounds overlap takes the pre-T85 path, a direct tile not at the view's zoom is made from the DEM tiles already rendered with the rest left empty and is not cached (so a later view at that zoom makes it whole), and a 503 is answered only while a DEM render is pending, 6 s at most. Also from T80's list: the online indicator shows "Paused" with a grey dot while direct fetching is off or the session limit is reached; the About credit listed "New" for LINZ and now says LINZ. Measured, cold z12 view by "Show the map around me" on the emulator (debug build; before = T80's build): Rainier 81.4 MB, 151 requests, 49 renders, to 21.6 to 28.7 MB, 55 to 68 requests, 17 to 19 renders (26 to 35%; the verifiers measured 28.7 and 24.8 MB); Mont Blanc 101.4 MB, 130 requests, 48 renders, to 36.9 to 46.4 MB, 51 to 86 requests, 17 to 20 renders; a Rainier z10 view 55.6 to 28.4 MB with the coarser overview. Release build by taps (`android-firstrun.py --direct`): first contours at Rainier 62 to 28 s (verifier 32 s), settled 67 to 44 to 48 s; Mont Blanc 58 to 31 s, settled 64 to 38 s; before, both places showed only the coarse fallback (about 2% of pixels), after, the full z12 contours (about 10%); the download and offline steps pass, and the 15 s cold-view target is still missed because six parallel 6 to 15 s renders take the time. Contours against the pack over Paradise: eleven tiles at z10 to z12 have the same levels from the pack and from direct sources, line counts within 4.1% (the worst tile; z12 identical). After the fix: device tour 10/10 four times on the implementer's emulator and twice on the verifier's, with and without a server (views 0 and 1 at 17.5 and 20.7 s with no server, 9.5 and 8.7 s with one); `/contours/7/20/44.pbf` 200 with the pack; a cold direct z12 view reached idle at z12, z10 and z8 with the 503 count flat once the renders finished (19 transient ones); a half-screen pan at z12 filled the newly exposed tiles with no cut edge. Files outside the Owns line: one hook line in `OnlineTiles.kt`, five lines and an import in `TileService.kt`, one line each in `OnlineOverlay.kt` and `PackAttribution.kt`. Known limits: a contour tile at the view edge can keep a half-cell gap at its out-of-view seam (a fraction of a pixel at z12; not observed); packs built on the phone now get the coarser-overview z8 to z11 tiles (not visible in the offline screenshot); `tools/android-push-pack.ps1` hung once after pushing. The first verifier was cut off by a usage limit after an hour and the second reused its emulator and worktree.

**2026-10-13: T86 merged (PR #85): first-launch crash in the navigation overlay.** Three agents on 2026-10-12 saw the app die on its first launch after a fresh install with `IllegalStateException: Reading a state that was created after the snapshot was taken` in `NavSession.getUi`, read by `NavOverlay` in the map screen; a relaunch always worked, and the device tools reported it only as "no loaded style within 120 s". Cause: `NavSession.ui` was a `mutableStateOf` inside the `NavSession` object, and T75's `AheadPrefetch` poll thread reads `NavSession.navigator` about 2 s after launch; when that thread was the first to load the object while the first composition's snapshot was already open, the state was created after the snapshot and the overlay's read threw. Ten fresh installs of the old build did not reproduce it on their own; a 4 s stall in the first composition made the old code crash on the second install with the exact trace, and the fix survived 3 of 3 under the same stall. Fix: the state moved to a new `NavUiState` object that only the UI path touches, created by `NavSession.prepare()` from `Application.onCreate` on the main thread before any composition (one line in `DystopoApp.kt`); `NavSession.ui` is a property over it, so a background thread touching `NavSession` creates no Compose state; a grep finds no other snapshot state under `nav/`. `NavUiStateTest` (JVM) shows that state first touched on another thread after a snapshot throws and that `NavUiState` prepared before the snapshot does not. Tooling: `tools/android_debug.py` gained `launch_crash`, `parse_crash`, `raise_if_crashed` and a `LaunchCrash` exception (with `since` on `endpoint()` and `bootstrap()`), `android-check.py`'s `restart_app` reports "the app crashed: <exception line> [Process, PID]" instead of the style timeout (it did so live against the crashing build), `android-ui.py` adds `launchCrash` to a failing part, and `tools/test_android_debug.py` tests the parser. Verified by implementer and verifier: ten fresh install-launch cycles clean on each of two emulators (`/health` in 3 s, no FATAL line), `check` green, device tour 10/10 (implementer) and 9/10 (verifier: view 0 at z8 rendered 0 contour lines although 5 contour tiles were generated, reproduced on main at 5d066f8 in a second worktree, so not this PR; T78's implementer saw the same z8 miss with no server set; filed for the sweep task). Optional hardening the verifier suggested: `Areas.list` and `Areas.screenOpen` are snapshot state inside `object Areas` that `AheadPrefetch`'s thread could in principle load first once navigation runs; initialise `Areas` from `DystopoApp.onCreate` the same way. Side finding: a fresh-install loop must start the activity by its full component name (`io.github.jasondorjeshort.dystopo/io.github.jasondorjeshort.dystopo.app.MainActivity`); `.MainActivity` launches nothing.

**2026-10-13: T78 merged (PR #84): peer sources on a trip.** One phone with the sources serves the others (Design goals, principle 6). While a phone's Share screen is open it publishes, inside the T72 sealed link under the share code, its pack catalog (the server's `/catalog` format with hashes, `PackCatalog.kt` in shared), its T75 area manifests, pieces of its pack files and tiles from its packs (`/peer/catalog`, `/peer/areas`, `/peer/file/`, all 426 in the clear). Another phone adds it with address and code ("Use its maps", `POST /share/peers/add`) or by discovery with a code it already has, and the peer becomes a `peer://<host:port>` source of the T74 fetcher, asked before the network, so packs and areas download through the existing downloader with its resume and SHA-256 checks unchanged (`POST /share/peers/download?pack=&activate=1`, `/share/peers`, `area`, `catalog`, `code`, `refresh`); the sharing phone lists each fetching phone with its bytes and files (`serving.fetchers`). A peer's piece size halves after a reply fails on an open connection, down to 256 KiB from 4 MiB (`chunkByPeer`, `shrinks`), added after the verifier saw replies cut short over a slow slirp path (`LanHttp.readReply: reply shorter than its content-length` on every 4 MiB piece at about 220 KB/s; the same code ran at 39.7 MB/s over loopback, and after the rebase the implementer could not reproduce the cut at 3.9 to 4.2 MB/s with the emulator throttled; the next step if it returns is a write deadline or a half-close on the sharer's server). Derived tiles are not shared: 16 contour tiles around Paradise measured on the emulators (ms per tile, mean) at z12 21.2 from a peer that already has it, 37.2 when the peer makes it, 18.5 regenerated locally; z13 28.8, 85.4, 51.5; z14 23.1, 22.5 (cached), 30.8, so transfer wins only for tiles the peer already holds, by at most 23 ms; the keyed contour route stays only for the measurement. Sealing and opening a 4 MiB piece runs at 28 MB/s per core, so the emulators' virtual Wi-Fi limited the transfer, not the encryption (four pieces in flight 2.8 MB/s against 2.5 with one). Bluetooth LE discovery is designed in PLANNING › Sync (a rotating token in the advertisement, the LAN address handed over encrypted), not built. Verified by implementer and verifier on separate emulator sets, the receiver's app blocked by iptables from everything except loopback and the sharer: the Rainier pack (226,815,990 bytes) downloaded from a peer in 75.8 to 83 s over the virtual Wi-Fi (2.8 to 3.1 MB/s; 6 s over an adb loopback path), both SHA-256 matching the sharer's catalog, 235 MB in from the peer and 0 bytes to any other address, the sharer's counters at 226.8 MB out to that phone, the job's source label `peer://10.0.2.x:8841`; the receiver then navigated Paradise to Panorama Point with airplane mode on (2,953 m, `/nav/status` active, screenshots at z11, z13 and z15 with contours, trails, the route line, Myrtle Falls at z15); a download stopped at 31.6% by closing the sharer's Share screen resumed from the 83,886,080-byte part file within 20 s of the sharer restarting with a new code and the peer re-added; `android-probe.py --share` 17/17 (plain routes 426, a GET of `/share/sealed` 405, wrong code 403, replay 409, the code unchanged); the catalog arrived 83 to 359 ms after discovery on the implementer's phones (the verifier's second and third emulators never got a Wi-Fi carrier, so discovery timing is the implementer's measurement); device tour 10/10 on the receiver with a server set, 9/10 with none (the z8 view draws no contour lines on main too; sweep task). Rebased onto T85, T86 and T77 before the merge (one PLANNING conflict). Limits: peers are not remembered across app restarts; the sharer's Share screen has to stay open. Files outside the Owns line: one hook line in `ShareRoutes.kt`, the `--share` mode in `tools/android-probe.py`, one test in `PackCatalogTest.kt`; `PackDownloader.kt` now reads through a `RangeConn` interface.

**2026-10-14: T90 merged (PR #94): peer sources kept across restarts, whole replies over the slow path.** A peer added on a phone is kept in `files/share/peers.json` (address, code, name, NSD name, last seen, last catalog and areas, up to four known codes); restored peers are listed at launch and retried at once, then from 15 s doubling to 5 min and on discovery, and become fetcher sources only after they answer; an unreachable peer stays listed with its last-seen time until Forget, and a kept code that no longer opens (`codeChanged`) is not retried, since each wrong try spends one of the sharer's five failures. Replies cut short on the emulator's user-mode network path are fixed at the share server: `share/ShareListener.kt` replaces the second `LocalServer` for the LAN port, waits for the client to close first after a reply, and closes a reply that makes no write progress for 60 s (counters under `listener` in `GET /share/status`); debug `POST /share/peers/piece` fetches one piece with its SHA-256. The emulator's `gsm` throttling profile does not slow API 35 here (2 to 7.5 MB/s), so the cut was reproduced on the path that shows it: receiver with Wi-Fi off, sharer through `adb forward`. On that path a sharer built from main failed in 34 to 43 s ("reply shorter than its content-length", four or five shrinks to 256 KiB, 0 bytes), and the T90 sharer delivered the whole pack in 3 of 3 runs for implementer and verifier alike (18.3, 15.6 and 15.9 s for the verifier, both SHA-256 matching, every reply closed by the client first). Kept peers: restored 0.1 to 0.7 s after a force-stop and relaunch, pieces matching the host's SHA-256; an unreachable peer showed "Not reachable; trying again in 59 s (last seen 2 min ago)" and reconnected when the sharer came back; a truncated peers file starts empty and is rewritten. Discovery with both emulators on virtual Wi-Fi: a fresh sharer resolved 0.79 to 1.02 s after its listener opened (resolve 2 to 8 ms), under the 5 s target; the finding `2026-10-10-emulator-share-direct-connect.md` is resolved and promoted to the sync reference. `android-probe.py --share` 17/17, two-emulator sync and the direct peer download pass, device tour 10/10 (verifier). The verifier found two defects, now T96: a phone with kept codes can spend the sharer's five failures after the sharer reopens its screen (the launch retry plus discovery's try-every-code), which rotates the sharer's code under the user; and the listener's linger has no bound on time or threads.

**2026-10-14: T96 merged (PR #98): kept peers never rotate the sharer's code; a bounded linger.** The two defects the T90 verifier found in phone-to-phone sharing. The code: a phone holding kept codes could spend a sharer's five failures after the sharer reopened its Share screen (main's build: the launch retry plus discovery trying every known code, 6 refused, the code rotated under the user). Now `share/PeerCodes.kt` decides which code goes to which phone: a 403 is recorded per code and phone (address, NSD name and the device id every phone already announces in its NSD record, read now) and kept in `peers.json`, so a kept peer is recognised at a new address and gets only its own kept code, a phone this one does not know gets at most one older code per app start, a refused peer becomes `codeChanged` (its retries cancelled, no longer a fetcher source, refresh and download answering 403 without sending), and Forget retires its code. The verifier then found a burst still rotated the code (a cold z12 view through a peer source after the sharer reopened: 7 refused), so every peer request now goes through one gate per address and code: when the code has not answered for a second, one request goes first and the rest wait for its answer (`PeerCodes.send`). Measured on two emulators isolated from other agents' by iptables: the relaunch case, the cold-view burst and 8 parallel pieces each cost the sharer 1 failure and 0 rotations in 2 of 2 runs (a unit test: 16 parallel requests with a changed code send exactly one); a working peer is not measurably slowed (cold z12 view settled in 3.6 s against 3.2 s, same tiles and bytes; a full 226.8 MB pack in 13.0 and 16.8 s against 15.6 and 16.7 s). The linger: `ShareListener` now has one watcher thread for every waiting socket, at most 32 at once (one more closes the oldest) and at most 2 minutes each, where 300 idle clients had held 300 threads for 30 s each and a vanished client could hold a 4 MiB reply's thread for about 11.6 h; on the emulator 300 idle clients peaked at 32 waiting with 1 linger thread (96 to 98 threads in the app against 396), all released by 33 s. T90's whole replies hold on the receiver-Wi-Fi-off path (2 of 2 and 3 of 3, both SHA-256 matching, every reply closed by the client first). Also: kept peers restored 0.5 s after a relaunch, `android-probe.py --share` 17/17, two-emulator sync, device tour 10/10; the sync reference gains the Waypoint chip, the long-press card and free-standing waypoints (T91) and a method for isolating an emulator pair; the emulator reference's direct-connect line is corrected.

**2026-10-14: T95 merged (PR #99): pinned tiles survive a source version change; the Offline button shows the size first.** The critic's blocking objection to ADR 0027, reproduced first on main's build: after a `/build` on the server moved the dem and osm versions, one online view left 33 of a 240-tile pinned corridor (26.1 MB down to 4.5 MB), and offline the route start had no trails at z15 and no data at z12. The rule now (ADR 0030): a pinned area records, per source, the content versions its tiles were fetched at (`Areas.Record.versions`; the manifest's optional `versions`), the online fetcher keeps the dem and osm tiles a pin holds at its recorded version when the source moves on (`AreaPins.holds`) and drops the rest, a lookup that misses the current version tries the versions the pins hold before the network, and a refresh fetches the current version only: the area card's Update (the card says "Older than the source. Update downloads the newer data." and the Storage screen's pinned row names the area) or the prefetch ahead on the route while navigating online; a download that completes whole records the versions it holds and the old version's tiles no pin holds are deleted (`OnlineFetcher.dropUnheld`), so nothing accumulates across repeated bumps; areas recorded before T95 take the old version at the first change. The critic's serious objection: the trip's Offline button first works out the corridor's manifest and shows "This route offline: about N MB (N tiles)." with Download and Cancel, or the size and the budget reason with Close when the pinned budget would be passed (409 from the route, nothing recorded), and uses T91's per-mode zooms (an 83 km drive: z8 to z13, 342 tiles, 36 MB). Also: each Storage row counts only its own tiles, the pinned row breaks its tiles down by kind and names any area older than its source, and the Online row's Clear now leaves pinned tiles alone (it used to delete them: 297 tiles and 36 MB on the verifier's device; `TileCache.clearUnkept`, `pinnedKept` in the answer). Measured on the implementer's and the verifier's emulators: 240 of 240 and 237 of 237 tiles kept across the bump, every zoom from z8 to z15 drawing terrain, contours, trails and the route line offline with 0 fetches (652 and 479 lookups answered from the pinned version); Update refreshed 237 tiles and the old ones went; an area pinned on main's build carried over through the upgrade and a third bump left only the area's version and the current one; Clear on the online row removed 74 unpinned tiles and kept all 256 pinned; device tour 10/10 three times. New strings for the owner's sign-off are listed in the PR. Not built: pinned bytes per source in `GET /areas` and calling an area older when the phone has switched servers (the critic's hidden coupling); the critic's objections 3 and 4 stand.

**2026-10-14: T88 merged (PR #96): the cold direct view within 15 s to contours, its stage timings, and the direct-source leftovers.** Where a cold direct view's time went (a phone with no server and no pack), measured stage by stage: `GET /firstrun/direct` reports `stages` (render permit waits and the part held for coarse tiles, fetch, encode, contour DEM waits, and the sources' cold range reads, reads joined by another render, disk hits, block decodes, geoid and resampling), `rangeStalls`, and a `timeline` of the process's first 800 events; release builds write the same lines to logcat (`DystopoDirect`). The cuts, each by a measured cause: a per-thread memo of the last sampled block with the four bilinear neighbours read from one block; concurrent renders share one download and one decode of a block (`Once`); 3DEP reads the geoid with its blocks and names its cells per row and column; the source pool queues block reads by the fanning thread's priority (coarse tiles, then the view, then out-of-view tiles); range reads keep their connections and a 4 s stall is retried; LERC2 header caps and a GeoTIFF tile index read on first use. On the phone: requests that wait on the sources run on the loopback server's wait pool (48 workers, no queue; `server.pools.wait` in `/stats`) so they never hold the tile workers; direct DEM requests have their own threads; 48 fetcher requests and 10 renders; a tile deeper than `directMaxZoom` renders its ancestor and in-view neighbours together; renders at the view's zoom wait up to 1.5 s for coarse renders in flight (decided by measurement: no hold 13.3 to 14.8 s to Rainier's first contours, a 3 s hold 6.0 to 15.3 s with the view's own tiles late on a fast link); a DEM tile not ready in 9 s (a contour tile's DEM in 7 s) answers 503 and the style reloads once the renders those answers waited on are done, with no second reload for the old style's 503s. The T87 leftovers: a contour tile made with an out-of-view halo tile left out is cached under a key naming it (`OnlineTiles.contourKey`, `ContourSeamKeyTest`) and made whole once that tile exists (the cached view-edge seam: a hairline through every line before a relaunch, every line continuous after, on both the implementer's and the verifier's crops); a contour tile a DEM pack's bounds overlap reads at the hillshade's zoom like the other direct tiles, taking the pack's tile wherever the pack answers one (the cold z8 tour view went from 24 coarse renders, 34 MB and 74 to 108 s to 9 renders, 9.7 MB and 46 to 49 s, the heap at the tour's end from 122 MB to 64 MB); a contour tile across a phone-built pack's edge is served without the missing DEM tile and not kept, where it answered 500 offline and z8 to z10 never went idle. Measured by taps on the release build (`android-firstrun.py --direct`, one emulator on the host; seconds to first contours / fine contours): the implementer at Rainier 5.6/10.4, 7.1/11.9, 7.6/13.9 and Mont Blanc 4.1/7.6, 4.7/6.3, 6.7/12.3 against main's Rainier 20.0/24.7, 20.6/21.5, 23.7/23.7; the verifier on the rebased head at Rainier 5.0/7.5, 5.6/8.7, 9.7/11.9 and Mont Blanc 5.5/5.5, 5.6/7.1, 4.0/6.1 against main's 12.6/14.9, 12.9/17.7, 8.5/21.0 and 7.6/7.6, 7.1/11.3, 10.5/11.6: the 15 s target met 3 of 3 at both places in both sets of runs; with four other agents' emulators on the host (link 0.8 to 1.6 MB/s) in 10 of 17 runs, the floor being bytes over the emulator's link plus one style reload. The debug timeline of a cold Rainier z12 view: 17.5 MB, 46 requests, 16 renders, the first 200 at 4.9 s, the last contour 200 at 9.3 s, cold reads 48 s summed over 29 across threads. A pack built on the phone (Paradise, 17 s) shows z8 to z12 offline with 0 bytes fetched and 0 tile errors, every screenshot viewed (z8 the pack's whole tile and paper around it, z9 its tiles over the overzoomed z8 one, z10 to z12 full contours). The device tour on a fresh AVD with no server: 9/10 (view 0, the cold z8 view, misses the tour's 30 s idle at 40 to 47 s; main 74 and 89 s, once with no contour lines drawn), 10/10 on every rerun with view 0 in 8 to 10 s; accepted as a follow-up since main fails the same way and worse. Heap at a tour's end 40 to 65 MB; peak 139 of 192 MB in a cold Mont Blanc z12 view with 10 renders in flight, no OOM in any run. Tried and reverted: renders and block decoding at background priority (slower behind a fresh emulator's first-boot work). Outside Owns: one line each in `DystopoApp.kt` and `OnlineService.kt`, two in `tools/android-firstrun.py`. `check` green, goldens unchanged.

**2026-10-14: T99 merged (PR #100): the sweep's update part, its sheet, `steps.tsv` with readers, the hook paths.** The update part's `lower-quiet` failure, confirmed with logcat on an API 35 emulator with debug-signed release builds (the release key is not in task worktrees): the previous build (v0.1.5-alpha's updater code, versionCode 269) checked the real releases API 15 s after its start and wrote `lastCheckAt`, which `install -r` keeps, so the current build made no check of its own; pointing the previous build at the mock alone did not fix it. The rule now: `UpdateFeed.CheckRecord` keys `lastCheckAt` and `lastAttemptAt` by the versionCode that recorded them (`checkedByCode`), so a build installed over another one, or over a record without a code, checks at its first start and a failed first check retries after an hour (`UpdateFeedTest`); for users that is one more read of the releases API after each install or update, and a sideloaded build learns of a newer release at once; the install result names the running build instead of "Installed null". The harness (`android-ui.py update`): the previous build is pointed at the mock from its first start and `create-data` waits for its check (`previousCheckAsked`), the wrong-certificate APK is re-signed with a throwaway `keytool` key so the part runs without the release key too, the app's `DystopoUpdate` lines go to `updater-log.txt`, and the install step force-stops the Settings app first and taps "Allow from this source" once (a second run on one emulator had Settings keep the app's old uid and the loop tap 42 times). Final run, 269 to 330 to 331 in 353 s (the verifier 269 to 332 to 333 in 344 s): all ten steps and `alive` pass, with the 331 build checking at its own first start; the part's 19 screenshots are on the sheet in a new section (`sweep-summary.py`, images in natural order, a summary entry with the versions and the two check counts). `steps.tsv`: `Add-Content` opens without read sharing, so with `tail -F` holding the file 100 of 100 appends failed and `main`'s sweep died at its first write; it is now appended through a stream with read, write and delete sharing, retried 20 times and rewritten once it can, and a sweep of `-NoDevice -Only check,notices` finished (exit 0, 111 s) with `tail -F` and `Get-Content -Wait` both holding the file. Hooks: `.claude/settings.json` runs them in Claude Code's exec form, `"command": "node", "args": ["${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.claude/hooks/<name>.js"]` (documented: no shell, the placeholder substituted into each argument, the session's start directory), after a `cd` into a subfolder had made the old relative commands fail with MODULE_NOT_FOUND and block every shell call; `test-hooks.js` runs each command from a subfolder and from a copy under a path with a space, 136 of 136 (ADR 0021 gains "How the hooks are run"). Mount Kenya's z9 and z12 renders are not a defect: the box is tall and the world-tour contact sheet pads every non-square render with its dark background, as since T50. Not run: the update part with the release key and the published v0.1.5-alpha APK, which the next sweep from the main checkout does. Outside Owns: `tools/sweep-summary.py`, one sentence in `android-online.md`, ADR 0021, the sweep skill, the finding (resolved, promoted to `release-realism.md`).

**2026-10-14: T98 merged (PR #101): contour tiles over a DEM pack read at least the pack's first zoom when a server is set.** The sweep's z8 tour view with no contour lines, in both runs, was not T88's direct path and not a cache: a debug build on an emulator uses `http://10.0.2.2:8765` (`AppSettings.EMULATOR_DEBUG_SERVER`, the host's live viewer) until a server is set, and the sweep had stopped that viewer to rebuild the install jars, so the tour ran with a server set but unreachable. With a server set, a contour tile the Rainier pack's bounds overlap but do not contain (8/41/89, 8/41/90 and MapLibre's z7 fallbacks) samples a coarser grid (z7 for a z8 tile, z6 for a z7 one), the online pyramid starts at the server's z6 and the pack at z8, so the window was read from the server's tiles only: every cell NaN, a 17-byte tile with no line, served 200 and never kept, which MapLibre held; the rerun's 1 ms per tile was the fetcher in backoff answering "unavailable" at once (run 1's 1.3 s average was the 3 s connect timeout). T87's `demMinZoom` had fixed the same symptom for the direct path only. The rule now (`OnlineTiles.serverContourOptions`, dispatched from `DirectSources.contourOptions` for a non-direct fetcher): with a configured server, a contour tile that a DEM pack's bounds overlap reads its window at least at the deepest first zoom of those packs, averaged down to the same grid (`sampleAtDemZoom`) and never deeper than the tile's spec zoom; the prefetch reads the same window; the direct path is unchanged. `/stats` `contourGeneration.noDemOverPack` counts contour tiles sampled all NaN where a pack overlaps (0 expected; `android-check.py` records the app's server state and warns on it). `PackFloorContourTest` (a synthetic pack from z8 over a pyramid from z6, the server unreachable) fails without the fix. Measured on API 35 emulators: on main with an unreachable server, view 0 drew 0 lines (five 17-byte tiles made in 0.4 to 2.1 ms) and the screenshot matched the sweep's; fixed, 175 lines (8/41/89 151 and 8/41/90 131 at z8, the z7 fallbacks 79 and 73; 8/41/91 with no pack under it empty, as offline should be) and 10/10 twice; with 8765 up, 180 lines (the pack's z8 read instead of the server's z7 gave 144 and 173 lines against 188 and 208, the same lines with fewer small rings; the seam between the two kinds of tile continuous in the crops); as the sweep runs it (`-Only device-boot,device-tour`, fresh AVD, 8765 up), 10/10 twice, view 0 180 lines in 23.4 then 9.2 s (the verifier 17.6 then 9.6 s, other views' line counts identical); no server set on a settled emulator, 10/10 twice, view 0 177 lines in 14.3 s cold (9 renders averaging 5.1 s, 7.6 MB) then 9.5 s; the verifier's dead-server and no-server tours 10/10 each twice. Still open from T88: with no server on a just-booted emulator, view 0 misses the 30 s idle (80.7 s; the DEM renders took 50 to 55 s each because block decoding and resampling run 27 to 34 times slower in the first minutes after boot). Also found: shade tiles (slope, multi-direction hillshade) use the same online pyramid, so at z6 and z7 over a pack's edge with the server down they are blank the same way (the verifier: `/shade/7/20/44` and `/shade/7/20/45` 1665-byte empty PNGs, `/shade/8/41/90` a real tile); the sweep's device tour inherits the debug default server, so its outcome depends on whether 8765 is up. Emulator quirks: a new AVD's first start exited twice with "Netsim Wifi ... is gone" and booted on the second; one fresh AVD's resolver sent only AAAA queries, so the 3DEP hosts did not resolve; a first test compile in the worktree failed on every `internal` function after a build-cache reuse (`:androidApp:clean --no-build-cache` fixed it). Outside Owns: one sentence in `android-app.md`, diagnostics in `android-check.py`, one sentence in `android-online.md`. `check` green, goldens unchanged.

**2026-10-15: T101 merged (PR #102): a scale bar on the phone map, and miles with one decimal.** One distance rule in `UnitFormat` (imperial: feet below a tenth of a mile, then miles with one decimal below 100 and none above; metric unchanged except that the unit and decimals now follow the rounded value, so 999.6 m reads 1.00 km), `UnitFormat.size` for a box's two sides, `metersPerPixel(zoom, lat)` (Web Mercator, 512-pixel world; equal to MapLibre's per-dp value to every digit shown) and `scaleBar(metersPerPixel, maxPx, units)` (the longest 1, 2 or 5 times a power of ten in the unit the distance rule would pick, 40 to 100% of the width); `UnitFormatTest` 11 tests (the unit edges, a z3 to z18 sweep at Rainier, Svalbard, Torres del Paine and the equator). Before: `distance` had switched to miles at 0.1 mi since T33, with two decimals; the strings that bypassed it were the pack catalog's size (now on the rule), the area card's corridor line (T106) and the router's own texts (T102). The bar: `controls/ScaleBar.kt`, lower left, at most 100 dp, following the camera and the units preference, 6 dp above the credit line, the route card and the track panels, hidden while the trip card is open (it reports no edge); a Map options row "Scale bar" (One unit, or Both units as a two-sided ruler), stored on the device and not synced (a `MapOptions` key would reload the style); `GET /scalebar` with a read-back against MapLibre's projection and `POST /scalebar?both=`. Measured by the implementer and the verifier at the Rainier summit: six zoom and unit combinations (z8, z13.5 and z16 in feet and metres: 10 mi, 0.2 mi, 200 ft, 20 km, 200 m, 50 m), each 0.000% against MapLibre's metres per dp and 0.08% under the ground distance across the centre; both units, and font scale 2.0 with the night style, legible; clearances in dp 65.9 over the credit line's 59.8, 211.8 over the hike route card's 205.7, 263.6 over the track panel's 257.5, the nav card at the top; the Paradise to Sunrise drive reads 51.0 mi on the route and trip cards (50.9 mi to the verifier's end point); the option toggled by taps and kept across a restart; device tour 10/10 twice; `check` green both times. Follow-ups: T107 ports the rule to the browser's scale bar; T102 the router's km and m texts whatever the units (the stored route description, the search-limit failure, the off-network fallbacks); T106 the corridor line and the update card's missing edge (the trip card's too, so the bar hides rather than rides); the both-units choice is not synced (it could join the synced settings in T103's tabs without a style reload); with tilt the bar is right only at the centre row; `GET /scalebar` `readBack` goes stale while the trip card hides the bar.

**2026-10-15: T102 merged (PR #106): turn directions in hike, bike and drive; the drive sentence in one place; the DriveOverlay build lock.** `shared/.../routing/Directions.kt` derives instructions from a route: each with a type, a priority 1 to 3 (so a later detail-level setting can filter), the distance along the route, the way's name (name, else ref, else class, as the owner's brainstorm says), the turn angle, a lead distance by mode and the stretch to the next; straight-through junctions are merged, a turn on a trail counts only where another way leaves the junction, pull-outs are ignored by car and a "keep" just before a turn is dropped (before those two rules the drive had 21 instructions, ten of them needless keep-lefts). The phone, the server's `/route` and `/route/edit` and `devtool route` all carry them: the route card shows "Show N directions", the nav card the next direction with its distance and an arrow, `/nav/status` lists `instructions` and `nextInstruction`, and `GET /nav/directions` and `POST /nav/directions?open=` are new. Measured: the Paradise to Sunrise drive 14 instructions, 12 shown at the default level (Head south on the service road; Turn right onto Longmire-to-Paradise Road; Turn left onto Stevens Canyon Road; Turn left onto SR 123; Turn left onto Mather Memorial Parkway); the Paradise to Panorama Point hike 8, 7 shown (Head northeast on Avalanche Lily Trail; Bear left onto Skyline Trail; Bear left to stay on Skyline Trail, minor and hidden by default; Turn right onto Alta Vista Trail; Continue onto Skyline Trail); a replayed drive and hike advance `nextInstruction` through every direction to arrival; screenshots at font scale 1.0 and 2.0 viewed; the server returned the same 14 instructions, also for an edit that does not reroute; routing tour 8/8 (the three Grand Canyon lines skipped, no pack); device tour 10/10; `check` green. ADR 0032: the drive sentence lives only in `Router.DRIVE_NOTICE` and "not a road navigator" is gone from the app's text; router descriptions and failure messages follow the units preference on the phone (T101's follow-up). T97's follow-up: a build lock in `DriveOverlay.cell` (`DriveOverlayRaceTest`: 2 builds without it, 1 with it). Not built, flagged under ADR 0031 as a data format change: directions stored with a trip, so a trip planned on a server and navigated with no pack on the phone has only depart, via points and arrive; voice and the detail-level setting stay later. The verifier repeated every run on the rebased branch (the turns sit at real trail junctions on an OSM render, including the Glacier Vista spur; a server-planned route with the phone's pack deleted gave 7 directions and advanced to arrival; `DriveOverlayRaceTest` fails with the lock removed) and noted: `Directions.next()` keeps a just-passed instruction as next until 40 m by car after it, so in the Sunrise parking-lot cluster the sharp left onto Sunrise Park Road was never shown as next at 90 km/h with fixes 25 m apart (it shows at slower speeds); roundabouts and "then" cues for instructions close together are not handled. Follow-ups: those two; with the route card's directions list open at font scale 2.0 the recentre button sits over the bottom chip of the stack (the Routes chip) and in follow mode the nav card covers the junction ahead; trails with only a ref are named by it from now on, but graph files built earlier keep them unnamed until rebuilt; the drive cuts the Sunrise Point hairpin through the parking lot; a font scale change restarts the app and loses an unsaved route; the old wording survives in a `trips.js` comment (T107); `routing-tour.txt` unchanged because `android-ui.py` ignores keys it does not know, so a directions check there needs the script first (T105's file).

One heading source (`controls/Heading.kt`): course over ground when moving, the compass when slow or still; the compass runs only while facing is on and the app is in the foreground (before: for all of navigation), and its reading is steady with the phone held upright (`NavDevice.kt`). The mode persists across restarts as north-up or facing (13 lines in `AppSettings.kt`). Debug: `GET /camera` reports the mode, whether the fix is centred and the heading source; `POST /camera/recenter?taps=`, `/camera/orientation?mode=`, `/camera/heading?deg=`. Measured on the release build by taps with a replayed track as emulator fixes: every tap after a pan left the puck 0.0 px from the centre with the mode unchanged, a second tap advanced it, a two-finger turn entered free rotate; facing within 0.05 degrees of a replayed heading over 255 settled samples and within 0.2 degrees of the emulator's virtual compass after about 10 s; through ten replayed fixes in navigation (97 samples) the nav card, the button and the camera never disagreed across a pan, the card's Recenter, a button tap and the card's North up; the three icons identical at font scale 1.0 and 2.0; device tour 10/10; `check` green. **2026-10-16: T108 merged (PR #110): turn directions saved with a trip, a crow mode, and T102's routing follow-ups.** Under ADR 0033 a route carries a new sync field `directions` (`RouteDirections`, base64url version 1, keyed by an FNV-1a 64 hash of the path value it was made for, capped at 64 KiB; Paradise to Sunrise 618 characters, the Panorama Point hike 460): Keep saves the plan's directions, every edit saves the editor's and keeps the untouched legs' (`Directions.splice`), and `Materializer.routePlan` takes them only while the key matches the path, so a path written without them (the browser, a v0.1.7 phone, two phones editing at once) drops them the same way on every replica. The phone database goes to schema 5 (`4.sqm`, frozen `4.sql`); on the first open after the update every route row is rebuilt from the log, so directions a newer peer synced into a v0.1.7 log survive (`V017TripFixtureTest`). Trips from v0.1.7 or the browser gain directions in the background when opened where a pack's graph covers them (`directionsMigrated`). The upgrade fixture `sync/v0.1.7-trips.dsync` was written by v0.1.7-alpha itself through the coordinator's live server and round-trips byte for byte on both stores. v0.1.7 phones skip the field (their route fields stop at `waypoints`; `RouteDirectionsTest`). Crow: `TravelMode.CROW` (`routing/Crow.kt`), a straight line between the points, every leg `STRAIGHT`, a DEM point every 25 m (at most 2,001 per leg), the initial bearing per leg in `summary.bearings` and `route.bearingDeg`, one arrival instruction, the hike time, no graph and no trail notice; `RemoteRouter` never asks the server; in the devtool (`route --mode crow`, `--dem` alone suffices), the server (`/route?mode=crow`) and the phone's mode chips, with the line "A crow route is a straight line between the points, not a path to walk". Paradise to Panorama Point: 1,873.2 m, bearing 18°, +430.4 / −27.9 m, 1:06, 76 points, the same from the devtool, the server and the phone. An unknown mode reads plainly ("walk (unknown mode)"), never as hike, and an edit is refused with a reason (400 on the phone and the server's `/route/edit`); ADR 0033 Decision 3, read in the 0.1.7 code: a crow trip on a v0.1.7 phone shows "crow" as its mode with the hike time, navigates as a hike, and an edit there reroutes the moved legs along trails under the name crow. **2026-10-16: T112 merged (PR #111): one cache budget over the phone's per-kind caps, the eviction rule chosen on replayed numbers (ADR 0034).** A layer over T74's caps (ADR 0025 principles 2 and 3), not a new store: one total (`AppSettings.cacheBudgetMb`) over the Storage screen's three rows, Online tiles, Contours and shading (every contour, shade and cliff tile the phone made, from packs and from downloaded DEM; the latter used to count as online tiles) and Source data; pinned areas and packs stay outside with their own budget. Presets Small 1 GB (default), Medium 8 GB, Large 128 GB and Custom (a size, or 10/25/50% of the free space turned into a fixed number once, as the owner said), set by `POST /storage?budgetMb=|preset=|percentFree=` in `storage/Storage.kt` (no `Routes.kt` line was needed). Floors 25% online, 15% derived, 5% sources; past the total, derived goes first down to its floor, then the source kind furthest above its floor, least recently used first within a kind and merged across the two caches that hold derived tiles by touch time; never pinned, never in view (the visible box plus half a view each side), never within 300 m of the next 3 km of a navigated route (`cachebudget/CacheGuard.kt`); a run frees 1% of the total of slack and is triggered after tile writes, every 30 s and when the total is set. `TileCache` and `ContourCache` keep their constructors, `resize` and directory layout (`TileCache` gains `onWrite`, `evictOldest`, `oldestTouch`, `bytesWhere`, `budgetEvictions`); the caps stay as per-cache ceilings and follow the total. The screen shows the total as a stacked bar, each kind against its floor and share, Clear per row; the per-cache budget chips are gone. Settings change under ADR 0033: an install without a budget gets `max(1 GB, onlineCacheMb + contourCacheMb)` once; default caps 1024 MB each; a stored cap may reach 1 TB through the budget only. The package is `cachebudget/`, not `cache/`: the repo `.gitignore` line `cache/` silently untracked the first version. The measurement, `devtool cache-replay --log <server log> --totals --rules lru,gdsf,lru-global [--forget-on-restart]`, replays the standalone server's JSON request log through the phone's kinds, floors, derived-first order, slack and a 5 s view protection. The coordinator's 8800 log had only 62 tile answers (too small to choose on); the task's own 8712 server, driven by `demo-check.py` over the demo tour, phone-style view lists and six seeded random walks around Rainier and Mont Blanc, gave 23 sessions and 10,540 tile answers. Random walks at 32 MB: LRU with the layer 8.2% online, 33.1% derived, 9.8% of bytes; GDSF (size-weighted frequency) 15.3% / 33.1% / 10.4%; one global LRU without floors 8.4% / 1.6% / 9.3%. All sessions at 64 MB: LRU 33.1% / 75.1% / 34.5% of bytes, GDSF 49.3% / 75.1% / 43.0%, GDSF forgetting its counts at a restart 41.6% / 75.1% / 33.2%; at 128 MB LRU 55.6% / 75.1% / 55.7%, GDSF 72.3% / 75.1% / 70.6%. Reading: the floors and derived-first order are what move the numbers (contour hits 33% against 1.6% on the walks, 74% against 6% over all sessions at 32 MB, for at most 3 points of online hits); GDSF wins 4.5 to 7 points of request hits on the walks but only 0.6 to 3.5 points of bytes, its large wins come from scripted tours replayed in the same order, and without persisted counts it falls below LRU in bytes. Chosen: LRU within a kind under the layer; since the cross-kind order is not plain LRU, ADR 0034 amends ADR 0025 principle 3 and PLANNING › Design goals › Caches points at it. Files against a database: LRU order survives restarts in the files' times, so no index database; only a frequency rule would need one; both to be revisited with a phone request log (wave 16). On the emulator: 20 public views outside the packs filled 141.8 MB (online 139.2, derived 2.6); with the camera on Matterhorn z13, `budgetMb=64` answered in 185 ms (run 59 ms), used 141,834,674 to 62,860,416 bytes, 528 online tiles freed, derived untouched under its floor, bytes in view and ahead 10,949,802 before and after, the map intact in the screenshot; three new places at 64 MB each triggered a run from the writes (23 to 33 ms) and used stayed under 64 MB; the total survived an app restart; bad values answer 400. The Storage screen at font scale 2.0 first squeezed the kind labels into broken words, fixed by moving the share under the bar; both scales re-shot and viewed. Tests: `BudgetPlanTest`, `CacheBudgetTest` (real caches in a temp dir), `CacheGuardTest`, `StorageKindsTest`, `CacheReplayTest`; device tour 10/10 twice; `check` green. The verifier repeated the replay over all 23 sessions (the 64 and 128 MB rows match the table) and over the coordinator's 8800 log (62 answers, no evictions), the presets and the 400s, the restart, both font scales and the device tour 10/10, and ran the eviction with no server instead of the implementer's 8765: from 195 MB (online 88, derived 2.9, sources 104) the view's 6,787,946 bytes were kept and the map was identical, but the total reached 64 MB only after about 3 minutes and 6 runs (140 MB at 5 s, 105 MB at 55 s), because the Source data kind, with its own 512 MB trim in `DirectSources`, is re-read while online tiles are cut and online was squeezed to its floor; within a minute holds in server mode, where Source data is 0. The verifier's clean rebase could not be pushed: the `git-guard` force-push rule rejected the `HEAD:task/T112` refspec the verifier brief prescribes (fixed in T113); the PR merged on GitHub without conflict. Follow-ups: T109 the Data and storage row's subtitle naming the total and help icons for the Storage rows; `DirectSources`' own 512 MB source trim should follow the total so a no-server eviction settles in one run; `pinnedCacheMb` still capped at 8 GB, small next to the Large preset; wave 16 a phone request log, then the frequency rule and the index database revisited (ADR 0034's trigger); the derived-first order was not exercised on the device (derived stayed under its floor at the 64 MB minimum), covered by tests and the replay; the new strings (presets, the budget explanation, the floor line) are first versions for the owner (ADR 0031).

**2026-10-16: T113 merged (PR #113): the git guard drops shell redirection tokens before judging a push.** Added mid-wave when the verifier of PR #111 could not push its clean rebase: both of its commands (the lease push of `HEAD:task/T112` from a detached worktree, and the `=ref:sha` lease form from a local branch) ended in `2>&1 | tail`, and the guard's tokenizer handed `2>&1` to the push as one more refspec, so the task-ref check failed and rule 1 reported a plain force push; the coordinator's own `git push origin --delete task/T108` with the same suffix was refused by rule 2 the same hour, and a first fix agent could not reproduce the denial because it replayed the commands without the suffix. `invocations()` in `git-guard.js` now drops redirection tokens (`2>&1`, `>file`, `>>file`, `2>/dev/null`, `1>&2`, `<file`, the split `2> file`) from a git invocation's arguments before the rules run; a pipe already ended the arguments; the `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<sha>` form was already a lease. Rule 1 also refuses a bare `HEAD` (destination unknown) and `:ref` deletions, and its advice names the `HEAD:task/Tnn` refspec form. Hook tests 141 to 154 (the two verbatim commands allowed; the delete with the suffix judged like the bare delete; `--force` with a redirection still denied; a lease push to `HEAD:main` denied with `2>/dev/null` and with `2> err.txt`). `./gradlew check` not run (hooks only). The verifier rebased cleanly, ran the hook tests on both trees (141 on main, 154 on the branch), read the filter (anchored at the token start on an optional descriptor or ampersand, then a redirection operator, so no refspec or branch name matches) and fed thirteen ad-hoc commands through the real hook: a redirection in the middle of the arguments is stripped, a plain force or a plus refspec with a redirection is still denied, a lease push to a non-task destination is denied with every redirection form, a pipe still ends the arguments, a commit message containing the text is untouched. Follow-ups: none; the lesson for briefs is in the `wave` skill's Windows-and-shells pointer already (redirections and pipes on guarded git commands).

**2026-10-16: T110 merged (PR #112): Android leftover sweep 8.** The no-server cold z8 view (T88, T105) measured by stages on five fresh AVDs with two or three other agents' emulators on the host: the view takes 9.7 to 14.5 s after the quiet wait before and after this task (before: 14.5 and 10.3 s with the normal wait, 24.2 s with the wait cut to 19 s as in T105's run C; after: 9.7 and 12.4 s), and the cost is the just-booted device, which decodes a 3DEP block in 1.8 to 3.3 s against 0.01 to 0.1 s when quiet, so T105's 43 s was device or host load, not the view. The measured reason and the tour's new rule: `android-check.py` gives the first, cold view `--first-wait-ms` (60 s; the others keep 30 s) and reports `idleSeconds` and `deviceCpu` per view. The LZW decoder in `shared/.../tiff/Lzw.kt` is one loop over plain locals with a running float predictor: 1.8 times faster interpreted (`-Xint` on a real block 234 to 253 ms before, 132 to 147 ms after; JIT 6 to 8 ms either way), byte for byte the same (`LzwTest` encodes like libtiff and compares with the old decoder kept in the tests). The credit line lagging a view (T106): sources are asked again when MapLibre reports the map idle (at most once a second) and the line composes when they change (`CreditLine.revision`); four views in a row (Mont Blanc, Glacier/Waterton, Rainier, Bonneville) each named their own sources, `lineMatchesView` true (`GET /attribution` adds `lineShown`, `lineMatchesView`, `onlineSourceIds`). The update offer and navigation (T106): during navigation the offer asks ("Installing closes the map and ends navigation." with After navigation, Install now, Not now), an install asked for during navigation waits for the session to end and starts by itself (`InstallWait`, `POST /update/install?now=1`), the offer's height is capped to the room below the nav card; measured at font scale 1.0 offer top 750.9 dp against the nav card's 284.2 dp, at 2.0 594.7 against 420.2 dp, `clearOfNavCard` true, and after `POST /nav/stop` the install started (`installsAfterNavigation` 1). Two bugs found on the way: navigation from a planned route kept the plan so the offer waited for a hidden card the whole session, and at 2.0 the offer covered the credit line's first line. The share gate (T106): wrong codes counted per address (`Request.peer`): five from one address in ten minutes lock that address out with a 429 until they age or the code changes, other phones go on, fifty from all addresses together still replace the code (`ShareGatePerPeerTest`; `android-probe.py --share --quick` green). The recentre button (T102) never reaches up to the top-right chips: the lowest of the track controls, the Routes chip (`TopChips.routesChipBottom`) and the nav card; with no room it hides (`noRoomBelowChips`); at 2.0 with the drive's directions open it hid, with them closed it sat clear, and the Tracks list open by taps left it clear (screenshots). The routing tour's directions check (T102): `android-ui.py routes` learns `dirs=` and `turn=` and `routing-tour.txt` gains the Paradise to Panorama Point line (8 directions, "Turn right onto Alta Vista Trail"); tour 11/11 with T108's crow lines. Both units synced (T101): `app.scaleBar.both` through `SettingsSync`, applied through the bar's setter with no style reload (`SettingsSyncBothUnitsTest`; `GET /sync/status` › `settingsSync.held` "on" after `POST /scalebar?both=on`; two emulators not run). The scale bar's `readBack` (T101): a hidden bar keeps no lengths (`notComposed`) and `GET /scalebar` reads back only a bar shown at the live camera; `main` had reported a hidden 0.5 mi bar with a 699.96% error, now `readBackSkipped: hidden`, and 0% at z8, z13.5 and z16. Device tour 10/10 on fresh AVDs, the final and the rebased build; `check` and the user-text check green. The verifier rebased cleanly and repeated every run on a fresh AVD with other agents' emulators on the host: three cold starts gave 62.4 s (a fresh AVD, tour view failed), 4.2 s (reboot with disk caches) and 5.0 s (a second fresh AVD); the 62.4 s run is a counterexample to the busy-device explanation, since `deviceCpu` showed the device quiet and the direct-source timeline a 36 s gap with no tile request, then a fast finish (open finding `docs/findings/2026-10-16-cold-view-gap-on-a-quiet-device.md`, wave 16); the credit line matched four views, the offer stayed clear of the nav card at both scales (at 2.0 its top edge clips the Routes chip by a few px, cosmetic) and the install started after `POST /nav/stop`, the share probe passed, the recentre button hid under the Tracks list at 2.0, routing tour 11/11, both-units `held` on, the hidden bar `notComposed`, `LzwTest` (4, against the retained `LzwReference.kt`) and `GeoTiffTest` green on jvm, debug and release with no golden changed, device tour 10/10 twice (9/10 on the 62 s run), `check` green; the rebased branch could not be pushed (the guard defect T113 fixed the same hour) and the PR merged on GitHub without conflict. **2026-10-16: T111 merged (PR #115): desktop, server and tooling leftover sweep 8.** The soak's hourly `route` request with no status (finding `2026-10-16-soak-route-requests-without-status.md`, resolved and promoted to `docs/SERVER.md`) was the hourly quick probe's own `short-body` case, `POST /route/edit` declaring 50 bytes and sending 6: the route waited for the body, at `http.maxRequestSeconds` (60 s) the JDK listener's timer closed the connection without an answer, and `/metrics` counted it as `none`; in T107's `soak.jsonl` each of the 12 increments falls in the first sample after an hourly probe, and a one-hour soak before the fix with every hourly item every 5 minutes (`-TripMinutes 5 -ShareMinutes 5 -BuildMinutes 5 -ProbeMinutes 5`) and the request log on showed 12 requests with status 0, every one that route at 60,019 to 60,957 ms, `outcome: connectionLost`, while trips (route 22 to 82 ms), share links (5 to 42 ms), builds (3.0 to 6.1 s) and the five-minute full GC were all fast and answered. Fix in `server/.../Gate.kt`: a body still arriving at the limit gets a 408 with `Connection: close` from a timer thread, any later answer from the route is refused, the request counts as a 4xx and in `/metrics` `requestTimeouts` (`dystopo_request_timeouts_total`), logged at warn as `requestTimeout`; the JDK listener's timer runs `Gate.TIMEOUT_GRACE_S` (5 s) later so the 408 goes out first, which bounds slow headers at 65 s instead of 60 (the slowloris `/healthz` probe answered after 65.4 s; the coordinator kept the 5 s grace over a 2 s one so a queued worker cannot miss it; SERVER.md's reverse-proxy paragraph says 65 s). `OperationsTest` covers a short body (408 within 1.5 to 6 s at a 2 s limit), a stalled chunked body and a slow body that arrives in time, with `/metrics` route 4xx 3, `none` 0, `requestTimeouts` 2; with the gate's timer off the short body gets no answer. The soak after the fix answered the 12 short bodies 408 in 60,002 to 60,036 ms, 0 status-0 lines, verdict PASS. `server-soak.ps1` now reports `unanswered` per route in `summary.json` (count, slowest, the samples where it grew), has an `unanswered` check (`-MaxUnanswered`, default 0; on T107's archive it says FAIL, route 12, slowest 60,914.6 ms), reads `/metrics` once more after the final probe, gains `-ShareMinutes`, trip and share events with timings, and `-ChartOnly` on an archive; a soak defect found on the way: since T107's seventh map option the rounds sometimes asked for `contourInterval=40` in metres (8 client 400s an hour), now 20. T102's directions in the browser's Trips box: the card lists the server's `instructions` at the phone's default detail level with the distance to the next, a count, the first five and "Show N more" / "Show fewer" (`window.dystopoTrips.state().directions`); `demo-check --route` gained a `directions` step (count, first five texts, arrival last, unfolds by a real click, screenshot); the Paradise to Panorama Point hike gave 10 directions in feet on `devtool serve --live` and the same 10 on the standalone server with users and token and `--trip-sync`; the Paradise to Sunrise drive 14 shown of 16 in metres; screenshots viewed.
